It is now strictly less accurate than the eligibility system's version in `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer`. Agents should trust that if they are called, eligibility has already approved them — a secondary veto inside `execute()` breaks that contract.
  - **Fix:** Remove the `isAcceptedNotMergedAttempt` guard at lines 52–56 of `EvolutionAgent.execute()` entirely. Rely solely on `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer.explainBlockers("evolution", ...)`, which already has the `hasAnyVariant` backstop and is the single source of truth for evolution eligibility.
  - **Changes:** `EvolutionAgent.java` — delete the early-return block (~5 lines) and the private `isAcceptedNotMergedAttempt(CycleContextData)` helper (~15 lines). `EvolutionAgentTest` — remove any tests that directly exercise the removed guard; confirm eligibility-level tests in `ExecutionEligibilityExplainerTest` cover the scenario.
  - **Note:** T1-CL is the root-cause fix; T1-CM removes the structural inconsistency. Together they eliminate both the stale `lastAttemptId` problem and the duplicate veto that amplified it.

- [x] **T1-CN: Root completion blocked by stale `REPLAN_REQUESTED` when all direct subgoals are terminal** (`GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-14 22:16 session):** All 8 original planner subgoals completed in ~3 minutes. When the last one (`goal-ff4657d6`) was fulfilled, `REPLAN_REQUESTED=0.80` was still alive in context (set by a stagnation event on the preceding subgoal). `promoteCurrentSubGoal()` hit `currentSubGoalId == null`, saw `replanPending = true`, and skipped `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()`. With remaining goals = 0 and all children COMPLETED, the root should have been declared done. Instead, the planner fired, saw the empty backlog, and re-injected 2 more loan subgoals — triggering the analysis decomposition chain described in T1-CO.
  - **Root cause:** The `!replanPending` guard in the `currentSubGoalId == null` branch was introduced by T1-BK to handle a deduplication-blocked injection case, not genuine completion. When the backlog is empty because all children finished, the guard is incorrectly blocking completion.
  - **Fix:** In `GoalPlannerService.promoteCurrentSubGoal()`, in the `currentSubGoalId == null` branch, before checking `replanPending`, call `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` unconditionally if all direct children of the root are terminal (COMPLETED or GIVEN_UP). If `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` marks the root as COMPLETED or GIVEN_UP, return immediately — the REPLAN signal is irrelevant when the backlog is genuinely empty. The `!replanPending` guard should only block completion when `getSubGoals(root)` still contains ACTIVE entries (i.e., the "null" is from dedup blocking injection, not real completion).
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — in the `else` branch at ~line 487: add an early-exit `if (allDirectChildrenTerminal()) { autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied(); return null; }` before the `replanPending` check. Add private helper `allDirectChildrenTerminal()`. `GoalPlannerServiceTest` — add test: all direct children COMPLETED + REPLAN_REQUESTED present → root marked COMPLETED, planner not re-entered.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CJ (the `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` delegation it introduced is the call target here).

- [x] **T1-CP: Revert T1-CA "inject at root" — nest replacement subgoals under the GIVEN_UP subgoal to preserve root completion ratio** (`PlannerAgent`, `GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-14 23:21 session):** 8 original root-level subgoals: 6 COMPLETED, 2 stagnated. Planner removed the 2 and injected 6 replacements via T1-CA's `effectiveParentId = null` → 14 direct root children, 6/14 = 43% < 50% threshold → root GIVEN_UP instead of COMPLETED. Had the replacements been nested under the 2 stagnated subgoals, root would have seen 8 direct children (6/8 = 75% → COMPLETED).
  - **Root cause:** T1-CA set `effectiveParentId = null` (root injection) to avoid nesting under a dead parent. Correct for preventing orphan cascades, but it inflates the root's direct-child denominator, making the completion ratio unreachable when multiple rounds of replanning occur.
  - **Fix A — `PlannerAgent.java`:** Remove the `effectiveParentId = null` override. Pass `currentSubGoalId` (the GIVEN_UP subgoal) directly as the parent to `injectOutcomeGoals()`. Keep the T1-CH fingerprint clearing — it is still needed when injecting under a GIVEN_UP node. The goal tree shape becomes: `root → endpoint7 (GIVEN_UP) → [replacement-a, replacement-b, ...]` instead of `root → [replacement-a, replacement-b, ...]`.
  - **Fix B — `GoalPlannerService.addSubGoal()`:** Remove the T1-CA defensive guard that rejects injection under a GIVEN_UP parent (lines ~725–730). Replace with a log-warn that records the injection without blocking it, so fresh replacement goals can be added as children of a GIVEN_UP subgoal.
  - **Fix C — Orphan cascade fix (replaces T1-CA's cascade protection):** `getRemainingGoals()` must exclude ACTIVE goals whose parent is GIVEN_UP **and** whose `planningRunId` is older than `currentPlanningRunId` — these are genuinely orphaned leftover children from a prior planning round. Freshly injected replacements (same run) share `currentPlanningRunId` and are not filtered out, so the system works on them normally. Implementation: in `getRemainingGoals()`, after the existing `status == ACTIVE && type == SUBGOAL` filter, add: `&& !isStaleOrphan(g)` where `isStaleOrphan(g)` returns true iff `g.getParentId() != null && goalMap.get(g.getParentId()).getStatus() == GIVEN_UP && g.getPlanningRunId() < currentPlanningRunId`.
  - **Changes:** `PlannerAgent.java` — remove `effectiveParentId = null` override (~6 lines). `GoalPlannerService.java` — remove GIVEN_UP guard in `addSubGoal()` (~5 lines), add `isStaleOrphan()` helper, update `getRemainingGoals()` filter. Tests: add `getRemainingGoals_excludesStaleOrphansUnderGivenUpParent` and `getRemainingGoals_includesFreshInjectionsUnderGivenUpParent`.
  - **Note:** For flat structures where the failing subgoal is itself a direct child of root, the "parent of failing goal" is root — the ratio still gets diluted in the same way as before. T1-CQ addresses that separately. This ticket restores the correct invariant (replacements are tree-local, not promoted to root) for the common nested case, and is the prerequisite for T1-CQ.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CA (reversing), T1-CH (fingerprint clearing — retained). T2-BW (injection budget — not affected by parent change).

- [x] **T1-CQ: `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` ratio denominator inflated by stagnation-replan direct children** (`GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Origin:** Observed 2026-04-14 23:21 session; flat-structure residual after T1-CP.
  - **Problem:** For flat goal trees (root → subgoals directly), replacement subgoals injected by stagnation replanning are also direct children of root — T1-CP does not help because the failing subgoal's parent IS root. Each wave of replacements grows the denominator without guaranteeing more completions, making the 50% threshold harder to reach with each replan round.
  - **Fix:** In `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()`, compute the ratio using only direct children whose `planningRunId` equals the **minimum** `planningRunId` among all direct children (i.e. the first-wave subgoals from the initial planning response). Replacement subgoals injected in later planning rounds have a higher `planningRunId` and are excluded from the denominator. A first-wave subgoal that is GIVEN_UP counts as a failure; its nested replacements (T1-CP) are irrelevant to the root ratio.
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — in `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()`, replace the flat `directChildren` list with `firstWaveChildren` = direct children filtered to `minPlanningRunId`. ~5 lines. `GoalPlannerServiceTest` — add test: 6 first-wave children (4 COMPLETED, 2 GIVEN_UP with nested replacements) + 4 second-wave children (all GIVEN_UP) → ratio computed as 4/6 = 67% > 50% → COMPLETED (not GIVEN_UP).
  - **Depends on:** T1-CP (establishes the nesting invariant that makes first-wave vs. replacement-wave meaningful). T2-BW (planningRunId already stamped on each subgoal — used here as the discriminator).

- [>] **T1-CO: Split and demoted (2026-04-14)** — the three-part fix was not a correctness bug; the loop was not broken, it was missing exit paths. Reassigned: **Part A → T3-BL** (criteria-banked early exit — scaffold present, needs wiring), **Part B → T2-CD** (fingerprint-repeat FULFILLED inference — steering quality), **Part C → T3-BM** (stateless LLM criteria check — new component over existing data).

- [>] **T1-CK: SUBGOAL_EXHAUSTED with partial criteria met should trigger graduation rather than GIVEN_UP** (`AutobuilderService`, `GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Problem:** T1-CF (the all-criteria-met FULFILLED promotion) only handles the case where every criterion is banked as true. When some but not all criteria are satisfied and the subgoal is exhausted, the code falls through to `GIVEN_UP` — discarding measurable progress and forcing the planner to re-derive work already done. T3-BK's graduation escape fires during normal evaluation cycles; by the time `SUBGOAL_EXHAUSTED` is reached, graduation either didn't fire or was too late.
  - **Fix:** In `AutobuilderService`'s `SUBGOAL_EXHAUSTED` case, add a second check after the T1-CF all-criteria guard: if `!areCriteriaCumulativelySatisfied(subGoalId)` but some criteria are banked as true (i.e. `criteriaPassCount > 0`), call `goalPlannerService.injectGraduationMicroSubgoals()` for the unmet criteria instead of calling `applyGoalProgress(GIVEN_UP)`. Clear exhaustion signals and inject a `REPLAN_REQUESTED` so the new micro-subgoals are picked up immediately.
  - **Implementation note:** `injectGraduationMicroSubgoals(String graduatingSubGoalId, List<Integer> unmetIndices, ...)` already exists (T3-BK). The missing piece is: (a) computing the unmet indices from `getCriteriaPassStatus` and the defined-criteria count, and (b) calling it from the exhaustion handler as a backstop. Guard with `mergeCount > 0` (at least one merge occurred — partial progress is real, not a blank subgoal). If `mergeCount == 0`, fall through to GIVEN_UP as today.
  - **Changes:** `AutobuilderService.java` — add partial-criteria backstop between T1-CF check and existing GIVEN_UP path (~15 lines). `GoalPlannerService.java` — expose a helper `getUnmetCriteriaIndices(String goalId)` returning `List<Integer>` of 1-based indices not yet banked as true. `AutobuilderServiceTest` — add test: exhausted + partial criteria + mergeCount > 0 → graduation injected, GIVEN_UP not called.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CF (the all-criteria gate comes first; this is the partial-criteria branch below it). T3-BK (graduation injection already implemented; this backstop re-uses it).

- [x] **T1-CS: T2-CC null-eval EVOLUTION_NOOP falsely promotes subgoals with zero prior implementation evidence** (`EvolutionAgent`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-16 02:18 session):** POST /members (`goal-5b53b878`) was promoted to FULFILLED at 02:18:07 via EVOLUTION_NOOP. No `Member.java`, `MemberController.java`, or any member-related code exists in the output. The LLM said `#NoDiff`; the graph had no prior evaluation for the new subgoal ID; T2-CC's null-eval branch trusted the claim and promoted immediately. The feature was never implemented.
  - **Confirmed (2026-04-16 03:47 session):** Same pattern reproduced. POST /members (`goal-7f40483e`) promoted at 03:48:11 via `Evolution noOp — #NoDiff implies current output satisfies all criteria. Promoting to FULFILLED`. Output directory contains only Book files (6 files from initial evolution). No Member files anywhere. The verify-in-place mechanism (removed by T2-CE/CF diff) would have caught this, but the current unconditional trust of `lastEvalStatus == null || "ACCEPTED"` makes it strictly worse.
  - **Root cause:** T2-CC's null-eval branch assumes `lastEvalStatus == null` implies the "cross-subgoal deferred-merge case" — that the code was already delivered by a prior subgoal's merge and this subgoal never got its own evaluation cycle. This assumption is correct for deferred-merge siblings (e.g. GET /books/{id} already implemented by the big books merge), but wrong for freshly-injected subgoals that have simply never been evolved before. The system has no way to distinguish the two cases using `findLatestEvaluationStatusForSubgoal` alone.
  - **Consequence:** The final output contains only Book-related files. POST /members, GET /members/{id}, and all Loan endpoints are absent from output despite being marked COMPLETED or GIVEN_UP. Root goal was declared FULFILLED with 3/8 endpoints actually implemented.
  - **Distinguishing the two cases:**
    - **Deferred-merge (safe to trust #NoDiff):** A prior subgoal's merge delivered the code. The LLM checks `--- CURRENT IMPLEMENTATION STATUS ---` and correctly sees it exists. `previousOutput` contains relevant files (e.g. BookController.java when handling GET /books subgoal).
    - **First-time injection (unsafe to trust #NoDiff):** Brand new subgoal, code never written. `previousOutput` contains no files relevant to this subgoal. The LLM either (a) is wrong, (b) is lazy, or (c) sees a different subgoal's code and misidentifies it as covering this subgoal.
  - **Fix (2026-04-20):** In the null-eval branch of `EvolutionAgent.runEvolution()` (where `lastEvalStatus == null`), added `hasOutputEvidenceForSubgoal()` — a tokenised keyword scan (terms ≥4 chars from description and criteria, length-filtered) against `previousOutput` file paths and content. If zero matches: emit `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST(1.0, decay=0.1)` and return (cycle ends without EVOLUTION_NOOP; next cycle runs divergent evolution naturally). If evidence found: emit EVOLUTION_NOOP as before (deferred-merge case is legitimate).
  - **Why DIVERGENCE_REQUEST not a second evolveDivergent call:** Running the LLM adapter a second time within the same cycle is expensive and could hide issues. Signalling divergence and ending the cycle cleanly is cheaper and follows the same path as other divergence triggers.
  - **Edge cases:** Empty `previousOutput` → no match → DIVERGENCE_REQUEST (correct for first-ever cycle). Empty terms (null description + null criteria) → returns `true` conservatively (allow NOOP — can't determine intent).
  - **Changes:** `EvolutionAgent.java` — new `hasOutputEvidenceForSubgoal()` helper + updated null-eval branch. Two tests: deferred-merge (evidence present → EVOLUTION_NOOP) and first-time injection (no evidence → DIVERGENCE_REQUEST, no second LLM call).
  - **Depends on:** T2-CC.

- [x] **T1-CT: Mutation meta.json double-write — `CyclePersistenceService` overwrites `MutationApplier` meta with post-promotion context** (`CyclePersistenceService`, `MutationApplier`, `FileVariantCollector`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-16 03:47 session):** Cycle 1 (`cycle-1776304026516`) evolved POST /books. `MutationApplier` wrote `meta.json` with `currentSubGoalId=goal-c8b919ad` (POST /books) during evolution at 03:47:32. At cycle end (03:47:41), `CyclePersistenceService.persistSnapshot()` re-wrote the same `meta.json` with the final context state: `currentSubGoalId=goal-5e212e11` (GET /books — the NEXT subgoal after promotion) and `merged=true` (because cycle 2 later marked it via `CycleMergeTracker`). The double-write creates two distinct problems.
  - **Problem A — subgoalId drift:** The mutation was produced FOR subgoal A, but `meta.json` records subgoal B (post-promotion). `FileVariantCollector` filters variants by `activeSubGoalId.equals(meta.id())`. On the NEXT cycle, the active subgoal is B — so the variant matches and merge can proceed. But if the variant is NOT merged immediately in that next cycle (e.g. completion fires first), the subgoal advances to C, and the variant is permanently orphaned: its meta says B, the active subgoal is C, the filter rejects it. In the 03:47 session, this one-cycle-offset worked for cycle 1→2 (merge succeeded) but failed for all subsequent cycles (variants orphaned).
  - **Problem B — false merged flag:** Cycle 2's `meta.json` shows `merged=true` because `CyclePersistenceService` wrote the context's `merged()=true` flag (set when cycle 2 merged cycle 1's files via `MergeAgent`). But cycle 2's OWN mutation files were never merged to output. `FileVariantCollector` reads `ctx.merged()` → true → skips. Cycle 2's mutation is permanently treated as already merged.
  - **Root cause:** `CyclePersistenceService.persistSnapshot()` calls `mutationSnapshotService.writeMutationMeta(context)` at cycle end when `evolutionRanThisCycle()` is true. This overwrites the initial `meta.json` written by `MutationApplier` during evolution. The final context reflects post-promotion subgoalId and the aggregate cycle `merged()` flag — neither of which describes the mutation's own state.
  - **Fix:** `CyclePersistenceService` should NOT re-write `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` at cycle end. The initial write by `MutationApplier` during evolution is correct: it captures the subgoalId AT THE TIME of mutation and `merged=false`. Only `CycleMergeTracker.markCycleMerged()` should update the `merged` flag (to `true`) on that specific cycle's meta. Remove the `mutationSnapshotService.writeMutationMeta(context)` call from `CyclePersistenceService.persistSnapshot()`. The snapshot (which IS the full context with post-promotion state) should only go to `snapshots/<cycleId>.json`, not to `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json`.
  - **Changes (implemented 2026-04-20):** `CyclePersistenceService.java` — removed `writeMutationMeta(context)` call; replaced with `context.withMutationPath(...)` to preserve the path on the returned context without overwriting the file. `CycleExecutor.java` — added `persistEvaluationResultToMeta()` called immediately after `applyEvaluationInterpretationIfPresent()`, before `applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation()`. This reads the existing meta.json (correct subgoalId, merged=false) and updates ONLY `mutationState` with the evaluation result, making `FileVariantCollector`'s ACCEPTED filter work for both same-cycle and cross-cycle merge.
  - **Test:** `CyclePersistenceServiceTest` — verifies no `writeMutationMeta` call from `CyclePersistenceService`, and `mutationPath` is still set on the returned context.
  - **Depends on:** None (standalone fix). Must be fixed BEFORE T1-CU — otherwise the merge-before-fulfillment gate will still see wrong subgoalIds.

- [x] **T1-CU: Accepted mutations orphaned — fulfillment fires before merge runs** (`CycleExecutor`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `FileVariantCollector`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-16 03:47 session):** 7 of 9 cycles had `merged=false` despite ACCEPTED mutations. Only cycle 2 merged (cycle 1's files). Cycles 3, 5, 6, 8, 9 all produced accepted evolution output that sits in `mutations/` and never reached `output/`. The final output directory contains only the 6 files from the initial Book evolution — no Members, no Loans. Yet the root goal was declared FULFILLED.
  - **Root cause (two interacting mechanisms):**
    1. **Completion fires before merge:** `CycleExecutor` derives the completion decision at the end of the coordinator loop. If score-based closure fires (score above threshold + meaningful diff), the subgoal is FULFILLED and promoted IMMEDIATELY — before the merge agent has a chance to run in a subsequent coordinator iteration. In cycle 2, merge ran before completion (timing: cycle 1's pending variant was picked up by the coordinator). In cycle 3+, completion fired first, promoting the subgoal and ending the cycle.
    2. **T1-CE subgoal-scope filter blocks orphaned variants:** After promotion, `FileVariantCollector` only serves variants matching the NEW active subgoalId. The orphaned variant from the previous subgoal (with T1-CT also contributing wrong subgoalIds) is invisible. Even if the next cycle tries to merge, it sees "missing variant" → merge blocked.
  - **Consequence:** The system believes it is making progress (mutations are produced, evaluated, accepted) but no code ever reaches `output/`. The output directory freezes at whatever state the last actual merge produced.
  - **Fix — require merge before fulfillment:** In `CycleExecutor.deriveCycleResult()`, add a merge-occurrence gate to the score-based closure path. A subgoal should NOT be marked FULFILLED unless `context.merged()` is true OR the subgoal has `mergeCount > 0` on its `GoalEntity` (a prior cycle already merged for this subgoal). If the score meets the closure threshold but no merge has occurred, return `CompletionDecision[complete=false, reason=Merge not yet performed for accepted mutation]` and let the next coordinator iteration run merge.
  - **Alternative / complementary — ensure merge runs before completion:** In `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, after meta-step completion and before the completion decision, force a merge-eligibility check. If `hasAnyVariant(context)` returns true AND `mutationState == ACCEPTED`, run the merge agent BEFORE checking completion. This ensures the cycle always: evolve → evaluate → merge → complete, in that order.
  - **Edge case — EVOLUTION_NOOP path:** When `AutobuilderService` promotes a subgoal via EVOLUTION_NOOP (#NoDiff), no merge is needed (the code is already in output from a prior merge). The merge gate should NOT block this path. Guard: `if (context.hasSignal(EVOLUTION_NOOP)) { /* skip merge-gate check */ }`.
  - **Changes:** `CycleExecutor.java` — add `!context.merged() && goalEntity.getMergeCount() == 0` guard to the score-based closure branch (~5 lines). OR `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.java` — inject merge-before-completion step (~10 lines). `CycleExecutorTest` — add test: accepted + score above threshold + merged=false → complete=false.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CT (mutation meta subgoalId must be correct for FileVariantCollector to find the variant). T1-CS (EVOLUTION_NOOP guard prevents false NoDiff promotions that bypass merge).
  - **Note:** The `FileVariantCollector` subgoal-scoping filter (T1-CE) is correct — cross-subgoal contamination is a real problem. T1-CU's fix ensures merge happens BEFORE the subgoal advances, so the filter never needs to serve orphaned variants.


- [x] **T1-CX: `persistEvaluationResultToMeta` emits spurious warning on eval-only cycles** (`CycleExecutor`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-20 12:03 session):** Cycle-1776679429054 (NOOP mode, no evolution) logged: `T1-CT: could not update mutationState in mutations/cycle-1776679429054/meta.json: Snapshot meta not found`. This fires because `persistEvaluationResultToMeta` falls through to the `mutationPath` branch: `ctx.evolutionRanThisCycle()` is false, but `ctx.mutationPath()` is non-null (carried forward from cycle 1's `CyclePersistenceService.withMutationPath(...)` call). The method then attempts to read a meta.json under the previous cycle's path, finds nothing, and logs a warning.
  - **Root cause:** `CyclePersistenceService.persistSnapshot()` sets `mutationPath = "mutations/" + ctx.cycleName()` on the returned context whenever `evolutionRanThisCycle()` is true. When the NEXT cycle inherits this context (no evolution), `ctx.mutationPath()` is non-null but refers to a folder that belongs to the prior cycle. The IOException is logged at WARN, polluting logs on every eval-only cycle.
  - **Fix (2026-04-20):** Downgraded the IOException catch in `persistEvaluationResultToMeta` from `log.warn` to `log.debug`. The cross-cycle update path is preserved intact — the `mutationPath` branch still runs and attempts the update; it just no longer logs a warning when the prior cycle's meta.json is missing. No cycleName guard added (that would have broken legitimate cross-cycle evaluation-after-evolution updates).
  - **Changes:** `CycleExecutor.java` — `log.warn` → `log.debug` in the catch block (~1 line).
  - **Depends on:** T1-CT. Minor/cosmetic — no correctness impact.

- [x] **T1-CW: `NoMeaningfulChangeHeuristic` reference to removed `evolutionVerifyInPlace` field** (`NoMeaningfulChangeHeuristic`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-16 diff review):** The T2-CE/CF diff removed `evolutionVerifyInPlace` from `CycleContextData` and `EvaluationProtocolContract`, but `NoMeaningfulChangeHeuristic` still references it in the current diff (removal of 3 lines). This is a compile-time fix — the heuristic's verify-in-place guard should already be gone.
  - **Fix:** Verify the diff for `NoMeaningfulChangeHeuristic.java` correctly removes the `if (input.evolutionVerifyInPlace()) return HeuristicOpinion.none(NAME)` guard. If the heuristic has other guards that make this path dead code, no further action needed.
  - **Changes:** `NoMeaningfulChangeHeuristic.java` — remove verify-in-place guard (~3 lines). Already part of the current diff.

- [x] **T1-CC: LLM network failures bypass circuit breaker — requests can hang indefinitely** (`LLMClient`, `LlmCircuitBreaker`)
  - **Root cause:** `LLMClient.ask()` only catches `HttpStatusCodeException` (HTTP error responses). Network-level failures — connection timeout, read timeout, connection refused, stalled local LLM — throw `ResourceAccessException` instead and propagate uncaught, bypassing the circuit breaker entirely. `RestTemplate` is constructed with no explicit timeouts (`SimpleClientHttpRequestFactory` defaults), so a local LLM that stalls mid-generation hangs the scheduler thread indefinitely.
  - **Fix A — add timeouts to RestTemplate:** Replace `new RestTemplate()` with a `RestTemplate` backed by `SimpleClientHttpRequestFactory` with `connectTimeout=5_000ms` and `readTimeout` configurable via `llm.api.read-timeout-ms` (default `120_000ms` — generous for local models). Add the config key to `application.yml`.
  - **Fix B — catch `ResourceAccessException` in `ask()`:** In the `catch` block alongside `HttpStatusCodeException`, also catch `ResourceAccessException` (Spring's wrapper for `IOException`, including timeout). Call `circuitBreaker.recordNetworkFailure()`. Add `recordNetworkFailure()` to `LlmCircuitBreaker` — routes to the same count-based threshold path as 5xx errors (increment counter, open after `CONSECUTIVE_FAILURE_THRESHOLD`). Network failures use the existing backoff rather than permanent open, since they are transient by nature.
  - **Changes:** `LLMClient.java` (~8 lines), `LlmCircuitBreaker.java` (add `recordNetworkFailure()`, ~6 lines), `application.yml` (add `llm.api.read-timeout-ms: 120000`).
  - **Note:** `connectTimeout` should be short (5s) — if the local server isn't listening that quickly it won't start. `readTimeout` must be long — local models can take 30–90s per response.

- [x] **T1-CZ: Analysis-spawned children cascade-abandon a criteria-satisfied parent** (`GoalPlannerService`, `AnalysisAgent`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-22 00:50 session, run 20260422_2):** POST /loans (`goal-…`) accumulated all 5 acceptance criteria as `✓` with `mergeCount > 0`. AnalysisAgent then injected 2 child subgoals under it (`analysisInjectionCount=2`). Those children stagnated and were GIVEN_UP. The cascade propagated upward and the parent was marked GIVEN_UP with `resolution="superseded by replanning"`, despite criteria already being banked as satisfied. This removes ~1/8 of the first-wave completion ratio on every occurrence and contributed to the run being marked GIVEN_UP at 3/8 instead of recognising the implicit 4/8.
  - **Root cause:** `GoalPlannerService.markGoalGivenUp()` (or the analysis-injection path that triggers it via `AutobuilderService`) does not consult the parent's `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` before cascading. The cascade is symmetric: a child's exhaustion propagates regardless of whether the parent has independent evidence of completion. Analysis-spawned children are meant to be *augmentations* (extra verification, polish) — their failure does not mean the parent's original criteria are unmet.
  - **Fix — two options:**
    - **A (preferred, minimal):** Before cascading GIVEN_UP from an analysis-spawned child to its parent, consult `areCriteriaCumulativelySatisfied(parentId)` (T1-CF helper). If true and `mergeCount > 0`, promote the parent to FULFILLED instead and do not cascade. The analysis-spawned child is left GIVEN_UP on its own merits.
    - **B (stricter invariant):** Tag subgoals produced by `AnalysisAgent.autoInjectExecutableSubGoals` with `metadata["spawnedBy"]="analysis"`. In the cascade path in `markGoalGivenUp()`, children with `spawnedBy=analysis` do not propagate upward — their exhaustion is self-contained regardless of the parent's criteria state.
    - Option A is narrower and builds on existing T1-CF infrastructure. Option B is more defensible long-term because it encodes the semantic difference between planner-intent children (siblings of the parent's work) and analysis-spawned children (augmentations on top).
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — add parent-criteria check (Option A) or spawner-check (Option B) before cascade propagation in `markGoalGivenUp()`. `AnalysisAgent.java` — tag injected goals with `spawnedBy=analysis` metadata (Option B). Tests: parent fulfilled with analysis children exhausted → parent stays FULFILLED; regular planner-sibling children still cascade as today.
  - **Depends on:** T1-BG (cumulativeCriteriaStatus), T1-CF (the FULFILLED promotion mechanism). T2-BW already tracks `analysisInjectionCount` per subgoal — spawner-tagging can reuse the same write path.
  - **Enables:** Correct first-wave completion ratios (T1-CQ / T2-CH) when analysis has decorated a completed subgoal with follow-up children.
  - **Landed (2026-04-30):** Option B implemented. `addSubGoal()` tags all goals injected with role `"analysis"` with `metadata["spawnedBy"]="analysis"`. `exhaustParentIfStuck()` filters `regularSiblings` (non-analysis) before computing `givenUpCount`, `completedCount`, and `effectiveThreshold` — analysis-spawned children are excluded entirely. Secondary T1-CZ check: if `hasAnalysisChildren && allChildrenTerminal && areCriteriaCumulativelySatisfied(parentId)`, promote parent to FULFILLED via `applyGoalProgress`. Four new tests: `addSubGoal_analysisRole_tagsSpawnedByAnalysis`, `addSubGoal_plannerRole_doesNotTagSpawnedBy`, `exhaustParentIfStuck_onlyAnalysisChildrenExhausted_criteriaNotSatisfied_doesNotExhaust`, `exhaustParentIfStuck_onlyAnalysisChildrenExhausted_criteriaSatisfied_promotesParentToFulfilled`, `exhaustParentIfStuck_mixedRegularAndAnalysisChildren_firesOnRegularThreshold`, `exhaustParentIfStuck_analysisChildrenAloneDoNotMeetRegularThreshold_noExhaustion`. Full suite 822 green.

- [x] **T1-DA: Planner `#SubGoalRemove` strands accepted-but-unmerged variant** (`PlannerAgent.processRemovals`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-28 23:17 session, cycle-1777411258630):** Subgoal `goal-15c2a0f1` (POST /loans/{id}/return) was active at cycle start. EvolutionAgent produced LoanController + LoanService (2 file changes, mutation `9ccabd35`). EvaluationAgent: `globalScore=0.75`, ACCEPTED. HeuristicNegotiator: ACCEPTED `score=0.704`. Coordinator then ran the planner, which obeyed a stale "subgoal is stuck" prompt nudge and issued `#SubGoalRemove goal-15c2a0f1`. `processRemovals()` marked it GIVEN_UP and advanced the active subgoal to `goal-5a38815c` (GET /loans?memberId). MergeAgent never ran for this cycle. `meta.json` shows `currentSubGoalId=goal-15c2a0f1, mutationState=ACCEPTED, merged=false` — orphaned. Next cycle: `MERGE: canExecute=false; blockers=missing variant` (T1-CE filter rejects the variant whose meta says `goal-15c2a0f1` while the active subgoal is `goal-5a38815c`). Knock-on: cycle 10 evolution declared `#NoDiff` (its `previousOutput` already showed the un-merged Loan code as the in-context state), wasting another cycle. Net: a successful evolution+evaluation produced zero output; one slot of the 8-subgoal first wave failed to count as a merge, contributing to the run being declared `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` at the 4/8 ratio threshold in cycle 11.
  - **Root cause:** T1-CU added a merge-occurrence gate to *score-based* fulfillment in `CycleExecutor.applyFulfillmentIfNeeded` (line 312, the `mutationState==ACCEPTED && !merged && mergeCount==0` guard). It does NOT cover the *planner-driven* prune path. `PlannerAgent.processRemovals()` calls `goalPlannerService.markGoalGivenUp(...)` then `setCurrentSubGoal(next)` with no check for pending accepted variants on the subgoal being pruned. So the same orphaning failure that T1-CU fixed for fulfillment can still happen one branch over.
  - **Fix — primary (structural guard in `processRemovals`):** Before calling `markGoalGivenUp(g.getId(), "superseded by replanning")`, check whether the subgoal has a pending accepted-but-unmerged variant. Conditions: `g.getId().equals(ctx.currentSubGoalId()) && ctx.mutationState()==ACCEPTED && !ctx.merged() && !ctx.hasSignal(EVOLUTION_NOOP)`. When true, refuse the prune for this iteration: `log.warn("T1-DA: refusing to prune subgoal [{}] — accepted mutation pending merge", g.getId())` and skip. Subsequent coordinator iterations can run merge first; the planner can re-attempt prune next cycle if it still considers the subgoal unwanted.
  - **Fix — alternative (force merge before prune):** In `PlannerAgent.execute()`, before calling `processRemovals(outcome)`, invoke `MergeAgent.execute()` if `ctx.mutationState()==ACCEPTED && !ctx.merged() && hasAnyVariant(ctx)`. This drains pending variants before the planner can disturb subgoal pointers. Less invasive on planner semantics but adds cross-agent orchestration coupling — option A (structural guard) is simpler.
  - **Fix — secondary (prompt hygiene, optional):** The planner was nudged by `⚠ This subgoal is stuck (REPLAN_REQUESTED signal (subgoal is stuck))` because `REPLAN_REQUESTED` was alive from earlier cycles 5–6 stagnation, but the freshly accepted mutation in cycle 9 contradicted it. Consider clearing `REPLAN_REQUESTED` in `EvaluationAgent` (or via `ContextLineageTracker`) when the cycle's negotiated state is ACCEPTED. This is a behavioural-quality follow-up, not a correctness gate — the structural guard (primary fix) makes the loop correct regardless of prompt quality.
  - **Changes:** `PlannerAgent.java` — add the pending-variant check at the top of the loop in `processRemovals(...)` (~6 lines). Tests: `processRemovals_refusesPruneWhenAcceptedVariantPending`, `processRemovals_allowsPruneWhenMutationStateRejected`, `processRemovals_allowsPruneWhenAlreadyMerged`, `processRemovals_allowsPruneWhenEvolutionNoOp`.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CT (mutation meta subgoalId is the source of truth for "which subgoal owns this variant"). T1-CU (companion fix — same invariant, different trigger). T1-BG (acceptance state on context).
  - **Enables:** First-wave merge accounting (T1-CQ, T2-CH) is no longer undercounted by planner prune races.

- [x] **T1-DB: T2-CH defers completion but leaves `currentSubGoalId=null` when active replacement subgoals exist** *(implemented 2026-04-29)* (`GoalPlannerService.getCurrentSubGoal`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-29 02:03 run):** After the first replacement subgoal (`goal-31a14c41`) was FULFILLED in cycle 10, `promoteCurrentSubGoal` advanced `currentSubGoalId` to null (no more siblings). The planner then ran mid-cycle and injected 3 more replacements under the GIVEN_UP parent (`goal-2c6bcdde`). From cycle 11 onward: `getCurrentSubGoal()` sees `currentSubGoalId=null` → calls `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` → T2-CH defers ("active replacement subgoals exist") → returns null. The 3 active replacement subgoals are never pointed to. Planner says "3 active subgoals already in backlog" every cycle but `currentSubGoalId` stays null and evolution is blocked by T1-BP. 22 cycles wasted, run manually aborted.
  - **Root cause:** `getCurrentSubGoal()`'s null-subgoal branch has no recovery path that calls `resolveNextActiveLeaf`. It goes straight to `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()`, which T2-CH defers. Nothing ever advances the pointer to the waiting active subgoals.
  - **Fix:** In `getCurrentSubGoal()`, in the `else` branch (root is ACTIVE, `currentSubGoalId=null`), call `resolveNextActiveLeaf(rootGoalId)` as the FIRST step. If it returns a non-null leaf, set `currentSubGoalId = leaf`, `ensureAttemptIdsFor(leaf)`, log the recovery, and return `goalMap.get(leaf)`. Only if `resolveNextActiveLeaf` returns null proceed to the existing T1-CN → T1-BK → autoComplete path. This mirrors the identical recovery pattern already in `promoteCurrentSubGoal()` (line 656).
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — add `resolveNextActiveLeaf` recovery block at top of the `else` branch in `getCurrentSubGoal()` (~6 lines). `GoalPlannerServiceTest` — test: `currentSubGoalId=null` + active replacements in graph → `getCurrentSubGoal()` returns the first active leaf and sets pointer.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CP (the T1-CP replacement-nesting pattern is what creates this scenario). T2-CH (the deferral gate is correct — this fix just adds recovery before it is needed).

- [x] **T1-DC: `mutationState` propagation is post-cycle — agents in same cycle read stale state (supersedes T1-DA)** (`CycleExecutor`, `EvaluationAgent`, `PlannerAgent`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-29 20:42 run):** 3 ACCEPTED mutations orphaned across 17 cycles (cycles 267168, 358472, 460271). T1-DA's guard never fired in any of them despite the runtime having T1-DA active (compiled at 02:22, run at 20:42; `grep T1-DA` on the log returns zero refusal entries). All three were lost via planner `#SubGoalRemove` of the active subgoal while a freshly-accepted (or pending) mutation was in flight, exactly the scenario T1-DA was designed to prevent.
  - **Root cause:** `CycleExecutor.applyEvaluationInterpretationIfPresent()` (line 355) is the **only** place that writes `mutationState` to the context (comment at line 371 makes this explicit). It runs at line 135 — **after** `adaptiveExecutionEngine.runCycle()` returns at line 125. So during the entire signal-driven loop (where the planner runs and may issue `#SubGoalRemove`), `context.mutationState()` is whatever it was at cycle start — never reflects evaluation's same-cycle decision. T1-DA's check `context.mutationState() == ACCEPTED` is therefore always false in the same cycle as evaluation; the guard is structurally unreachable. The same staleness affects `ROLE_MERGE` (emitted in `applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation` at line 461, also post-`runCycle`) — which is why the merge agent in the signal-driven loop always reports `missing ROLE_MERGE` even after evaluation accepts. `EvaluationAgent` itself writes `evaluationOutcome`, `lastEvaluationOutcome`, and `evaluationInterpretation` (line 225-231) — those are the fields that *are* fresh during the signal-driven phase.
  - **Why this is architectural, not symptom:** T1-DA, T1-CT (persist `mutationState` to meta.json), T1-CU (merge-occurrence gate on fulfillment), the `missing variant` filter — all patches around the same single-writer constraint. Each patch covers one consumer; new consumers that read stale `mutationState` need new patches. The constraint itself is the problem.
  - **Fix — primary (move `mutationState` write into EvaluationAgent):** In `EvaluationAgent.execute()` after line 228 (`.withEvaluationInterpretation(result)`), also call `.withMutationState(result.mutationState()).withGoalProgress(result.goalProgress())`. Delete `applyEvaluationInterpretationIfPresent` from `CycleExecutor` (or shrink to a no-op verification). Same-cycle agents (planner, merge, analysis) now see the up-to-date state. `ROLE_MERGE` should likewise be emitted by EvaluationAgent on ACCEPTED outcomes so the merge agent in the signal-driven loop becomes usable. Removes the entire class of "stale `mutationState` read" bugs.
  - **Fix — secondary (run merge inline immediately after evaluation accepts):** Builds on the primary fix. In the signal-driven loop, after EvaluationAgent returns ACCEPTED, run merge synchronously before yielding to other agents. The mutation is committed before any subsequent agent (planner) can disturb the subgoal pointer. Eliminates the need for any prune guard.
  - **Removes:** **T1-DA** can be deleted entirely (the elaborate four-condition guard becomes dead code; planner naturally sees `mutationState=ACCEPTED` and any sane removal heuristic skips the active subgoal in that state). T1-CT becomes simpler (no cross-cycle persistence dance needed). The T1-DA tests (`processRemovals_refusesPruneWhenAcceptedVariantPending`, etc.) get replaced by tests verifying that EvaluationAgent sets `mutationState` and that the planner (next agent in the cycle) reads `ACCEPTED`.
  - **Residual case (small remaining guard):** Cycle 267168 in the same run had a *pending* (unevaluated) mutation when the planner pruned. Even with the primary fix, `mutationState` is null at planner time because evaluation hasn't run. A one-line guard checking `lastEvolutionFingerprint() != lastEvaluatedEvolutionFingerprint()` (i.e. "evolution proposed something this cycle that hasn't been evaluated") still has value. Much smaller surface area than current T1-DA.
  - **Changes:** `EvaluationAgent.java` — add `.withMutationState(...).withGoalProgress(...)` to the context-builder chain at lines 225-231; emit `ROLE_MERGE` signal when `result.mutationState() == ACCEPTED`. `CycleExecutor.java` — remove or shrink `applyEvaluationInterpretationIfPresent`. `PlannerAgent.java` — delete the T1-DA guard in `processRemovals`; optionally add the unevaluated-mutation one-liner. Tests: same-cycle planner sees `mutationState=ACCEPTED` after evaluation; same-cycle merge runs in signal-driven loop; existing T1-DA tests deleted or rewritten against the new contract.
  - **Depends on:** Nothing — this is the foundational ordering fix.
  - **Supersedes:** T1-DA (delete after this lands).
  - **Landed (2026-04-29):** EvaluationAgent now chains `.withMutationState(result.mutationState()).withGoalProgress(result.goalProgress())` and emits `ROLE_MERGE` (1.0/1.0/"evaluation-accepted") when `result.mutationState() == ACCEPTED`. CycleExecutor.applyEvaluationInterpretationIfPresent removed. PlannerAgent.processRemovals's T1-DA four-condition guard replaced with the one-line residual: refuse when `g.getId().equals(currentSubGoalId) && lastEvolutionFingerprint != null && lastEvolutionFingerprint != lastEvaluatedEvolutionFingerprint`. Old T1-DA test `processRemovals_refusesPruneWhenAcceptedVariantPending` replaced; added `processRemovals_refusesPruneWhenEvolutionUnevaluated`, `processRemovals_allowsPruneWhenFingerprintsMatch`, `processRemovals_allowsPruneWhenNoEvolutionFingerprint`, plus EvaluationAgent tests for in-context `mutationState`/`goalProgress` writes and ROLE_MERGE emission. Full suite green (793 tests).

- [x] **T1-DD: Tree-edge integrity — parent terminal status must reconcile with descendants** (`GoalPlannerService.markGoalCompleted`, `markGoalGivenUp`, `applyGoalProgress`, `promoteCurrentSubGoal`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-29 22:24 run, 46 cycles):** End-of-run goal tree had non-leaf subgoals marked COMPLETED while their direct children were still ACTIVE or GIVEN_UP. Concrete pattern: `applyGoalProgress(goalId=…, FULFILLED)` fired through the *non-current* branch (`GoalPlannerService.java:1626-1628`), which calls `markGoalCompleted(goalId)` directly with no descendant inspection. The children remain in the graph but are unreachable: `getRemainingGoals()` excludes them (parent is COMPLETED), and the next `resolveNextActiveLeaf` walk can return them only if it re-enters through the now-terminal parent. Operator-visible symptom: subgoals appear to vanish; the run ends GIVEN_UP at a worse first-wave ratio than the actual completion state warrants. Also exercised by `AutobuilderService` line 393 (#NoDiff promotion path) when the noOp subgoal is mid-tree.
  - **Root cause:** `markGoalCompleted(id, resolution)` (line 1085) is a status flip with a Neo4j save — it has no awareness of children. `promoteCurrentSubGoal` (line 663) does check `findNextSiblingOrAncestor` and triggers a planner call when siblings are non-active, but it never blocks parent-completion on ACTIVE descendants. `applyGoalProgress(goalId, FULFILLED)` for the non-current case is the worst offender: it short-circuits straight to `markGoalCompleted` with no integrity check at all. T1-CZ covers the *cascade-from-failed-children* direction; the *parent-orphans-children* direction is uncovered.
  - **Why this is correctness, not steering:** A goal-tree node whose status contradicts its descendants is structurally inconsistent. Downstream queries (`getRemainingGoals`, `resolveNextActiveLeaf`, `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` ratio computation, the SSE goal-state snapshots, the JSON `GoalStack.json`) all assume the invariant. Once it breaks, every consumer drifts. This is the same class of bug as T1-CB (root status clobbered by `getCurrentSubGoal`) and T1-CJ (root auto-completed via scope thrash) — invariant on the goal tree, not a steering choice.
  - **Fix — primary (refuse-completion guard):** In `markGoalCompleted(id, resolution)`, before flipping status, query `getSubGoals(id)` and refuse the flip if any direct child is ACTIVE. Two policies:
    - **A (defer):** log `T1-DD: refusing to mark [{id}] COMPLETED — N active descendants. Re-pointing currentSubGoalId to next active leaf.` Call `resolveNextActiveLeaf(id)` and re-point `currentSubGoalId` to it. Parent stays ACTIVE, gets promoted naturally when descendants finish.
    - **B (cascade-on-completion, dangerous):** mark all ACTIVE descendants COMPLETED with resolution `"cascade from parent fulfilled"`. Strictly stronger but loses information about subgoals that were genuinely incomplete. **Do not pick B.**
  - **Fix — secondary (`applyGoalProgress` non-current branch):** The path at line 1626-1628 should call `markGoalCompleted` via the same primary-fix guard. No second code path.
  - **Fix — tertiary (debug-only invariant assertion):** At end of `runCycle`, add `assertGoalTreeIntegrity()` (debug log only): walk every COMPLETED node; if it has any non-terminal descendant, log `WARN T1-DD: tree integrity violation goalId={} status=COMPLETED activeChildren={}`. Helps detect a regression here before symptoms accumulate.
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — guard at top of `markGoalCompleted` (~6 lines); update `applyGoalProgress` non-current branch to log `T1-DD: skipped — active descendants` when guard fires (~3 lines); `assertGoalTreeIntegrity` end-of-cycle hook (~12 lines, behind a debug flag). Tests: parent with one ACTIVE child → `markGoalCompleted(parentId)` is a no-op and `currentSubGoalId` advances to the child; parent with all-terminal children → completion proceeds as today; root-completion path unchanged.
  - **Depends on:** Nothing structurally, but pairs with T1-CZ (the opposite cascade direction) — implementing both together gives a coherent tree-edge invariant.
  - **Enables:** First-wave completion ratio (T1-CQ / T2-CH) becomes trustworthy. Coverage-review (T1-DE) can rely on goal-tree state being consistent rather than guessing what is "really" terminal.
  - **Landed (2026-04-30):** Primary guard added at top of `GoalPlannerService.markGoalCompleted(id, resolution)`: when any direct child is `ACTIVE`, the flip is refused, `currentSubGoalId` is re-pointed to `resolveNextActiveLeaf(id)`, and a WARN `T1-DD: refusing to mark [{id}] COMPLETED — N active descendants` line is emitted. Secondary: `applyGoalProgress` non-current FULFILLED branch now logs `T1-DD: skipped — applyGoalProgress non-current FULFILLED for goalId=… kept ACTIVE due to active descendants` when the post-call status remains `ACTIVE`. Tertiary: new public `assertGoalTreeIntegrity()` walks every COMPLETED node, logs WARN per violation, returns the count; called once per cycle in `CycleExecutor.runCycle` after the score recorder + interpretation block. Pre-existing test `getCurrentSubGoal_T1CB_whenRootCompletedAndCurrentSubGoalIdNull_returnsNull` updated to mark the leaf before root (the prior setup relied on the bug T1-DD now prevents). Six new tests in `GoalPlannerServiceTest`: parent-with-active-child no-op + re-pointing, parent-with-all-terminal-children proceeds, root-completion path unchanged, applyGoalProgress non-current FULFILLED no-op, integrity assertion fires + returns 1 on a forced bad state, integrity assertion returns 0 on a clean tree. Full suite 813 green.

- [x] **T1-DE: Coverage-review re-injects given-up subgoals without dedup limit** (`GoalPlannerService.triggerManualCoverageReview`, `GoalSimilarityService`, `CoverageRetrospectiveService`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-29 22:24 run):** Operator triggered `POST /coverage-review` three times during the session (lines 17970, 37787 of the log; a third invocation at 22:50 visible in tail). Each invocation logged `coverage-review: found N missing feature(s) — injecting as first-wave subgoals` with N=16, then 38. Many of the "missing features" the LLM emitted were paraphrases of subgoals already present in the tree as GIVEN_UP from earlier runs/coverage-review iterations. Result: the first-wave denominator inflated from 8 to 16 to 24 (visible in the second `T1-BZ/T1-CQ` log at line 37268: `totalDirectChildren=16`), driving the completion ratio down from 4/8=0.50 to 3/8=0.38 without any new merges actually closing the gap. The system then re-issued GOAL_EXHAUSTED on a worse ratio than the prior cycle.
  - **Root cause:** `GoalPlannerService.triggerManualCoverageReview()` (line 1416) calls `addSubGoalsBatch(missing, rootGoalId, true, "coverage-retro", null)` directly. `addSubGoalsBatch` (line 971) does not consult `GoalSimilarityService`. The `GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT = 1` constant + `countGivenUpMatches` semantic-similarity check only exists inside `PlannerAgent.inject()` and `PlannerAgent.planInto()` (lines 315-318, 417-420). Coverage-review bypasses the planner agent entirely. `CoverageRetrospectiveService.findMissingFeatures` does pass the GIVEN_UP descriptions to the LLM in the prompt (the "Work items attempted" section), but the LLM consistently re-emits paraphrases anyway — soft prompt instructions are not a substitute for hard dedup.
  - **Why this is correctness:** GIVEN_UP retry quota was a deliberate decision (T1-CI, T1-N, T2-BO) to stop the planner from churning on unachievable goals. Coverage-review's bypass silently undoes that decision. The session-budget for coverage-review is also unbounded — operator can invoke as many times as they like without backpressure.
  - **Fix — primary (route through similarity dedup):** Before `addSubGoalsBatch(missing, ...)`, filter `missing` through:
    ```java
    List<String> filtered = new ArrayList<>();
    for (String desc : missing) {
        long givenUpCount = similarityService.countGivenUpMatches(desc);
        if (givenUpCount >= GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT) {
            log.warn("T1-DE: dropping coverage-review proposal '{}' — already GIVEN_UP {} time(s) (limit={})",
                    desc, givenUpCount, GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT);
            continue;
        }
        filtered.add(desc);
    }
    ```
    Use the same `GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT` constant (move from `PlannerAgent` to `GoalSimilarityService` or a shared `DedupConstants`). Return both `injected` and `dropped` counts in the REST response so the UI can show "16 found, 12 dropped as duplicates of GIVEN_UP".
  - **Fix — secondary (per-session budget):** Track `consecutiveCoverageReviewsWithoutMerge` in `GoalPlannerService` (or `AutobuilderService`). Increment on each `triggerManualCoverageReview()` call; reset on any merge. If it exceeds `MAX_COVERAGE_REVIEWS_WITHOUT_MERGE = 2`, refuse the call with a 409 Conflict response carrying the reason. Forces the operator to pause and inspect rather than thrash.
  - **Fix — tertiary (LLM prompt tightening):** In `CoverageRetrospectiveService.buildPrompt` add a rule "Do NOT propose work items that paraphrase any of the GIVEN_UP entries above. If everything significant has been attempted, respond #COMPLETE." Cheap belt-and-braces — primary fix doesn't rely on it.
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.triggerManualCoverageReview` — add the dedup filter and budget counter (~20 lines). `PlannerAgent.GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT` → move to `DedupConstants` or `GoalSimilarityService`. `AutobuilderController` (line 75) — return `{injected, dropped, droppedExamples}` instead of the bare list. `CoverageRetrospectiveService.buildPrompt` — append the no-paraphrase rule. Tests: `triggerManualCoverageReview_dropsParaphrasedGivenUpProposals`, `triggerManualCoverageReview_refusesAfterTwoConsecutiveNoMergeInvocations`, `triggerManualCoverageReview_resetsBudgetOnMerge`.
  - **Depends on:** T2-BO (GIVEN_UP retry limit infrastructure). T1-DD recommended for ordering — the budget counter relies on accurate "merge happened" signals which T1-DD makes consistent.
  - **Enables:** Coverage-review can be re-positioned as a cheap operator-driven exhaustion bypass without becoming an unbounded thrash trigger.
  - **Landed (2026-04-30):** Primary: `GoalSimilarityService.GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT = 1` (public static); `PlannerAgent` local constant replaced with reference to it. `GoalPlannerService` gets `@Setter @Autowired(required=false) GoalSimilarityService goalSimilarityService`; dedup loop filters each LLM proposal through `countGivenUpMatches` before injection; dropped count surfaced in `CoverageReviewResult`. Secondary: `consecutiveCoverageReviewsWithoutMerge` counter added; `MAX_COVERAGE_REVIEWS_WITHOUT_MERGE = 2`; budget refused with `budgetExceeded=true`; counter reset in `incrementGoalMergeCount`. Tertiary: `CoverageRetrospectiveService.buildPrompt` adds "Do NOT propose GIVEN_UP paraphrases" rule. New record `CoverageReviewResult(injected, dropped, budgetExceeded)` replaces bare `List<String>` throughout (`triggerManualCoverageReview`, `triggerCoverageReview`, controller). Controller returns 409 + `{budgetExceeded, message}` on budget exceeded; otherwise returns `{gapsFound, injectedCount, droppedCount, injectedFeatures}`. Three new tests: `dropsParaphrasedGivenUpProposals`, `refusesAfterBudgetExhausted`, `mergeResetsBudget`. Existing six `triggerManualCoverageReview` tests updated to use `result.injected()`. Full suite 822 green.

- [x] **T1-DG: `planningRunId` never increments — T1-CQ first-wave-only ratio filter is silently dead code** (`PlannerAgent`, `GoalPlannerService`, T1-AD Fix B re-plan path, `CoverageRetrospectiveService`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-30 17:34 run):** Final `GoalStack.json` shows all 15 direct subgoals of the root carry `planningRunId: 1` — both the 8 first-wave subgoals from the initial plan AND the 7 second-wave subgoals injected during the cycle-7 re-plan. T1-CQ's `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` ratio uses "direct children whose `planningRunId` equals the minimum among direct children" to filter to the first-wave only, so it can determine "did the originally-planned work succeed?" without later replan waves diluting the denominator. With every subgoal sharing run-id 1, the filter is a no-op: all 15 are treated as first-wave, the ratio resolves to 12/15 = 0.80 instead of the correct 8/8 = 1.00. The run was therefore reported as "root goal satisfied: 12/15 first-wave children completed (ratio=0.80)" when it should have read "8/8 first-wave completed (ratio=1.00)" — and would have hit that resolution at end of cycle 7, before the duplicate-injection waste.
  - **Root cause:** Whichever planning entrypoint injected the second-wave (T1-AD Fix B null-subgoal re-plan, or `PlannerAgent.injectOutcomeGoals`, or coverage retrospective) does not increment a per-invocation counter and stamp it onto each new subgoal's metadata. T1-CQ documents that `planningRunId` is "already stamped on each subgoal" and uses it as the discriminator, but the stamping site only assigns the initial run-id; subsequent invocations re-use the same value.
  - **Fix:**
    1. Add `int currentPlanningRunId` field on `GoalPlannerService` (or persist on the root goal's metadata so it survives restart). Default 1.
    2. Add `int beginPlanningRun()` that atomically increments the counter and returns the new value. Call it at the entry of every planner invocation: `PlannerAgent.execute()` (initial bootstrap), `PlannerAgent.planInto()` (T1-AD Fix B null-subgoal re-plan), and `CoverageRetrospectiveService.findMissingFeatures` callsite in `triggerManualCoverageReview`.
    3. Pass the returned run-id through `addSubGoal`/`addSubGoalsBatch` and stamp it on each new `GoalEntity.metadata.planningRunId`.
    4. Confirm T1-CQ's filter then sees distinct run-ids and correctly restricts the ratio denominator to the first wave.
  - **Verification:** Re-run a goal where the planner is forced to re-fire (e.g. set up a session that reaches null-subgoal recovery), inspect `GoalStack.json` — first-wave subgoals should carry `planningRunId: 1`, second-wave `planningRunId: 2`, etc. T1-CQ's auto-complete log should reference the correct first-wave count.
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — add field, accessor, `beginPlanningRun()` (~10 lines). `PlannerAgent.java` — call `beginPlanningRun()` at start of `execute()` and `planInto()`; pass run-id through to `addSubGoal*` calls (~6 lines). `addSubGoalsBatch` / `addSubGoal` — accept and stamp `planningRunId` (~3 lines). `GoalEntity.java` already supports the metadata field. `CoverageRetrospectiveService.java` — same pattern at the injection callsite (~3 lines). `Neo4jGoalGraphService` — confirm metadata round-trip. Tests: `beginPlanningRun_incrementsAndStamps`, `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied_excludesSecondWaveByPlanningRunId` (fixture with 8 first-wave COMPLETED + 4 second-wave GIVEN_UP → root COMPLETED, not GIVEN_UP).
  - **Depends on:** T1-CQ (defines the consumer of this counter; T1-DG makes it work).
  - **Enables:** T1-CQ to actually filter (currently dormant), and indirectly T1-DH (pre-plan completion check is a different fix to the same observed symptom — together they make the cycle-7 boundary correct).
  - **Observed payoff:** Run 17:34 would have terminated cleanly at end of cycle 7 with root COMPLETED (8/8) instead of cycling through cycles 8–10 wasting LLM calls on duplicates and finishing at 12/15.

- [x] **T1-DH: Pre-plan completion check — `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` must be consulted before re-planning fires on null subgoal** (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `AutobuilderService`, `GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-30 17:34 run):** End of cycle 7: last first-wave subgoal `goal-0cd06185` (GET /loans?memberId) was promoted to FULFILLED via T2-CC. `currentSubGoalId` advanced to null because no more first-wave was active. T1-AD Fix B's "allow exactly one re-plan attempt" then triggered the planner; the planner emitted 7 #SubGoalAppend entries for endpoints already represented by COMPLETED first-wave siblings. Had `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` been consulted before T1-AD Fix B injected the re-plan, root would have been marked COMPLETED at 8/8 and no re-plan would have run.
  - **Root cause:** T1-CN added an unconditional `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` call inside `GoalPlannerService.promoteCurrentSubGoal()` — but the T1-AD Fix B re-plan path does not always reach that branch in the cycle where the last subgoal completes. The re-plan can fire from a different entrypoint (engine-level null-subgoal handler) that bypasses the auto-complete check; by the time `promoteCurrentSubGoal` runs in the next cycle, the planner has already injected duplicates, the direct-children list now includes second-wave entries, and the ratio path no longer sees a clean 8/8.
  - **Fix:**
    1. Identify every entrypoint that triggers planner re-invocation when `currentSubGoalId == null` (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine` null-subgoal handler, `AutobuilderService.handleSubGoalExhaustion`, T1-AD Fix B path, T1-CA injection-on-empty-backlog). Audit list against the call graph.
    2. At each such entrypoint, call `goalPlannerService.autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()` *before* invoking the planner. If it marks the root COMPLETED or GIVEN_UP, abort the re-plan, emit `GOAL_FULFILLED` (or `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` for the GIVEN_UP path) and let the cycle terminate.
    3. Use T1-DG's `planningRunId` filter so the ratio reflects first-wave reality, not all direct children.
  - **Composability with T1-DG:** Either fix alone partially helps; together they close the gap. T1-DG ensures the ratio uses only the first wave (so even if a re-plan does fire, the root completion can still resolve correctly later); T1-DH prevents the re-plan from firing in the first place when first-wave is already complete.
  - **Changes:** `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.java` — pre-plan completion check at the null-subgoal branch (~5 lines + log). `AutobuilderService.java` — same pattern at any direct planner-trigger callsite (~5 lines). Tests: `nullSubgoal_with8of8FirstWaveCompleted_skipsReplanAndEmitsGoalFulfilled`, `nullSubgoal_with4of8FirstWaveGivenUp_4Completed_emitsGoalExhausted`, `nullSubgoal_withFirstWaveStillActive_proceedsToReplan`.
  - **Depends on:** T1-CN (the auto-complete delegation it introduced is the call target). T1-DG (the ratio filter must work for the auto-complete to make the right decision; without T1-DG the call still produces wrong answers when prior re-plans already injected dilutive children).
  - **Note:** This ticket and T1-DG together would have eliminated cycles 8–10 entirely on the 17:34 run.

- [x] **T1-DJ: `deriveCycleResult` mis-classifies COMPLETED root as `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` when subgoal pointer is null** (`CycleExecutor.deriveCycleResult`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-30 17:34 run, cycle 11):** Cycle started with `currentSubGoalId=null` (root had been auto-completed at the end of cycle 10 with COMPLETED status, ratio 12/15). Deliberation returned `PAUSE` mode with a generic 5-step "implement the Books resource" plan (which never executed). Cycle ended with `resultState=GOAL_EXHAUSTED` despite the root goal being recorded as COMPLETED in the goal tree. Downstream effect: `AutobuilderService` halted with the wrong terminal narrative (`GOAL_EXHAUSTED` triggers `SYSTEM_PAUSED + human-review` semantics), masking the genuine completion in dashboards/logs.
  - **Root cause:** `CycleExecutor.deriveCycleResult` derives the terminal state from cycle-local signals (`hasSignal(GOAL_EXHAUSTED)`, `hasSignal(SUBGOAL_EXHAUSTED)`, etc.) without checking the root goal's terminal status. If `currentSubGoalId == null` and a `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` signal is present (or inferred from absence of progress), the result is set to `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` even when the root has already been marked COMPLETED elsewhere. This is the same bug class as T1-CB (`getCurrentSubGoal` clobbering GIVEN_UP root) but on the result-derivation side rather than the goal-state mutation side.
  - **Fix:** In `CycleExecutor.deriveCycleResult`, before returning `GOAL_EXHAUSTED`, check `goalPlannerService.getRootGoal().getStatus()`:
    - `COMPLETED` → return `GOAL_FULFILLED`.
    - `GIVEN_UP` → return `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` (existing behaviour, root genuinely failed).
    - `ACTIVE` → return `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` (existing behaviour, root cannot make progress).
  - **Defensive companion:** Add an assertion in `AutobuilderService.handleGoalExhausted` that checks the root status and logs a warn-level message if it sees a COMPLETED root reaching this handler — this catches future regressions of the same shape.
  - **Changes:** `CycleExecutor.java` — root-status check in `deriveCycleResult` (~5 lines). `AutobuilderService.java` — defensive assertion (~3 lines). Tests: `deriveCycleResult_completedRootWithNullSubgoal_returnsGoalFulfilled`, `deriveCycleResult_givenUpRootWithNullSubgoal_returnsGoalExhausted`, `deriveCycleResult_activeRootWithNullSubgoal_returnsGoalExhausted`.
  - **Depends on:** None hard. T1-CB and T1-CJ already protect the goal-state mutation side; T1-DJ closes the parallel gap on the result-derivation side.
  - **Observed payoff:** Run 17:34 cycle 11 would have emitted `GOAL_FULFILLED` instead of `GOAL_EXHAUSTED`, producing the correct terminal narrative for a genuinely-successful run.
  - **Confirmed again (2026-05-01 run 00:28):** C14 terminated as `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` despite T1-DH having marked the root COMPLETED via `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` just before emitting the signal. T1-DJ is the missing final piece of the DG+DH+DJ triad.

- [x] **T1-DI: Meta-step accepted mutation silently discarded — MergeAgent `can_execute_false` after meta-step evaluation** (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-01 run 00:28, cycles C6 and C8):** Two mutations were negotiated and accepted (C6 score=0.753, DeterministicScoreHeuristic; C8 score=0.839, DeterministicScoreHeuristic) but never merged (`cycle_end merged=false`). In both cases the mutation pipeline (evolution → evaluation → negotiation) ran as meta-steps before the fitness-ranking loop because the deliberation's `agentSteps` included EVOLUTION+EVALUATION. After the meta-steps returned, `MergeAgent` was `can_execute_false` in the fitness-ranked phase.
  - **Root cause:** Meta-step execution drives each agent against a sub-context chain. `EvaluationAgent` writes `mutationState=ACCEPTED` and emits `ROLE_MERGE` into that chain — but the post-meta-step context is not threaded back as the entry context for the fitness-ranking loop. The fitness-ranking loop therefore opens with the pre-meta-step context (no accepted mutation, no `ROLE_MERGE` signal), so `MergeAgent.canExecute()` returns false.
  - **Fix:** In `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, after the meta-step execution loop completes, use the returned context as the starting context for the fitness-ranking phase. If propagation is already supposed to happen, trace why `ROLE_MERGE` is absent at the fitness-ranking `canExecute` check.
  - **Changes:** `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.java` — thread the post-meta-step context into the fitness-ranking loop entry (~2–5 lines). Tests: meta-step evolution+evaluation produces ACCEPTED → MergeAgent eligible in subsequent fitness-ranking pass; `cycle_end merged=true`.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DJ (commit the DG/DH/DJ triad first so context wiring is stable).

- [x] **T1-DK: Evaluation ranked `can_execute_false` before evolution runs in the same fitness-ranked cycle** (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-01 run 00:28, cycles C7 and C13):** Evolution was selected in the fitness-ranked phase and proposed a mutation. Evaluation had already been ranked `can_execute_false` in the same pass (no pending mutation at ranking time). The mutation was evaluated one cycle later in the next cycle's pre-ranking meta-step phase, sometimes after the subgoal had already advanced, causing boundary mismatches.
  - **Root cause:** All agent eligibility checks run at ranking time before any agents execute. Evolution and evaluation are ranked together; at ranking time no mutation exists, so `EvaluationAgent.canExecute()` returns false. After evolution runs there is no second-chance check in the same cycle.
  - **Fix:** In `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, after the ranked-agent execution loop completes, run a targeted second-pass: if evolution ran this cycle and `mutationState` is PROPOSED/PENDING, re-check evaluation eligibility and run it if eligible.
  - **Changes:** `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.java` — post-loop second-pass evaluation gate (~8–10 lines). Tests: cycle with evolution selected in fitness-ranked phase → evaluation runs in same cycle; mutation scored without waiting for next cycle.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DI (context-propagation fix should land first so the evaluation result is visible to merge in the same cycle).

- [x] **T1-DL: Deliberation LLM call wasted when `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` is already in context** (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-01 run 00:28, cycle C14):** T1-DH injected `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` into the context before the cycle began. `DeliberationService` was still called (~2 s) and returned PAUSE with 4 agent steps asking to plan and implement the Books resource. The steps were never executed.
  - **Fix:** In `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.runCycle()`, short-circuit deliberation if `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` or `SYSTEM_PAUSED` is already present in the context — return a synthetic PAUSE result without calling the LLM.
  - **Changes:** `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.java` — 3-line guard before the deliberation call. Tests: context with `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` → deliberation not invoked, cycle ends `GOAL_EXHAUSTED`.
  - **Depends on:** None.

- [x] **T1-DM: Deterministic `BUILD_FIX` subgoal injection on post-merge `COMPILE_ERROR`** (`MergeAgent`, `GoalPlannerService`; later extracted via T5-K to `core/blocker/BlockerSubgoalInjector`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-03):** With T4-AN-1 enabled, post-merge `COMPILE_ERROR` produced a `BuildResultHeuristic` CRITICAL REJECTED opinion but the goal tree was unchanged. The planner's count-based replan guards left the active feature subgoal in place; the next cycle's evolution kept proposing feature-shaped diffs while the build stayed red. The system needed a deterministic, planner-bypassing way to make build-fix the current work after a broken merge.
  - **Fix:** `MergeAgent` calls `injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent(BuildOutcome)` after the post-merge build executor returns `COMPILE_ERROR`. The helper:
    1. Checks `hasActiveBuildFixSubgoal()` — scans `getCurrentSubGoal()` + `getRemainingGoals()` for any ACTIVE goal already tagged `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX`. If present, returns (idempotent — no double-injection).
    2. Builds a description `"Fix compile error: " + summariseStderr(stderrTail)`. `summariseStderr` strips ANSI, prefers the first informative `[ERROR]`-prefixed line, skips bare `[ERROR]` framing and `-> [Help N]` continuations, truncates to 200 chars.
    3. Calls `goalPlannerService.addSubGoal(newId, description, /*setAsCurrent=*/true)` so the next cycle works on the build-fix immediately rather than continuing on whatever stuck subgoal the LLM was looping on.
    4. Tags `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX` (constant later extracted to `BlockerTypes` under T1-DN) so the idempotency check finds the goal on subsequent cycles.
    5. Sets two default acceptance criteria — `"Project compiles without errors"` and `"Compile error reported in the previous build is resolved"` — replaced by `BuildErrorClassifier`-driven criteria under T2-CT.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** today the system can keep advancing feature work while the build is concretely broken. Bypassing the planner is the only reliable way to make build-fix the current work in the same cycle.
  - **Acceptance:** post-merge `COMPILE_ERROR` produces exactly one new ACTIVE subgoal tagged `[BUILD_FIX]`, set as current; subsequent cycles' COMPILE_ERROR results are no-ops (idempotency); a subsequent COMPILE_OK clears the BUILD_FIX subgoals (T1-DR).
  - **Depends on:** T4-AN-1 (post-merge compile gate provides `BuildOutcome`).
  - **Pairs with:** T1-DN (NoDiff trust gate refuses `EVOLUTION_NOOP → FULFILLED` shortcut on the new BUILD_FIX subgoal), T2-CT (classifier produces error-specific acceptance criteria), T5-K (extraction of `BlockerSubgoalInjector` once a second caller surfaces).
  - **History:** filed and landed 2026-05-03 but bundled into commit `7d11723` (T1-DN/T2-CT) without its own roadmap row. Entry reconstructed retroactively 2026-05-07.

- [x] **T1-DN: NoDiff trust gate — refuse `EVOLUTION_NOOP → FULFILLED` shortcut when concrete state contradicts the LLM** (`EvolutionAgent`, `AutobuilderService`, new `core/build/BlockerTypes`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04 01:10 run, behaviour-2026-05-04_01-10-10.ndjson):** After cycle 1 merged 27 Java files but no `pom.xml`, `MergeAgent.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` injected a `BUILD_FIX` subgoal (`goal-7e2f88dc…`). On cycle 2 the LLM responded `#NoDiff` — its rationale captured at autobuilder.log:1729 hallucinates "the required dependencies for Spring Boot Web and Validation are present in the pom.xml" — and `AutobuilderService.runCycle` (lines 406–425) called `applyGoalProgress(FULFILLED)` unconditionally because `EVOLUTION_NOOP` was set. The build was never fixed; the BUILD_FIX subgoal was bypassed; subsequent feature subgoals (`GET /books`, `POST /members`, …) repeated the same pattern. Final log shows 18 `mutation_rejected` events, every reason citing "missing POM file".
  - **Root cause — two trust leaks compounding:**
    1. **Inner leak (`EvolutionAgent.runEvolution`, line 121):** the `noOp` branch trusts `lastEvalStatus == null` as the cross-subgoal deferred-merge case. `BUILD_FIX` subgoals are *always* in this state on first encounter (freshly injected, never evaluated) and so always take the trust path even though their existence proves the build is broken.
    2. **Outer leak (`AutobuilderService.runCycle`, line 406):** `EVOLUTION_NOOP` triggers `applyGoalProgress(FULFILLED)` with no consultation of `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` (which would show unmet criteria) or `buildOutcome.failed()` (which would show the codebase is broken).
  - **Fix — inner gate (`EvolutionAgent`):** in `runEvolution` immediately after the `subGoalId` lookup, before the existing `lastEvalStatus` branch at line 126, add: if the active subgoal has `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX` and is ACTIVE, force divergence — emit `REPETITION_DETECTED(decay=0.05)` + `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST(decay=0.1)` with source `noDiff-on-blocker`, return without `EVOLUTION_NOOP`. Requires injecting `GoalPlannerService` into `EvolutionAgent` (currently absent — verified). New helper `isActiveBuildFixSubgoal(String)` consults `getGoalById` + status + metadata key.
  - **Fix — outer gate (`AutobuilderService`):** introduce `whyRefuseNoDiffPromotion(CycleContextData)` returning `null` when promotion is allowed, or a short reason string when refused. Refuse if `buildOutcome != null && buildOutcome.failed()` or `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` contains any `Boolean.FALSE`. On refusal, emit `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST` with source `noDiff-trust-gate:<reason>` and skip the FULFILLED promotion; otherwise fall through to today's promotion path.
  - **Fix — extract constants:** the `metadata[blockerType]` key and `BUILD_FIX` value, currently package-private at `MergeAgent:64,66`, become two-callers under T1-DN. Promote to a new `core/build/BlockerTypes.java` (`public static final String METADATA_KEY = "blockerType"; public static final String BUILD_FIX = "BUILD_FIX";`). Update both call sites. This is the first deliverable of T5-K (deferred extraction of `BlockerSubgoalInjector`).
  - **Fix — T3-BR cap carve-out (`EvaluationAgent`):** `MergeAgent.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` sets two acceptance criteria on every BUILD_FIX subgoal ("Project compiles without errors", "Compile error resolved"). These criteria are never banked while the build is red, so T3-BR's `criteriaIncomplete` branch fires on every fingerprint-repeat and REPLAN_REQUESTED would be emitted at the cap of 3. Wrap the REPLAN emission at `EvaluationAgent:131-132` with `!BlockerTypes.BUILD_FIX.equals(goalPlannerService.getGoalById(currentSubGoalId).getMetadata().get(BlockerTypes.METADATA_KEY))` — skip REPLAN for BUILD_FIX, log a warning, and fall through to stagnation-only. Requires `BlockerTypes` (extracted above). Add one test case to `EvaluationAgentTest`: `evaluate_T3BR_buildFixSubgoal_atRetryCap_doesNotEmitReplanRequested`.
  - **Tests:** `EvolutionAgentTest` — new `noDiff_onActiveBuildFixSubgoal_T1DN_forcesDivergence_andDoesNotEmitEvolutionNoOp` slotted next to the existing `noDiff_*` cases at `:283–384`. Existing `noDiff_*` tests adjust to ensure `goalPlannerService.getGoalById(...)` returns null (default Mockito) so the new guard doesn't incidentally fire. `AutobuilderServiceTest` — extend the T2-CC suite at `:456–495` with four new cases: `runCycle_noOpWithFailingBuild_T1DN_refusesPromotion_forcesDivergence`, `runCycle_noOpWithUnmetCriteriaPassStatus_T1DN_refusesPromotion`, `runCycle_noOpWithCleanBuildAndAllCriteriaPassing_promotesAsBefore` (regression), `runCycle_noOpDeferredMergeCase_nullCriteriaPassStatus_promotesAsBefore` (regression of original T2-CC purpose). Existing `runCycle_evolutionNoop_promotesSubgoalToFulfilledViaT2CC` adds `.buildOutcome(null)` to its setup so the new gate passes through.
  - **Edge cases:** `buildOutcome == null` (build disabled, never run) → refusal-on-build returns false; only criteriaPassStatus blocks promotion. `buildOutcome=COMPILE_OK` + criteriaPassStatus has FALSE → refused (correct: code compiles but evaluator says criterion unmet). Inner gate fires before graph lookup, outer gate is defence-in-depth for any future `EVOLUTION_NOOP` emitter.
  - **Risks:** Adding `GoalPlannerService` to `EvolutionAgent` widens its surface; one read, narrow contract, acceptable. Slightly tightens T2-CC — the legitimate deferred-merge case has `buildOutcome` null/OK and `criteriaPassStatus` empty/null, which both checks pass through unchanged.
  - **Acceptance:** behaviour log shows `mutation_rejected reason="noDiff-on-blocker"` on first `#NoDiff` against an active BUILD_FIX subgoal; subgoal stays ACTIVE; no `subgoal_completed` event for any BUILD_FIX subgoal until a subsequent cycle's post-merge build returns `COMPILE_OK`.
  - **Depends on:** T2-CT (test fixtures only — runtime is independent). Pairs with T4-AN-3 to form the minimum viable subset that closes the deadlock.

- [x] **T1-DO: Tighten `hasOutputEvidenceForSubgoal` beyond substring scan** (`EvolutionAgent`, `EvolutionProtocolContract`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04):** T1-CS (line 491, done 2026-04-20) added `hasOutputEvidenceForSubgoal` as a guard against trusting `#NoDiff` when no on-disk evidence exists. The implementation tokenises descriptions/criteria on `[^a-zA-Z0-9]+`, takes any token ≥4 chars (`EvolutionAgent:307–321`), and substring-matches against output content. For BUILD_FIX criteria like *"Project compiles without errors"* the term set becomes `{compiles, without, errors, project, build, previous, resolved, reported}` — generic English words that match almost any Java code (e.g. `errors` appears as a variable name in `GlobalExceptionHandler.java`). The guard returns true and the LLM's `#NoDiff` is trusted. T1-DN closes the BUILD_FIX-specific damage; T1-DO removes the underlying weakness so future blocker types can't repeat the trick.
  - **Fix:** Replace the inline tokenizer with `EvolutionProtocolContract.extractSurfaceScanTerms` (private at `:616`, promote to `public`). That extractor already filters `SURFACE_SCAN_STOP_WORDS`, prefers HTTP paths and camelCase identifiers, and excludes `COMMON_CRITERIA_TOKENS`. Apply to both description and criteria. Critically: **flip the empty-set behaviour from fail-open to fail-closed** — current line 322 returns `true` when terms can't be extracted ("be conservative — allow noOp"); after T1-DO, return `false` ("no anchor — refuse to validate the LLM's claim"). Generic descriptions that produce no anchor terms (BUILD_FIX-style) trigger the divergence path naturally.
  - **Optional follow-up in same ticket:** extend `extractSurfaceScanTerms` to also pick up PascalCase identifiers (excluding `COMMON_CRITERIA_TOKENS`). Currently it matches `\b[a-z][a-zA-Z]{4,}\b` (camelCase) and HTTP paths only. Subgoals like *"Implement DemoApplication startup"* would otherwise be empty-term and over-trigger fail-closed. One extra regex + filter; tested via the same fixtures.
  - **Tests:** new `noDiff_T1DO_stopWordOnlyDescription_hasNoEvidence_doesNotEmitNoOp` (uses the actual BUILD_FIX description and the failing run's Java files in `previousOutput`; asserts `EVOLUTION_NOOP` not emitted, `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST` is) and `noDiff_T1DO_featureNamedSubgoal_findsEvidence_emitsNoOp` (uses *"Implement POST /books endpoint"* with `/books` references in output; asserts `EVOLUTION_NOOP` is emitted). Audit existing T1-CS tests at `EvolutionAgentTest:283–384` — fixtures must contain camelCase or HTTP-path terms that the new extractor finds; adjust only if a regression appears.
