  - **Edge cases:** stop-word-only subgoal → empty terms → fail closed → divergence (catches the failing run). PascalCase-only subgoal (without follow-up) → false-negative risk = one extra divergence cycle; recoverable. Empty `previousOutput` → early return false unchanged. Path is reached only when `lastEvalStatus == null`; real prior evaluations take the graph-based path and don't consult `hasOutputEvidence`.
  - **Risks:** fail-closed flip can over-trigger `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST` for legitimate generic descriptions. Mitigated by feature-named subgoals being the norm; worst case is one extra divergence cycle, not a deadlock.
  - **Acceptance:** the BUILD_FIX `#NoDiff` cycle would emit `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST(source="T1-CS-no-evidence")` even without T1-DN's blocker-specific guard. Existing T1-CS tests still green.
  - **Depends on:** None at runtime; lands cleaner after T1-DN because both touch `EvolutionAgent.runEvolution`.

- [x] **T1-DP: BUILD_FIX recovery resilient to planner subgoal restructuring** (`BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`, `FileVariantCollector`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04):** After T1-DN/T2-CT shipped, a follow-on run still failed to merge a valid `pom.xml` fix. Trace: cycle N injects `BUILD_FIX` subgoal `goal-OLD`; evolution proposes `pom.xml`; evaluator REJECTS (LLM cannot run the build); on cycle N+1 the planner's `planInto` restructures the goal tree — the `BUILD_FIX` subgoal is replaced by a plain `goal-NEW` ("Add pom.xml at project root"), no `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX`. Two follow-on filters then block the recovery:
    1. `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` keyed on `goal.metadata[blockerType]==BUILD_FIX` → abstains for `goal-NEW` → no CRITICAL ACCEPTED override → mutation stays REJECTED.
    2. `FileVariantCollector.collect` filtered cycle variants whose `meta.json:subGoalId != activeSubGoalId` → the `pom.xml` variant produced under `goal-OLD` was never offered as a candidate under `goal-NEW`, even when subsequent evaluation accepted a fresh `pom.xml`.
  - **Root cause — wrong key:** Both filters used the *subgoal identity* as a proxy for "is this a build-config concern". That proxy breaks under any planner reshuffle: the subgoal is metadata about *intent*; the build state and the file-path are the *concrete* facts. Build-config files (`pom.xml`, `build.gradle*`, `settings.gradle*`) are project-wide artefacts that don't belong to any one subgoal — the merge scope must follow the file, not the goal.
  - **Fix — heuristic gating (`BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`):** Replace `isActiveBuildFix(subGoalId)` with `buildOutcome != null && buildOutcome.result() == BuildResult.COMPILE_ERROR`. Drops the `GoalPlannerService` dependency entirely (was only used to read `metadata[blockerType]`). The heuristic now fires whenever (a) the carried `BuildOutcome` shows `COMPILE_ERROR`, and (b) the mutation touches a build-config file — independent of how the planner has shaped the goal tree. If the proposed fix is wrong, the post-merge `BuildResultHeuristic` (T4-AN-1) catches it next cycle and re-injects a `BUILD_FIX` subgoal.
  - **Fix — variant scope (`FileVariantCollector`):** Add `BUILD_CONFIG_FILES` set + `isBuildConfigFile(String)` helper. In both subgoal-id filter sites (single-path collection at `:60–67` and `allMutationPaths` aggregation at `:104–115`), exempt build-config paths from the `subGoalId` mismatch skip. All other files retain subgoal-scoped collection. Comment in code explains the project-wide rationale.
  - **Tests:** `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristicTest` rewritten — drops `@Mock GoalPlannerService` + `@ExtendWith(MockitoExtension.class)`; happy paths now feed `BuildOutcome(COMPILE_ERROR, …)` via `ctxWith(build, evolution)`; new case `evaluate_compileError_plannerCreatedSubgoalWithNoBuildFixMetadata_stillAccepts` proves the resilience scenario; abstention guards reframed (`evaluate_noBuildOutcome_abstains`, `evaluate_buildCompileOk_abstains`); legacy subgoal-state cases removed. `FileVariantCollectorTest` — `collect_skipsVariantBelongingToDifferentSubgoal` renamed to `collect_includesBuildConfigVariantRegardlessOfSubgoal` (asserts `pom.xml` from `goal-OLD` is returned under `goal-ACTIVE`); new `collect_skipsJavaFileVariantBelongingToDifferentSubgoal` proves Java sources still respect the scope.
  - **Edge cases:** Build disabled (`autobuilder.build.enabled=false`) → `BuildOutcome` null → heuristic abstains (correct: no ground truth to gate on). `pom.xml` proposed while `COMPILE_OK` → heuristic abstains (correct: build is fine, defer to LLM). Concurrent variants of the same `pom.xml` from different `subGoalId` cycles → both included; ordinary variant ranking selects the most recent ACCEPTED one. The build-config exemption is a one-way relaxation — no prior gate becomes stricter.
  - **Risks:** A genuinely irrelevant `pom.xml` mutation could now bypass the heuristic on any COMPILE_ERROR cycle, but the post-merge build gate is the authoritative result and will REJECT next cycle if the fix was wrong. The variant-collector exemption could surface a stale `pom.xml` from an old cycle as a merge candidate — acceptable: `MergeAgent` already handles variant overlap by preferring the latest accepted.
  - **Acceptance:** When the planner replaces a `BUILD_FIX` subgoal with a plain "Add pom.xml" subgoal, the `pom.xml` mutation under the original cycle is mergeable under the new active subgoal; `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` overrides to ACCEPTED purely on `BuildOutcome=COMPILE_ERROR` + build-config file touch. Existing 872+ tests stay green.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DN (constants), T4-AN-1 (`BuildOutcome` carry-over). Lands as a defensive complement to both.

- [x] **T1-DQ: `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` runaway loop — no consecutive-failure dampener** (`BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04 12:10 run):** 10 of 12 merges touched `pom.xml`; 6 were pom.xml-only. Every merge was force-accepted by `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` despite the LLM correctly scoring each at 0.25 REJECTED. The post-merge build kept returning COMPILE_ERROR ("release version 21 not supported" — caused by `maven-compiler-plugin:3.1`, the Maven-bundled default when no parent POM is used, not recognising the `<release>` element; JDK was correctly set to 21 via seed). The COMPILE_ERROR propagated to the next cycle via `ContextLineageTracker`, triggering the heuristic again unconditionally.
  - **Root cause:** `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` has no memory of prior failed attempts. Each cycle it sees `COMPILE_ERROR` in context + a build-config file in the mutation → always overrides to ACCEPTED. The design intention ("let the post-merge gate be authoritative") is sound for one or two attempts, but there is no exit condition when the mutation repeatedly fails to fix the build. The result is an infinite loop that only terminates when the planner gives up the BUILD_FIX subgoal or the pom.xml mutation happens to work.
  - **Fix:** Add a `int consecutiveBuildFixAttempts` counter to `CycleContextData` (carried via `ContextLineageTracker`, reset to 0 on `COMPILE_OK` or subgoal change). In `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic.evaluate()`, before overriding, check `consecutiveBuildFixAttempts < MAX_BUILD_FIX_ATTEMPTS` (suggest 3). Increment the counter in `MergeAgent` when a build-config mutation is accepted under COMPILE_ERROR context. Above the threshold, the heuristic abstains — normal LLM/heuristic evaluation decides, and the planner will eventually GIVEN_UP the subgoal.
  - **Note:** The root maven-compiler-plugin version issue (LLM generates `<release>21</release>` but the default bundled plugin is 3.1 which doesn't support it) is an evolution quality problem — the LLM should generate a pom.xml with explicit `<plugin><version>3.13.0</version></plugin>` or use `<maven.compiler.source>` properties. T1-DQ does not fix that, but stops the infinite retry loop.
  - **Changes:** `CycleContextData.java` (add counter field + `withIncrementedBuildFixAttempts()` and `withResetBuildFixAttempts()` methods), `ContextLineageTracker.java` (carry counter), `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic.java` (threshold guard), `MergeAgent.java` (increment on build-config acceptance under COMPILE_ERROR).

- [x] **T1-DR: Stale BUILD_FIX subgoal remains ACTIVE after build is fixed** (`MergeAgent`, `GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04 12:10 run):** `goal-28da8913` ("Fix compile error: Failed to execute goal org.apache.maven.plugins:maven-compiler-plugin...") remained ACTIVE in the goal tree at session end even though the post-merge build later returned COMPILE_OK. In the next session, the system will attempt to work on this stale subgoal, potentially generating new pom.xml mutations for a problem that no longer exists.
  - **Root cause:** `MergeAgent.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent()` creates BUILD_FIX subgoals when the build fails, but there is no symmetric cleanup when the build passes. A BUILD_FIX subgoal is only removed by (a) the planner explicitly calling `#SubGoalRemove`, or (b) the subgoal exhausting via normal exhaustion counting. Neither happens automatically on a COMPILE_OK result.
  - **Fix:** In `MergeAgent`, in the post-merge build result handling block (the `if (buildOutcome.result() == BuildResult.COMPILE_ERROR)` branch), add a symmetric `else if (buildOutcome.result() == BuildResult.COMPILE_OK)` branch that calls a new `clearActiveBuildFixSubgoals()` helper. The helper scans current + remaining goals for any ACTIVE goal with `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX` and marks them GIVEN_UP (with a log message explaining the cleanup). If `currentSubGoalId` points to a BUILD_FIX goal, advance the pointer to the next ACTIVE subgoal.
  - **Changes:** `MergeAgent.java` (~20 lines: add COMPILE_OK branch + `clearActiveBuildFixSubgoals()` method).

- [x] **T1-DS: `target/` directory in `output/` not excluded from file readers** (`FileService`, `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer`, `CycleContextCoordinator`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04 12:10 run):** After the first post-merge `mvn compile`, Maven created `output/target/` containing compiled `.class` files, `maven-status/`, META-INF, etc. None of the callers that walk the `output/` directory filter this subdirectory. Specific impact:
    1. `CycleContextCoordinator.readFolderContentsResolved(outputPath)` → `currentOutput` map includes `.class` binary content (read as garbled text) flowing into the evolution prompt as "current output files". Bloats context and confuses the LLM.
    2. `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer.collectNonJavaFilesByType()` lists ALL non-Java files including `.class`, `*.MF`, maven metadata in the "Non-Java project files" prompt section — LLM sees dozens of build artefacts as if they were project files.
    3. `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer.summarizeProjectStructure()` (Java file walk) would include `.java` files from `target/generated-sources/` if present.
  - **Note:** `HeuristicScoringEngine` already has a manual `path.contains("/target/")` guard (line 104) — an ad-hoc fix for the same class of problem. `DiffConfigProperties.excludeFolders` is configured as `["logs", "test", "responses"]`; `target` is absent.
  - **Fix A (config — minimal):** Add `output/target` to `autobuilder.diff.exclude-folders` in `application.yml`. This covers `DiffService` but not `FileService.listFiles()` or `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer`.
  - **Fix B (code — complete):** Add a `target/` filter in `FileService.listFiles()` — filter out any path component named `target`. Or, more precisely, filter in `CycleContextCoordinator` before building `currentOutput`, and in `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer.collectNonJavaFilesByType()` and `summarizeProjectStructure()`. Consider making the exclusion list configurable (add `exclude-folders` support to `FileService` itself) so the same config governs all walkers.
  - **Risk:** Low. `target/` contains only derived build artefacts — nothing the LLM should ever see or mutate.
  - **Changes:** `application.yml` (add `target` to exclude-folders), `FileService.java` or `CycleContextCoordinator.java` and `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer.java` (filter `target/` in directory walks).

- [x] **T1-DT: Hard build-block gate on feature subgoals while COMPILE_ERROR is carried** (new `core/build/BuildBlockGate` or `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `MergeAgent`/`BlockerSubgoalInjector`)
  - **Problem:** `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` caps at 3 consecutive attempts (T1-DQ) and then **abstains** — control returns to the normal LLM/heuristic path. With `BuildOutcome.COMPILE_ERROR` still carried forward via `ContextLineageTracker`, an active feature subgoal (e.g. after a planner restructure that drops `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX` per T1-DP) can drive a feature evolution. Pre-merge evaluation has no ground truth (LLM cannot run the build), so a feature mutation may merge cleanly on top of broken code; only the post-merge `BuildResultHeuristic` would catch it — and only if the new mutation itself made the build worse, not if it just left an existing breakage in place. Compile correctness must be a **hard gate**: while build is red, the only eligible work is build-fix work.
  - **Fix:** Introduce a `BuildBlockGate` consulted from `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer.canExecute(role, ctx)`. When `ctx.buildOutcome() != null && ctx.buildOutcome().result() == COMPILE_ERROR && autobuilder.build.enabled=true`:
    1. **Eligible roles narrow** to `analysis` plus (`planner` only when no BUILD_FIX subgoal is active — to allow injection) plus (`evolution`/`merge` only when `currentSubGoalId` carries `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX`).
    2. **If no BUILD_FIX subgoal is active**, force `BlockerSubgoalInjector.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` (extracted via T5-K) before role evaluation — sidesteps the planner entirely. Closes the post-restructure window where T1-DP's heuristic-level recovery is the only line of defence.
  - **Why T1 not T2:** today the system can advance feature work while the build is concretely broken. That's an observable correctness defect (wrong code merged on top of broken code), not a steering nuance.
  - **Acceptance:** Behaviour log shows `agent_skipped reason="build_blocked"` for evolution on a non-BUILD_FIX subgoal while COMPILE_ERROR is active; no feature merges land while build is red; when no BUILD_FIX subgoal exists, one is injected and becomes current within the same cycle.
  - **Depends on:** T5-K (extract `BlockerSubgoalInjector` from `MergeAgent`) — caller surface grows from one to two, which was the documented threshold for extraction.
  - **Pairs with:** T1-DU (fitness backstop), T2-CU (planner BUILD_FIRST rule).

- [x] **T1-DU: Fitness-ranker priority surge under COMPILE_ERROR — `BUILD_BROKEN` signal** (`BasicSignals`, `ContextLineageTracker`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `MergeAgent`)
  - **Problem:** Even with T1-DT's hard gate, the fitness ranker has no native notion of "build is broken — this is the highest-priority work." Ranking depends on `ROLE_*` signals + base priority + jitter; a stale ROLE_PLANNER signal can win against ROLE_EVOLUTION on a BUILD_FIX subgoal in marginal cases.
  - **Fix:** Define `BasicSignals.BUILD_BROKEN`. Emit from `ContextLineageTracker.deriveContextFromPrevious` whenever the carried-forward `BuildOutcome.result() == COMPILE_ERROR`, with `decayPerCycle=0.0` (only cleared, never decays) and `strength=1.0`. Clear from `MergeAgent` post-build when `BuildResult.COMPILE_OK` is observed (alongside the existing T1-DR cleanup). In `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.fitnessFor`, add `BUILD_BROKEN` to the supported-signal set for `evolution`/`merge` and as a depression for `planner`/`evaluation`.
  - **Why backstop and not primary:** T1-DT is the principled fix — it forecloses ineligible roles entirely. T1-DU just ensures that within the eligible set, build-fix work outranks anything else.
  - **Acceptance:** With `BUILD_BROKEN` active, `behaviourLogger.agentFitnessRanked` shows evolution/merge ranked above planner/evaluation in fitness order.
  - **Depends on:** Independent. Pairs with T1-DT.

- [x] **T1-DW: Filesystem rollback on post-merge COMPILE_ERROR** (`MergeAgent`, new `core/merge/MergeRollbackService`, `BuildResultHeuristic`)
  - **Problem:** T4-AN-1's docstring says CRITICAL REJECTED *"can roll back the merge or feed into next-cycle evolution context"* — but nothing actually rolls back. The merge has already happened on disk; the heuristic only affects scoring. Worse, when `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` force-accepts a wrong `pom.xml` (or a malformed build-config file), the broken file becomes the new on-disk starting point — each subsequent attempt has to reason about *that* breakage instead of the original problem. The 2026-05-04 12:10 run's 6 consecutive `pom.xml` force-accepts each left disk in a slightly different broken state.
  - **Fix:**
    1. **Snapshot before merge.** `MergeAgent` captures original content of every path it's about to write into a `MergeRollbackPoint` keyed by `cycleId + mergedPaths`. Stored under `meta/rollback/{cycleId}.json` (filesystem) plus held in `CycleContextData.lastRollbackPoint` for the current cycle.
    2. **Rollback condition.** Post-merge build returns `COMPILE_ERROR` AND the *previous* cycle's `BuildOutcome.result()` was `COMPILE_OK` (or `null` for first cycle). Don't roll back when "less broken → more broken in a different way" — restoring to a state we never had makes things worse, not better.
    3. **Restore mechanism.** Write original content back; remove paths that were newly-added. Emit `BehaviourEvent.MergeRolledBack { cycleId, restoredPaths }`.
    4. **Interaction with `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`.** Force-accepted build-config mutations are exempt from rollback (T1-DQ's cap and T2-CV's escalation are the right answer for those). Rollback only triggers for *non-build-config* mutations that broke compilation.
  - **Why T1 not T4:** silent corruption of disk state is a correctness defect, not new infrastructure. Without rollback, T1-DT's gate is meaningful only at the boundary; broken state still accumulates within build-fix loops.
  - **Why this is a Chain H prereq (not afterthought):** T1-DT prevents *future* feature merges on broken builds. T1-DW prevents *current* feature merges from leaving broken state. Both gates needed for "build correctness is invariant."
  - **Acceptance:** session where cycle N merges code that breaks compile rolls back at end of cycle N; cycle N+1 starts from the cycle-N-1 filesystem state; behaviour log shows `merge_rolled_back` event. Build-config mutations under `COMPILE_ERROR` context (force-accept path) do NOT trigger rollback.
  - **Risk:** rollback-then-injection of BUILD_FIX subgoal could oscillate if the original code was *also* slightly broken and the LLM flips between two equivalent-broken states. Mitigation: rollback condition requires `previousBuildOutcome.result() == COMPILE_OK`; if previous was already `COMPILE_ERROR`, no rollback (already in BUILD_FIX flow).
  - **Depends on:** T5-K (extracted blocker injector — pairs cleanly). Chain H reorder: T5-K → **T1-DW** → T1-DT → T2-CU → T1-DU.

- [x] **T1-DX: Rollback must unwind cycle state, and next cycle must see the rollback** (`MergeAgent`, `MergeRollbackService`, `CycleMergeTracker`, `CycleContextData`, `CycleExecutor`, `ContextLineageTracker`, `EvolutionProtocolContract`, `PlannerProtocolContract`, `BehaviourEvent`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 run, behaviour-2026-05-07_01-02-31.ndjson, autobuilder-2026-05-07_01-02-31.log):** Three consecutive cycles each followed the same path — propose mutation → score ACCEPTED (~0.82) → merge → post-merge `COMPILE_ERROR` (`no POM in this directory`) → T1-DW rolls back. After all three rollbacks, `firestarter/output/` contains only empty directory skeletons (no `pom.xml`, no Java files, no `application.properties`). Yet the goal tree advanced through Books → Members → Loans, and the final cycle ended `result=GOAL_FULFILLED, merged=true`. The operator-visible state says "goal complete"; disk state says "nothing happened."
  - **Two compounding gaps in T1-DW:**
    1. **State-unwind gap.** Rollback restores files and `buildOutcome` only. It does NOT clear `mutationState`, `merged`, `cycleResultState`, or unregister the cycle from the merge tracker. `CycleExecutor`'s score-based closure path runs after `MergeAgent` returns, sees `mutationState=ACCEPTED + merged=true + score≥threshold`, and calls `applyGoalProgress(FULFILLED)`. The subgoal advances on phantom progress.
    2. **Forward-blindness gap.** Restoring `buildOutcome` to the previous COMPILE_OK (or null for the first cycle) is correct for the *filesystem* invariant T1-DW protects, but it erases every signal that the previous attempt failed: `ContextLineageTracker` does not emit `BUILD_BROKEN`, `BuildBlockGate.isActive()` returns false, no `[BUILD_FIX]` subgoal exists, the planner's `--- BUILD RESULT ---` block is empty, and the evolver's prompt has no record that the last mutation broke the build. The LLM has no reason to vary its approach and proposes the same broken pattern next cycle. The 2026-05-07 run shows three identical roundtrips with no learning.
  - **Fix — Part A (state unwind in `MergeAgent` rollback branch):**
    1. After `mergeRollbackService.rollback(...)`, set `context = context.withMerged(false).withMutationState(null).withCycleResultState(null)`. Closure must not fire on a rolled-back cycle.
    2. Unregister the cycle from `CycleMergeTracker` so `markCyclesAsMerged` does NOT mark it merged downstream. Either skip the `markCyclesAsMerged` call entirely on the rollback path, or add `cycleMergeTracker.untrack(cycleId)` and call it before the existing post-rollback flow.
    3. Score-side cleanup: clear `score`, `lastScore` propagation for this cycle (or set a `rolledBackThisCycle` flag that `CycleExecutor.attemptSubgoalClosure` consults and skips).
    4. Add an explicit `rolledBackThisCycle` (or equivalent guard) on `CycleContextData` for use by `CycleExecutor.attemptSubgoalClosure` and any other downstream closure paths. Cheaper than chasing every flag the closure consults.
  - **Fix — Part B (forward signal — make rollback visible to the next cycle):**
    1. Add `Optional<RolledBackEvidence> lastRollback` to `CycleContextData` carrying `{ cycleId, restoredPaths, stderrTailSummary, kind (from BuildErrorClassifier) }`. `MergeAgent` populates it on the rollback branch.
    2. `ContextLineageTracker.enrichLineage` carries `lastRollback` forward exactly one cycle (it represents *what the prior cycle did*, not a persistent state). Cleared as soon as the next cycle's evolution ran or another merge happened.
    3. **Render in evolution prompt** (`EvolutionProtocolContract` or via `BuildResultPromptBlock` extended with an `Audience.EVOLUTION_AFTER_ROLLBACK` variant): a `--- LAST CYCLE ROLLED BACK ---` block listing the restored paths and the rollback's cause stderr summary. Closing directive: *"Your previous mutation was rolled back because it broke the build with: <summary>. Choose a structurally different approach this cycle — do not re-propose the same files in the same shape."*
    4. **Render in planner prompt** (analogous block): the planner sees that a rollback occurred so its replanning decision is informed; without this, planner proposes the same Members/Loans subgoal next cycle and the cycle repeats.
    5. Emit `BehaviourEvent.MergeRolledBack` already exists (T1-DW). Extend payload with `stderrSummary` and `errorKind` so post-hoc analysis doesn't have to cross-reference the build log.
  - **Why T1, not T2/T5:** today the system reports `GOAL_FULFILLED` while having shipped nothing. That's not a steering nuance or observability gap — it's an operator-deceiving correctness defect. The fix sits at the same correctness tier as T1-DW itself.
  - **Why this is a T1-DW follow-up, not a revision:** T1-DW's filesystem-level invariant (don't leave disk in a broken state) is correct and should not be reverted. T1-DX adds the cycle-state and forward-signal layers that T1-DW left implicit. Splitting keeps each ticket's blast radius small and lets T1-DW's regression tests stay focused on filesystem behaviour.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. In a session where cycle N rolls back: cycle N's `cycle_end` reports `merged=false, resultState=ROLLED_BACK` (or equivalent non-completion state), and the active subgoal is NOT promoted to FULFILLED.
    2. Cycle N+1's evolution prompt contains a `--- LAST CYCLE ROLLED BACK ---` block listing the restored paths.
    3. Cycle N+1's planner prompt (when planner runs) contains the same block.
    4. With Part A + Part B in place, replaying the 2026-05-07 seed (no `pom.xml` in bootstrap) produces at most one roundtrip before either (a) the LLM proposes a `pom.xml` or (b) the system surfaces the deadlock to the operator — not three silent rollbacks ending in `GOAL_FULFILLED`.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Crash between Part A's state unwind and the cycle-end log.** Without persistence, cycle N+1 boots with the in-memory state as-is — should be fine because the rollback already touched the filesystem; the worst case is one extra rollback report on restart.
    - **Rollback during a meta-step cycle.** Meta steps may have run agents in arbitrary order; the unwind must apply uniformly regardless of which path produced the COMPILE_ERROR.
    - **Back-to-back rollbacks (`lastRollback` carry-forward chain).** If cycle N+1 also rolls back, `lastRollback` from cycle N is overwritten by cycle N+1's. Track only the most recent; older rollbacks are recoverable from `behaviour-*.ndjson` if needed.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Subgoal closure regression.** Some closure paths might rely on `merged=true` for non-rollback reasons (e.g. test-only cycles); audit `CycleExecutor.attemptSubgoalClosure` and the score-based closure helper before flipping. Prefer the explicit `rolledBackThisCycle` guard over silently changing `merged`.
    - **Prompt bloat.** The new block adds ~5–10 lines per prompt for one cycle after each rollback; acceptable.
    - **Carry-forward over-extension.** If `lastRollback` survives more than one cycle, the prompt repeats stale rollback info and the LLM treats it as the present state. Strict one-cycle TTL is required (cleared by `ContextLineageTracker` after rendering).
  - **Depends on:** T1-DW (which is the layer this ticket extends). Independent of T1-DT/T1-DU/T2-CU — those gates only fire when `BuildOutcome.COMPILE_ERROR` is *carried* across cycles, which T1-DW deliberately prevents on the rollback path; T1-DX restores the cross-cycle visibility through `lastRollback` rather than through `buildOutcome`.
  - **Pairs with:** T3-BT (bootstrap-time build-system seeding — addresses the structural cause of the missing-POM rollback loop; T1-DX addresses the protocol-level damage when any first-cycle compile failure occurs).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after analysing the 2026-05-07 01:02 run. The same defects would surface for any first-cycle compile failure, not just missing-POM — T3-BT removes the most common trigger but does not subsume T1-DX.

- [x] **T1-DY: Defer persistent merge-state mutations until the build gate confirms the merge** (`MergeAgent`, `EvaluationAgent`, `GoalPlannerService`, `CycleExecutor`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 run, behaviour-2026-05-07_01-38-50.ndjson):** With T1-DX shipped, rolled-back cycles correctly report `result=ROLLED_BACK, merged=False`. But the run still ended with `GOAL_FULFILLED` after an empty `output/`. Pattern: 6 cycles alternated `rollback (3 agents) → 2-event early-exit cycle (zero agents)`. The early-exit cycles fire the **T3-BL "criteria-banked early exit"** path at `CycleExecutor:90-122`, which calls `applyGoalProgress(FULFILLED, "all acceptance criteria banked")` whenever both `allCriteriaDefinedAndBanked(subgoalId)` and `getGoalMetadataInt(subgoalId, "mergeCount") > 0` are true. Both guards report true on the cycle following a rollback because the rolled-back cycle's pre-build state mutations were never undone.
  - **Two compounding state-pollution sources surviving rollback:**
    1. **Merge-side state pollution (`MergeAgent`).** Lines 175-189 fire BEFORE the post-merge build runs:
       - `goalPlannerService.incrementGoalMergeCount(subgoalId)` — `mergeCount` increments to 1, never decremented on rollback. T3-BL's `mergeCount > 0` guard now reports true on a subgoal that has never actually merged anything.
       - `goalPlannerService.recordMergedFiles(subgoalId, paths)` — records paths into `GoalEntity.metadata[mergedFiles]` that no longer exist on disk after T1-DW restores them.
       - `behaviourLogger.mutationMerged(...)` — the behaviour trace says `mutation_merged` on a cycle that ends in `merge_rolled_back`, which is contradictory and breaks any downstream tooling that pairs the two events.
    2. **Evaluation-side state pollution (`EvaluationAgent`).** Lines 175-180 of `EvaluationAgent.runEvaluation` call `goalPlannerService.saveCriteriaPassStatus(subgoalId, cumulativeCriteriaStatus)` immediately after the LLM evaluator returns. Evaluation runs BEFORE merge in the cycle pipeline, so the evaluator's "criteria passed" assertions are persisted to `GoalEntity.metadata[criteriaPassStatus]` while the build outcome that would prove or refute them hasn't been observed yet. T1-DW's filesystem rollback and T1-DX's context unwind do nothing to this on-entity state. T3-BL then reads `allCriteriaDefinedAndBanked == true` from a banking that was based on code that was reverted.
  - **Fix — Layer 1 (deferred merge-side mutations in `MergeAgent`):**
    1. Move `incrementGoalMergeCount`, `recordMergedFiles`, and `behaviourLogger.mutationMerged` from their current pre-build position (`MergeAgent:175-189`) to a post-build code path that fires only when the rollback branch did NOT execute. Concretely: extract a `commitMergeState(context, mergedPaths, fileCount)` private method; call it from the post-build trailer when `!rolledBackThisCycle`.
    2. Set `lastAcceptedMutationFileCount` (also currently set in the trailer) at the same commit point so it's not propagated for rolled-back cycles either.
    3. Ordering invariant after this change: file write → build → either (a) build-OK → commit merge state, register graph node, mark cycle merged, emit `mutation_merged`; or (b) build-FAILED → rollback, evidence capture, emit `merge_rolled_back`, return rolled-back context. No persistent state changes happen between file-write and the build verdict.
  - **Fix — Layer 2 (criteria banking deferred until build confirms — `EvaluationAgent` + `GoalPlannerService`):**
    1. **Option A (chosen):** EvaluationAgent stops calling `saveCriteriaPassStatus` directly. Instead, it accumulates the criteria assessment on `CycleContextData.cumulativeCriteriaStatus` (already happens) and sets a new `pendingCriteriaPersist=true` flag. A post-merge confirmation step in `MergeAgent` (or a new `EvaluationConfirmationStep` invoked from `CycleExecutor` after the build verdict) flushes the pending status to `GoalEntity.metadata[criteriaPassStatus]` only when the build did NOT roll back.
    2. **Option B (alternative if A is too invasive):** Add `unbankCriteriaForCycle(subgoalId, cycleId)` to `GoalPlannerService`. The current `saveCriteriaPassStatus` call gains a `cycleId` parameter so banked criteria are tagged with which cycle persisted them; rollback calls `unbankCriteriaForCycle(currentSubGoalId, cycleId)` to remove only the criteria banked by the rolled-back cycle. Heavier in `GoalEntity.metadata` shape changes — adds a per-criterion source-cycle map.
    3. Pick A if `EvaluationAgent` is the only writer of `criteriaPassStatus`; pick B if other code paths (analysis-injected micro-subgoals, planner) also bank criteria and an unbank operation is the safer abstraction.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** the same operator-deceiving outcome as T1-DX — the system reports `GOAL_FULFILLED` while having shipped nothing. Pure correctness defect; no steering nuance.
  - **Why a separate ticket from T1-DX:** T1-DX intentionally limited its blast radius to the cycle-state context layer (`CycleContextData` flags + `CycleExecutor` closure helpers + filesystem unwind) so its regression surface stayed narrow. The persistent-state layer (`GoalEntity.metadata`) is a different ownership surface — both `MergeAgent` (mergeCount, mergedFiles) and `EvaluationAgent` (criteriaPassStatus) write to it, and the right fix touches both. Splitting keeps each ticket reviewable.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. Replaying the 2026-05-07 01:38 seed (no `pom.xml` in bootstrap) produces no `GOAL_FULFILLED` cycle. Every rolled-back subgoal stays ACTIVE.
    2. After a rolled-back cycle, `goalPlannerService.getGoalMetadataInt(subgoalId, "mergeCount")` returns 0 (not the count that would have been reached if the merge had stuck).
    3. After a rolled-back cycle, `goalPlannerService.allCriteriaDefinedAndBanked(subgoalId)` returns false (or, if other criteria were genuinely banked from a prior un-rolled-back cycle, those persist — only the rolled-back cycle's criteria banking is undone).
    4. Behaviour log no longer emits `mutation_merged` followed immediately by `merge_rolled_back` for the same `cycleId`. Either `mutation_merged` (success path) or `merge_rolled_back` (rollback path) — never both.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Build is disabled (`autobuilder.build.enabled=false`).** Today MergeAgent skips the build executor entirely, so the rollback branch never fires. The deferred-commit code must still run in this no-build path — treat "build disabled" as "build implicitly OK" and commit the merge state. Otherwise this fix breaks every test/dev-mode flow that runs without the build gate.
    - **Build is enabled but `successfullyMergedPaths.isEmpty()`.** No merge happened; today the SKIPPED action is registered at line 162-165. Commit-state code must NOT run in this branch — there is no merge to commit.
    - **Build returns COMPILE_ERROR but rollback condition declines (`prevWasOk=false` or build-config mutation).** T1-DM injects a BUILD_FIX subgoal, T1-DQ counts the attempt — these are existing behaviours and must remain. The merge DID persist on disk (no rollback), so commit-state code must run. The injected BUILD_FIX subgoal is a separate goal entity from the current subgoal — its mergeCount/criteria are tracked separately and not affected by this ticket.
    - **Layer 2 — analysis-injected criteria.** `AnalysisAgent` may add criteria to a subgoal mid-life (T3-BE). Those criteria are typically banked by future evaluations; pure-evaluation banking is the only writer affected by Option A. Verify before choosing.
    - **Layer 2 — graduation micro-subgoals.** T3-BK injects micro-subgoals when a subgoal is structurally stuck on unmet criteria. Those criteria are explicitly UNBANKED on the parent before injection — the existing un-bank operation in `GoalPlannerService.injectGraduationMicroSubgoals` is the natural template if Option B is chosen.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Test regression on commit-state ordering.** Tests that count `incrementGoalMergeCount` invocations, watch `mutationMerged` ordering, or assert `mergeCount` after a mocked compile failure may break. Audit `MergeAgentTest`, `MergeAgentIT`, `MergeAgentAdoptionIT`, `CycleExecutorTest` (T1-CU paths read `mergeCount`), and `GoalPlannerServiceTest`.
    - **Layer 2 invasiveness.** Option A requires a new persist-confirmation step that doesn't exist today. The cleanest place to put it is inside the same MergeAgent post-build trailer that commits the merge state — `commitMergeState` becomes `commitMergeAndEvaluationState`. Keeps the persist boundary in one place.
    - **Cross-feature with T2-CV (escalation).** When T2-CV's stage-1 escalation lands and force-accepts a build-config mutation under COMPILE_ERROR, the merge persists (no rollback) and the commit-state code runs. That's correct behaviour — the force-accept path explicitly wants `mergeCount` to advance so `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` can detect the runaway loop via T1-DQ's dampener.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DX (the cycle-state unwind layer; this ticket adds the persistent-state unwind layer on top). No external dependencies.
  - **Pairs with:** T3-BT (bootstrap-time build seeding removes the most common trigger of the rollback loop that exposes this defect), T2-CV (escalation path that intentionally persists state on the build-config-mutation branch — both paths must remain distinguishable).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 immediately after T1-DX shipped, after analysing the 2026-05-07 01:38 run which still ended `GOAL_FULFILLED` despite T1-DX clearing context-level state. The defect proves the closure surface is wider than the context — persistent goal-entity state is its own protocol layer that needs its own unwind discipline.

- [x] **T1-DZ: Wire rollback evidence into Chain H gates** (`BuildBlockGate`, `ContextLineageTracker`, `BlockerSubgoalInjector` call site)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 02:04 run, behaviour-2026-05-07_02-04-36.ndjson):** With T1-DW/T1-DX/T1-DY all shipped, 13 cycles produced 10 rollbacks and 0 false `GOAL_FULFILLED` (T1-DY's fix held). But the run was a perfect rollback loop: same `MISSING_POM` error every cycle, LLM proposed Java files (`Book.java`, `BookController.java`, `LoanController.java` …) cycle after cycle, never a `pom.xml`. **Zero log mentions of `BuildBlockGate`, `injectBuildFix`, or `BUILD_BROKEN`** across 50K+ log lines — the entire Chain H sat idle for the whole session.
  - **Root cause — design conflict between T1-DW and Chain H:**
    - T1-DW restores `buildOutcome` to `previousBuildOutcome` after rollback. For the first rollback, `previousBuildOutcome == null` → after rollback, `buildOutcome = null`. ContextLineageTracker carries it forward. Every subsequent rollback restores to the same null. The "carried COMPILE_ERROR" condition that drives every Chain H mechanism never holds.
    - Every Chain H trigger keys off `ctx.buildOutcome().result() == COMPILE_ERROR`:
      - `BuildBlockGate.isActive(ctx)` → false → `ensureBuildFixSubgoalExists` no-op → no BUILD_FIX subgoal injection
      - `ContextLineageTracker` BUILD_BROKEN emission requires the same → never fires
      - `BuildResultPromptBlock.render()` returns null → no `--- BUILD RESULT ---` block in any prompt
      - T2-CU's BUILD_FIRST planner directive only useful if planner sees the build state
    - T1-DX added `lastRollback` evidence as a one-cycle-TTL signal that DOES reach the prompts (rendered by `RolledBackPromptBlock` in evolution + planner prompts), but T1-DX deliberately scoped its fix to the prompt layer and did not wire `lastRollback` into the gates, the signal emitter, or the BUILD_FIX subgoal injector. The LLM gets a hint about the rollback in the prompt but it's drowned out by the active subgoal description (`"Implement Books resource"`), the META INTENTION block reinforcing that subgoal, and the goal tree containing zero `[BUILD_FIX]` subgoals.
  - **Fix — treat `lastRollback` as evidence the build is effectively broken:**
    1. **`BuildBlockGate`:** introduce `effectiveBuildOutcome(ctx)` returning either the current `BuildOutcome.COMPILE_ERROR` or a synthetic `BuildOutcome` constructed from `lastRollback` evidence (kind, stderrSummary, cycleId). `isActive(ctx)` returns true when `effectiveBuildOutcome != null`. `ensureBuildFixSubgoalExists(ctx)` passes the effective outcome to `BlockerSubgoalInjector.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent`. The injector's existing `BuildErrorClassifier.classify(stderr)` re-identifies the kind from the summary (verified: the MISSING_POM regex matches the standard `"no POM in this directory"` substring that lives in `RolledBackEvidence.stderrSummary`), producing the right BUILD_FIX subgoal description and acceptance criteria.
    2. **`ContextLineageTracker`:** BUILD_BROKEN signal emitted when `buildOutcome.COMPILE_ERROR` OR `lastRollback != null`. Cleared otherwise. Mirrors the gate's effective-outcome rule.
    3. **No changes to `BlockerSubgoalInjector`** — the synthetic BuildOutcome passed by the gate is structurally identical to a real one for the injector's purposes (description summary + classifier-driven criteria + metadata tagging).
    4. **No changes to `BuildResultPromptBlock`** — the `RolledBackPromptBlock` that T1-DX added already covers the prompt-side messaging. Once a BUILD_FIX subgoal exists the planner sees the `[BUILD_FIX]` tag in the goal tree (T2-CU's `formatGoalInfoList` change) and the BUILD_FIRST contract rule fires.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** rolled-back sessions today silently bypass all the safety gates Chain H was built to enforce. The same root-cause as T1-DT/T1-DU (build correctness not first-class); this just extends the trigger surface to include rollback evidence, which T1-DW deliberately uses to hide the failure from `buildOutcome`.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. Replaying the 2026-05-07 02:04 seed (no `pom.xml` in bootstrap) produces a BUILD_FIX subgoal by cycle 2 (rather than cycling on Books/Members/Loans subgoals indefinitely).
    2. With BUILD_BROKEN active from rollback evidence, `agent_fitness_ranked` shows evolution/merge ranked above planner/evaluation in the cycle following any rollback.
    3. The BUILD_FIX subgoal description names the actual missing artifact (`"Fix compile error: ...no POM in this directory..."`) — proves the classifier correctly re-identified the kind from the synthetic outcome.
    4. Behaviour log shows `agent_skipped reason="build_blocked"` for evolution on a non-BUILD_FIX subgoal in the cycle following a rollback.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Cycle that follows a rollback AND has a fresh COMPILE_ERROR.** `effectiveBuildOutcome` prefers the current `buildOutcome.COMPILE_ERROR` over `lastRollback` so the freshest evidence drives the gate.
    - **Cycle that follows a successful BUILD_FIX merge.** `lastRollback` is cleared by `ContextLineageTracker` (T1-DX's TTL: only carried when `previousContext.rolledBackThisCycle()`). `buildOutcome.COMPILE_OK` clears the BUILD_BROKEN signal already (T1-DR/T1-DU). Gate goes inactive. System resumes feature work.
    - **Synthetic BuildOutcome's `cycleId` field.** Use `lastRollback.cycleId()` so `BuildResultHeuristic.isFromCycle(currentCycleId)` correctly returns false (the rollback is from a previous cycle, not the current one) — prevents the heuristic from CRITICAL-REJECTING a fresh mutation on the basis of stale evidence.
    - **Idempotency.** `BlockerSubgoalInjector.hasActiveBuildFixSubgoal()` short-circuits when one is already present, so calling `ensureBuildFixSubgoalExists` every cycle while rolled-back evidence persists doesn't double-inject.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Synthetic BuildOutcome leaking into other consumers.** Other readers of `ctx.buildOutcome()` (e.g. `BuildResultHeuristic`) must not see the synthetic. Keep the synthesis strictly inside `BuildBlockGate`; do NOT write the synthetic onto the context. Confirm `BuildResultHeuristic` continues to read `ctx.buildOutcome()` directly (real outcome, possibly null) and is unaffected.
    - **Classifier mis-identification on summary text.** The classifier patterns are designed for full Maven stderr; they should still match against the single-line summary, but verify per-kind: MISSING_POM regex is substring-based (`"no POM in this directory"`) ✓; CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL needs `"symbol: class X"` text in the summary; PACKAGE_DOES_NOT_EXIST needs `"package X does not exist"`. Worst case is fall-through to UNKNOWN — generic criteria, still useful.
    - **Loop with the planner restructuring.** If the planner removes the auto-injected BUILD_FIX subgoal (against the BUILD_FIRST rule), the gate re-injects on the next cycle (idempotency check is structural, not history-based). T1-DP already covers the heuristic-level recovery — pairs cleanly.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DW (provides the rollback path), T1-DX (provides the `lastRollback` evidence and one-cycle TTL), T1-DT/T1-DU (the gate and signal this ticket newly triggers).
  - **Pairs with:** T3-BT (root-cause fix at bootstrap removes the most common trigger), T2-CU (planner already has the BUILD_FIRST rule and `[BUILD_FIX]` tag rendering — no changes needed).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after analysing the 2026-05-07 02:04 run. T1-DX intentionally limited its scope to the prompt layer; T1-DZ is the principled completion that wires the rollback signal into all four Chain H consumers (gate, signal, injector, planner-via-goal-tree). Two-step option: (1) tighten T1-DW's rollback condition so it doesn't fire when `previousBuildOutcome == null` (smaller change but loses T1-DW's first-cycle protection); (2) THIS — the principled wiring (broader change but keeps T1-DW intact).

- ~~**T1-DV** (re-tiered T1→T2, 2026-05-14; folded into T2-CV — all three escalation stages absorbed there; standalone ticket removed)~~

- [x] **T1-EA: Allow `evaluation` under an active BUILD_FIX subgoal** (`BuildBlockGate`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 20:16 run, autobuilder-2026-05-07_20-16-33.log):** Cycle 3 (cycle-1778178071034) — current subgoal was BUILD_FIX (`Fix compile error in pom.xml: ...`), evolution produced a correct `pom.xml` mutation, but the cycle ended `result=CONTINUE, merged=false` with **no evaluation, no merge, and no build run**. Cycle 4 then re-evaluated and merged the same mutation, build COMPILE_OK. The fix took 2 cycles instead of 1.
  - **Root cause:** `BuildBlockGate.isEligible` lines 80-88 — `case "evolution", "merge" -> isCurrentSubgoalBuildFix(ctx);` lets evolution and merge run when the active subgoal is BUILD_FIX, but `default -> false` blocks evaluation. With evaluation blocked, no mutation reaches `mutationState=ACCEPTED`, so merge has nothing to write, so no build runs, so the BUILD_FIX subgoal stays open. The cycle that *fixes* the build is the one that proves it via the build run — by blocking evaluation we forfeit that signal until the next cycle.
  - **Fix:** add `case "evaluation" -> isCurrentSubgoalBuildFix(ctx);` to `BuildBlockGate.isEligible`. Symmetric to evolution and merge: the gate's intent is "while build is red, only build-fix work proceeds" — and evaluating a BUILD_FIX-subgoal mutation IS build-fix work.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** silent half-cycle waste on every successful build repair. Each rollback chain pays one extra cycle of LLM time + scheduler latency to converge. Compounds with T2-CV's escalation thresholds — the staged-attempt counter advances faster than necessary because each "fix attempt" actually takes two cycles to complete.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. In a cycle where current subgoal carries `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX` and evolution produces an ACCEPTED-graded mutation, evaluation runs in the same cycle and merge consumes the result. Build runs in the same cycle. `merged=true` if successful.
    2. `BuildBlockGate.isEligible("evaluation", ctx)` returns true when current subgoal is BUILD_FIX, false otherwise (preserves the gate's blocking effect for non-BUILD_FIX evaluation under a red build).
    3. Existing T1-DT/T1-DU/T1-DZ tests continue to pass — evaluation eligibility on a non-BUILD_FIX active subgoal is unchanged.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Evaluation under a BUILD_FIX subgoal but the mutation isn't a build-config file.** Allowed — the evaluator scores it, build proves it. If the mutation broke things, T1-DW rolls back as usual.
    - **No active subgoal (currentSubGoalId null).** `isCurrentSubgoalBuildFix` returns false → evaluation blocked. Same as evolution/merge today. No change.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Evaluator wrongly accepting a non-fix mutation as ACCEPTED, then merge writes it.** The post-merge build gate (T1-DW) still rolls back if the mutation breaks compilation, so the worst case is the same one-cycle waste we have today, just with a build run added. Acceptable; the build run is the truth.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DT (defines the gate this ticket extends).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DB (skip-on-prior-rollback merge guard — together they remove both halves of the wasted-cycle pattern).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after the 2026-05-07 20:16 run analysis. Small ticket — ~1 line in `BuildBlockGate.java` + ~2 tests.

- [x] **T1-EB: Build-blocked deadlock breaker — emit `SYSTEM_PAUSED` after N consecutive zero-work cycles** (`AutobuilderService`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `CycleContextData`, `BasicSignals`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-08 20:02 run, behaviour-2026-05-08_20-02-30.ndjson, 91 cycles):** the last ~25 cycles ended `mut=null result=CONTINUE merged=false` with `signals={BUILD_BROKEN=3.00, ROLE_ANALYSIS=3.00}` and every other role gated by `canExecute=false`. The behaviour log's `META LEARNINGS` block in the final cycle confirms the deadlock at scale: `evaluation CAN_EXECUTE_FALSE 65×`, `evolution CAN_EXECUTE_FALSE 65×`, `planner CAN_EXECUTE_FALSE 41×`, `analysis CAN_EXECUTE_FALSE 21×`, `merge CAN_EXECUTE_FALSE 19×`. Each cycle still ran a meta-deliberation LLM call (~10s/cycle), proposed a 5-step plan, every step was skipped with `Meta steps present but none executable → falling back to signal-driven ordering`, signal fallback also produced nothing, cycle ended no-op. Run was eventually killed externally; left to itself it would have burned LLM credit indefinitely.
  - **Root cause:** there is no termination guard for the specific failure mode "build is red AND no role is eligible". T1-AH ships a stagnation circuit breaker for `STAGNATING` cycles, but a sequence of `CONTINUE merged=false` cycles with `mut=null` does not register as STAGNATING — the loop is technically progressing (cycles are completing) just doing nothing. T2-CV's signal-driven escalation also doesn't catch it: stage 1 triggers on `REPETITION_DETECTED` or `BUILD_BROKEN.age` with no `mergeCount` change — but when *no mutation is proposed at all*, neither condition fires. T2-CV requires agents to produce something to detect repetition; the zero-work deadlock is precisely the case where nothing is produced.
  - **Fix:**
    1. New `consecutiveZeroWorkCycles` counter on `CycleContextData`. Increment when `cycle.merged == false && mutationState == null && !rolledBackThisCycle`. Reset on any merge OR any cycle that produced a mutation (even rejected — proves the system wasn't deadlocked).
    2. In `AutobuilderService` post-cycle hook: when `BUILD_BROKEN` is active AND `consecutiveZeroWorkCycles >= autobuilder.deadlock.zero-work-limit` (default 5), emit `GOAL_EXHAUSTED` + `SYSTEM_PAUSED` with source `build-blocked-deadlock`. Same pattern as T2-CB's `EMPTY_BACKLOG_INJECTION_LIMIT` halt.
    3. Optional: also short-circuit the per-cycle meta-deliberation LLM call when the deadlock state is detected, so the final 5 cycles before the halt don't burn meta calls. Cheap pre-call guard in `MetaDeliberationAgent.decide()`: if `BUILD_BROKEN >= 1.0 && consecutiveZeroWorkCycles >= zero-work-limit - 2`, return NOOP/Stop without calling the LLM.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** the system was burning LLM credit at ~10s × N=25 cycles ≈ 4 minutes of LLM time producing zero useful output. That's a correctness defect (resource waste with no recovery path), not a steering quality issue. Same severity tier as T1-AF (LLM circuit breaker — also resource-protection) and T1-AH (stagnation breaker — analogue for the non-build-broken case).
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. After N=5 consecutive cycles meeting the zero-work condition while `BUILD_BROKEN` is active, the loop emits `GOAL_EXHAUSTED + SYSTEM_PAUSED` and the scheduler halts.
    2. A merge or a rejected-but-proposed mutation in any of those N cycles resets the counter (the system wasn't actually deadlocked — agents ran).
    3. Without `BUILD_BROKEN`, the breaker does not fire — that's T1-AH's territory.
    4. Behaviour event `system_paused { reason: "build-blocked-deadlock", consecutiveZeroWorkCycles: N }` emitted on halt for offline analysis.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **N-1 cycles of zero work, then a successful BUILD_FIX merge.** Counter resets on the merge. Correct.
    - **Zero-work cycle that wasn't BUILD_BROKEN-driven** (e.g. all roles already executed, NOOP). Breaker doesn't fire — defer to existing T1-AH path.
    - **Analysis runs and produces an outcome but no mutation** (analysis is structural, not mutation-producing). Should this count as "work"? Yes — the run analysed and emitted signals/subgoals. Treat any non-NOOP agent execution as work. Implementation: in addition to the mutation gate, reset on any `agent_selected` event in the cycle.
  - **Risks:**
    - **False positives if a legitimate build-fix attempt takes >5 cycles.** Mitigation: configurable threshold; default 5 chosen because the observed deadlock had 25+ cycles of pure no-op — anything sub-5 is well within normal recovery timing.
    - **Halts the loop for the operator instead of self-recovering.** Acceptable: `SYSTEM_PAUSED` is the right answer when the system genuinely can't make forward progress on its own. T2-CV's proactive escalation (replan → analyze) is designed to prevent T1-EB from ever firing; T1-EB is the backstop for when that escalation also fails.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CV (proactive signal-driven escalation — T1-EB is the stage-3 analogue that fires when T2-CV's earlier stages haven't broken the loop), T2-DE (analysis eligibility audit — if analysis is correctly executable while build is red, the deadlock pathology disappears and T1-EB rarely fires), T2-DF (meta-deliberation role pre-validation — eliminates wasted LLM calls per stuck cycle even before the breaker trips).
  - **Depends on:** None. Self-contained.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-08 after the 2026-05-08 20:02 run analysis. ~50 LOC + ~3 tests. Small.

- [x] **T1-EC: Compile-first gate — write mutation files, run `mvn compile`, skip LLM if compile fails, restore and proceed if it passes** (`EvaluationAgent`, `BuildExecutor`, `MergeRollbackService`, new `CompileProbeService`) — shipped commit 42d023e
  - **Observed (2026-05-09 04:14 run, behaviour-2026-05-09_04-14-42.ndjson, cycles 49–58):** The LLM evaluator scored mutations ACCEPTED at 0.85 for BookController.java code containing `incompatible types: inference variable T` generics errors. These errors were only caught post-merge by T4-AN-1's build gate, triggering a rollback. The pattern repeated: rollback → next cycle's evolution proposes similar code → LLM approves again → another rollback. 9 cycles burned on the same compile error class. Separately, `ErrorResponse` was created in `exception/` and `model/` packages across different cycles; each evolution chose a different import path and the LLM evaluator had no way to know which version was on disk.
  - **Root cause:** Evaluation is entirely in-memory — the mutation's changed files are never written to disk before the LLM call. The LLM evaluates proposed code as text without knowing what the real project state on disk looks like. It cannot detect that BookController imports `ErrorResponse` from the wrong package, or that a generics bound is violated, because it never compiles against the actual accumulated project state.
  - **How the code actually works (verified):**
    - `EvaluationAgent` reasons from `context.projectDiff()` (in-memory diff) and `context.evolutionOutcome()` — no filesystem writes before the LLM call.
    - `MergeAgent` is the only place that writes files to output/. Its inner loop: snapshot originals → `fileService.writeFileResolved()` per changed file → `buildExecutor.execute(fileBasePath, cycleName)` → rollback via `mergeRollbackService.rollback()` if COMPILE_ERROR.
    - `BuildResultHeuristic` reads `input.buildOutcome()` — set by MergeAgent post-merge, never by EvaluationAgent. The `--- BUILD RESULT ---` prompt block shows the *previous cycle's* result, not the current mutation's.
  - **Key insight:** `mvn compile` is cheaper than an LLM call (local process vs. remote API call). Calling the LLM on code that doesn't compile wastes the more expensive resource. Compile should gate LLM evaluation, not the other way around.
  - **Fix:** Extract the write → compile → restore sequence from `MergeAgent` into a new `CompileProbeService`. Call it from `EvaluationAgent` *before* the LLM invocation:
    1. For each changed file in `context.evolutionOutcome()`, snapshot the current on-disk content and write merged content to output/ (same as MergeAgent lines 147–153).
    2. Run `buildExecutor.execute(fileBasePath, cycleName)` → `probeOutcome`.
    3. **If COMPILE_ERROR:** restore files via `mergeRollbackService.rollback()`, set `mutationAcceptanceState = REJECTED` with reason "compile-probe failed", return immediately — **no LLM call**.
    4. **If COMPILE_OK:** restore files, proceed with the LLM evaluation as normal. The compile result can optionally be surfaced in the prompt as a `--- COMPILE RESULT: OK ---` confirmation, but the main value is already captured by skipping the LLM on failure.
  - **Components:**
    - `CompileProbeService` — extracts write/compile/restore from MergeAgent. ~80 LOC. Both MergeAgent and EvaluationAgent call it.
    - `EvaluationAgent` — inject `CompileProbeService`, call before LLM adapter, return early on COMPILE_ERROR. ~20 LOC.
    - No changes to `EvaluationProtocolContract`, `BuildResultHeuristic`, or `CycleContextData` required for the core fix.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** An LLM call on uncompilable code is pure waste — the result is always wrong and the rollback is guaranteed. 14 such wasted cycles in 58 (24%). Same tier as T1-EB (resource waste with no self-recovery path).
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. When evolution proposes a mutation that fails `mvn compile`, EvaluationAgent returns REJECTED immediately — no LLM call is made.
    2. When probe compile succeeds, LLM evaluation proceeds unchanged.
    3. Probe files are always restored before EvaluationAgent returns, regardless of compile result.
    4. Post-merge build gate (T4-AN-1) remains in place as a second check for edge cases where probe passed but full build fails.
  - **Depends on:** T4-AN-1 (`BuildExecutor`, `MergeRollbackService` — reuse). T1-DW (filesystem rollback — safety net if probe restore fails).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DG (rollback-recurrence escalation — fallback for cases where probe passed but merge-time compile fails).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-09 after the 04:14 run analysis. Chain I primary fix.

- [x] **T1-ED: ACCEPTED mutations never reach merge — meta-state persistence + subgoal-shift between evolution and merge** (`EvaluationAgent.persistMutationStateToMeta`, `CycleExecutor` / `MutationSnapshotService` ordering, `FileVariantCollector`, possibly `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-14 19:30 run, `behaviour-2026-05-14_19-30-33.ndjson`, 19 cycles):** One merge in 19 cycles — the bootstrap merge in cycle 1 (15 files). After bootstrap, **four cycles produced LLM-ACCEPTED mutations** (cycles 2, 5, 16, 17 — scores 0.86 / 0.80 / 0.85 / 0.81) and **every one of them was blocked at merge with `can_execute_false`**. T1-EC's compile probe worked correctly (4 probe-rejects, no post-merge rollbacks), but the system landed zero substantive work because the merge path never opened. Filesystem evidence from `firestarter/mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json`:

    | Cycle | LLM result | meta.json `mutationState` | meta.json `currentSubGoalId` |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | 2 (1778779912831) | ACCEPTED 0.86 | **null** | `goal-ce1310f7` |
    | 16 (1778780300441) | ACCEPTED 0.85 | ACCEPTED ✓ | `goal-69caf20d` |
    | 17 (1778780344033) | ACCEPTED 0.81 | **null** | `goal-d6ba40f9` |

  - **Root cause hypotheses (two failure modes, both must be addressed):**
    1. **Silent meta.json persistence failure for cycles 2 and 17.** `EvaluationAgent.persistMutationStateToMeta` (EvaluationAgent.java:347) does `readMutationMeta(metaRelativePath) → existingMeta.withMutationState(ctx.mutationState()) → writeMutationMeta(...)`. The catch at line 361 swallows `IOException` at debug level. `MutationSnapshotService.readMutationMeta` throws `IOException("Snapshot meta not found at: ...")` when the meta.json doesn't yet exist (`MutationSnapshotService.java:44`). So if the evaluator runs before the evolution-stage snapshot has written meta.json, the state is lost silently. Then `FileVariantCollector.getAllVariantPaths` reads `mutationState != ACCEPTED → skip` and `hasAnyVariant` returns false → merge `can_execute_false`. The bootstrap cycle bypassed this because it took a different write path. Need to confirm by re-running with TRACE/INFO logging on the catch site and inspecting whether meta.json exists at persist-call time.
    2. **Subgoal pointer shifts between mutation snapshot and merge eligibility.** Cycle 16 *did* persist `mutationState=ACCEPTED`, but every cycle in this run has a different `currentSubGoalId` (15 distinct IDs across 19 cycles — planner/analysis are restructuring the goal tree on every cycle, ref. T2-DD/T2-DH territory). `FileVariantCollector.getAllVariantPaths` (FileVariantCollector.java:118) requires `activeSubGoalId.equals(ctx.currentSubGoalId())` of the saved meta, except for build-config files (T1-DP carve-out). So an ACCEPTED variant snapshotted under one subgoal becomes unmergeable the moment analysis/planner replaces the active subgoal pointer — even within the same cycle, if the merge eligibility check runs after a post-evaluation analysis/planner step that mutates the pointer. The mechanism is the same one T1-DP fixed for `pom.xml`, but the broader feature-file case is still exposed.

  - **How the code actually works (verified):**
    - `EvaluationAgent.execute` writes `mutationState` to the in-memory context at line 302, emits `ROLE_MERGE` with strength 1.0 / decay 1.0 at line 311 on ACCEPTED, then calls `persistMutationStateToMeta` at line 332.
    - `persistMutationStateToMeta` computes `metaRelativePath = "mutations/" + ctx.cycleName() + "/meta.json"` and reads/rewrites it. Failure is swallowed at debug level — no warning, no behaviour event.
    - `FileVariantCollector.hasAnyVariant` walks `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` for every cycle directory; rejects entries where `mutationState != ACCEPTED` or `currentSubGoalId != activeSubGoalId` (build-config files exempt).
    - `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer.case "merge"` (line 177–210) requires `ROLE_MERGE` + active subgoal + `hasAnyVariant() == true` + no `SUBGOAL_SCOPE_MISMATCH`. The 0/3 ACCEPTED variants reaching `hasAnyVariant()==true` is the gate.

  - **Why T1, not T2:** With T1-EC in place, the compile probe correctly filters bad mutations. The ones that survive — score 0.80+, LLM ACCEPTED, all heuristics on board — are the system's actual work product. Dropping every one of them on the floor at the merge gate isn't a steering-quality regression; it's a correctness failure. The run produced **zero substantive merges in 19 cycles** with otherwise-healthy ACCEPTED mutations.

  - **Investigation plan (start here — root cause may need refining):**
    1. Add `log.warn` (not debug) in `EvaluationAgent.persistMutationStateToMeta` catch block, plus a `BehaviourEvent.MutationMetaPersistFailed` to surface it in `behaviour-*.ndjson`. Re-run; confirm whether cycles 2/17 are throwing `IOException` (meta missing) vs. some other path.
    2. Audit cycle-stage ordering: when is `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` first written? If after `EvaluationAgent`, `persistMutationStateToMeta` is structurally a no-op — the persist call must move to a stage where meta.json is guaranteed to exist, or `EvaluationAgent` must `writeMutationMeta(ctx)` rather than read-modify-write.
    3. Log every mutation of `currentSubGoalId` within a cycle (analysis-proposed subgoals, planner re-decompositions, etc.) with the cycle phase that performed the mutation. Confirms or refutes the post-evaluation subgoal-shift theory for cycle 16.
    4. If shift confirmed: the cleanest fix is to bind merge eligibility to the *snapshot's* subgoal lineage, not the current cycle's pointer. The variant is provenance-stamped already (`meta.currentSubGoalId`); merge can validate the lineage ("is the meta's subgoal an ancestor of or equal to the active subgoal, OR is the active subgoal a descendant the planner just spawned to refine the meta's goal?") rather than strict equality. Alternatively, queue accepted-but-unmerged variants and re-evaluate at each cycle's merge phase until a matching subgoal becomes active or a TTL expires.

  - **Acceptance:**
    1. When `EvaluationAgent` accepts a mutation, `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` on disk reflects `mutationState=ACCEPTED` by the end of the cycle. A failure to persist emits a WARN log and a `mutation_meta_persist_failed` behaviour event.
    2. An ACCEPTED mutation whose snapshot subgoal matches the active subgoal at evaluation-accept time passes `FileVariantCollector.hasAnyVariant()` for the rest of the same cycle, even if analysis/planner mutate the subgoal pointer after evaluation runs.
    3. A regression test covers the cycle-16 scenario: evaluation accepts → analysis runs post-evaluation → subgoal pointer shifts → merge still finds the variant.
    4. A regression test covers the cycle-2 scenario: evaluation accepts on a cycle where the snapshot was created in the same cycle by evolution; meta.json contains `mutationState=ACCEPTED` after evaluation returns.

  - **Depends on:** None (independent of T1-EC). Would benefit from the same `BehaviourEvent` infrastructure used by T2-DF for `meta_step_filtered`.
  - **Pairs with:** T1-EB (overdue watchdog — without T1-EB, a session like this run loops indefinitely; without T1-ED, T1-EB would simply halt earlier on the same broken state).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-14 after analysis of 2026-05-14 19:30 run. Highest-priority new T1 — T1-EC removed the compile-rollback waste; T1-ED is now the dominant reason the loop produces no work.

- [x] **T1-EE: `EVOLUTION_NOOP` signal cascade promotes the wrong subgoal to FULFILLED** (`AutobuilderService`, `BasicSignals`, `Signal`, `GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-18 20:35:35 run, `behaviour-2026-05-18_20-35-35.ndjson`, 6 cycles, 3 merges, GOAL_FULFILLED):** LLM declared `#NoDiff` on exactly 3 subgoals — `goal-29d04861` (GET /books), `goal-8958c1dd` (GET /members/{id}), `goal-70eb3b24` (GET /loans?memberId). Yet `GoalStack.json` shows 8 subgoals COMPLETED, including 2 with no `criteriaPassStatus` and resolution `"evolution #NoDiff — output already satisfies all criteria (T2-CC)"`: `goal-9e4daa69` (GET /books/{id}) and `goal-f7cf6b23` (POST /loans). **Neither was the active subgoal when its corresponding `#NoDiff` was declared** — they were promoted via `#NoDiff` bypass on the next subgoal's pointer because the signal from the prior subgoal was still alive on the context.
  - **Trace from the live run:**
    ```
    20:36:55  EvolutionAgent — LLM declared #NoDiff for sg=goal-29d04861 (GET /books). Signalling noOp.
    20:37:09  CycleExecutor — Subgoal FULFILLED (score=0.7546) for sg=goal-29d04861 — promote branch → currentSubGoalId=goal-9e4daa69
    20:37:12  AutobuilderService — Evolution noOp — #NoDiff implies current output satisfies all criteria.
              Promoting to FULFILLED (subGoalId=goal-9e4daa69)
    ```
    The post-cycle bypass at 20:37:12 promoted `goal-9e4daa69` (GET /books/{id}) — but the `#NoDiff` evidence was generated for `goal-29d04861` (GET /books). Same pattern fires later: `#NoDiff` for `goal-8958c1dd` cascades a FULFILLED into `goal-f7cf6b23`.
  - **Root cause:** `AutobuilderService:408` checks `context.hasStrongSignal(EVOLUTION_NOOP, 0.5)` **after** `cycleExecutor.runCycle()` returns. Inside `runCycle`, evaluation can FULFILL the current subgoal via score-based closure and advance `currentSubGoalId` to the next subgoal. The `EVOLUTION_NOOP` signal the evolution agent emitted for the prior subgoal is still on the context. The bypass then reads the **new** `currentSubGoalId` and promotes it without any verification — no LLM call, no criteria banking, no merge.
  - **Why T1, not T2:** Subgoals are marked FULFILLED with zero evidence — no `#NoDiff` claim, no evaluation score, no merge. The system asserts "goal complete" for work it never validated. In the analysed run, 2 of 8 subgoals (25%) took this path. We have direct evidence the resulting output is silently non-functional (the parallel-store Book bug observed in the same run — `BookController` and `BookService` each hold their own static `List<Book>`, so POST /loans always returns 404 because `LoanService` reads from `BookService.books` which is empty).
  - **Fix — primary (tag signal with originating subgoal id):** Extend `Signal` so emitters can attach a subgoal id to the meta map, then have `AutobuilderService:408` reject the signal when the id no longer matches `currentSubGoalId`:
    1. `EvolutionAgent` at the `#NoDiff` deferred-merge/noOp path: emit `Signal.of(EVOLUTION_NOOP, 1.0, 0.0, reason).withMeta("subGoalId", subGoalId)`.
    2. `AutobuilderService:408`: before consuming, read `noOp.meta().get("subGoalId")`. If non-null and not equal to `context.currentSubGoalId()`, clear the signal and skip the promotion (debug log). If equal or null (back-compat), proceed.
  - **Fix — secondary (consume on subgoal advancement):** In `GoalPlannerService.promoteCurrentSubGoal` (or wherever the pointer advances after FULFILLED), call `context.withoutSignal(EVOLUTION_NOOP)` so a stale signal can never survive a pointer change. Belt-and-braces with Fix #1; less precise alone (would also wipe a legitimate same-cycle EVOLUTION_NOOP for the next subgoal, though that case doesn't arise in practice — evolution runs only once per cycle).
  - **Recommended:** Both. Fix #1 is the load-bearing semantic fix; Fix #2 is defensive.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. `EVOLUTION_NOOP` signal carries `subGoalId` in its meta map (verified by unit test on `EvolutionAgent`).
    2. `AutobuilderService:408` only takes the FULFILLED-via-`#NoDiff` path when the signal's `subGoalId` matches `currentSubGoalId` (regression test: cycle where `EvolutionAgent` emits `EVOLUTION_NOOP` for sg-A, evaluation promotes sg-A to FULFILLED advancing to sg-B, must NOT promote sg-B via the bypass).
    3. Every subgoal in `GoalStack.json` with `resolution="evolution #NoDiff ..."` after a fulfilled run has a corresponding LLM `#NoDiff` response in `responses/` for that specific subgoal id.
  - **Depends on:** None.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DH (the `EVOLUTION_NOOP` carve-out in `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer` is the load-bearing reason evaluation can close the legitimate `#NoDiff` subgoal at all; without it, the legitimate noOp path also fails). T4-AV / T2-CM extension (would catch the consequences of T1-EE's broken promotions — non-functional output that passes structural and compile gates).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-18 after analysing the 20:35:35 fulfilled run. Estimated ~40 LOC + 2 tests. Single PR.

- [x] **T1-EF: `ROLE_EVALUATION` signal decay deadlock — meta-step evolution does not reinstate evaluation eligibility** (`EvolutionAgent`, `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer`, or `InterpretationService`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-19 00:04 run, 31 cycles):** After a compile-probe rejection in cycle `1779141972792`, `ROLE_EVALUATION` decayed below the 0.10 threshold required by `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer` line 109. In the subsequent cycles (`1779141992636`, `1779142089366`, `1779142173847`…) evolution ran only as a meta step (correctly writing mutations), but the meta-step execution reinforces only `ROLE_EVOLUTION` (via `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.executeMetaSteps → reinforceSignal(signalKeyFor("evolution"))` — not `ROLE_EVALUATION`). Result: evaluation hit `can_execute_false` on the signal check before even reaching the fingerprint guard, so mutations written by evolution sat un-evaluated until something external re-injected `ROLE_EVALUATION` above threshold.
  - **Root cause:** `ROLE_EVALUATION` is only reinforced when evaluation actually runs (signal-driven phase, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine` post-execution). When evolution runs exclusively as a meta step it does not emit `ROLE_EVALUATION`. The signal therefore drains to 0 after any rejection, and the gate at line 109 permanently blocks evaluation from recovering autonomously.
  - **Fix options:**
    1. **EvolutionAgent emits `ROLE_EVALUATION`** when it writes files (alongside the existing `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` emission). Semantically clean: "I produced a mutation — evaluation is now needed."
    2. **`ExecutionEligibilityExplainer` loosens the signal gate** to accept `ROLE_EVALUATION < 0.10` when `evolutionRanThisCycle=true` or `hasUnevaluatedPriorEvolution`. The signal becomes advisory rather than mandatory.
    3. **Interpretation service re-injects `ROLE_EVALUATION`** when `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` is present and `ROLE_EVALUATION` is absent/weak. Keeps the signal emission policy in one place.
  - **Recommended:** Option 1 — emission in EvolutionAgent is the lowest-coupling fix and mirrors the existing `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` pattern. One line added in `EvolutionAgent.runEvolution()` alongside `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED`.
  - **Acceptance:** After a compile-probe rejection, evaluation is eligible in the very next cycle where evolution (meta or signal-driven) writes files. `ROLE_EVALUATION` does not drain to 0 across consecutive meta-step-only cycles.
  - **Effort:** ~5 LOC + 1 test. Depends on: None. Pairs with: T1-EC (compile probe gate — the rejection that triggers the drain).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-19 after analysing the 00:04 run.

- [x] **T1-EG: Post-rollback `meta.json` not updated — same broken mutation re-applied on next cycle** (`MergeAgent`, `MutationSnapshotService`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-19 00:04 run):** Cycles `1779142505125` and `1779142561302` both roll back the identical 8 files (`MemberService.java`, `LoanService.java`, `BookController.java`, `DemoApplication.java`, `MemberRequest.java`, `ErrorResponse.java`, `MemberController.java`, `LoanController.java`) with the same `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL: method setReturned(boolean)`. The mutation was applied and rolled back in cycle 505125. In cycle 561302 it was applied and rolled back again. Identical error, identical files, two consecutive cycles.
  - **Root cause:** T1-DW (`MergeAgent`) restores filesystem files on `COMPILE_ERROR` via `MergeRollbackService`. It does not update the mutation's `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json`. The file still reads `mutationState=ACCEPTED`. On the next cycle, `FileVariantCollector.hasAnyVariant()` scans mutation directories, finds this ACCEPTED entry, and the merge gate concludes a mergeable variant exists — triggering a second identical attempt.
  - **Fix:** In `MergeAgent` (or `MutationSnapshotService`) after a successful T1-DW rollback, write `mutationState=ROLLED_BACK` (or `REJECTED`) into the mutation's `meta.json`. `FileVariantCollector` already skips non-ACCEPTED states — the fix is purely in the rollback path adding a meta persist step analogous to what `EvaluationAgent` does on ACCEPTED.
  - **Acceptance:** After a T1-DW rollback, the mutation directory's `meta.json` reflects `mutationState=ROLLED_BACK`; `FileVariantCollector.hasAnyVariant()` returns false for it; the mutation is never re-attempted.
  - **Effort:** ~20 LOC + 1 regression test (verify meta.json after rollback). Depends on: T1-DW (rollback infrastructure — reuse). Pairs with: T1-EH (deferred merge coherence — once T1-EG prevents re-apply, T1-EH prevents the coherence failure on the first apply).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-19 after analysing the 00:04 run. Directly caused by the same deferred-merge mutation that exposes T1-EH.

- [x] **T1-EH: Deferred merge applied against stale codebase — compile probe result is invalidated by intervening mutations** (`MergeAgent`, `CompileProbeService`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-19 00:04 run):** The 8-file mutation that rolled back with `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL: method setReturned(boolean)` was ACCEPTED by the compile probe at evaluation time — meaning `Loan.java` had a `setReturned(boolean)` setter on disk when evaluation ran. By the time the deferred merge was applied (a later cycle), another mutation had modified `Loan.java` to remove that setter, invalidating the probe result. The merge applied the old mutation against a codebase that had diverged.
  - **Root cause:** The compile probe in `EvaluationAgent` (T1-EC) stages the mutation's changed files and runs `mvn compile`. The ACCEPTED decision is cached in `meta.json`. Deferred merges re-use that cached ACCEPTED state without re-probing against the current on-disk state. If any intervening merge changes a file that the deferred mutation depends on, the cached probe result is stale.
  - **Fix — re-probe on deferred apply:** In `MergeAgent`, when applying a deferred mutation (one ACCEPTED in a prior cycle), run `CompileProbeService` again against the current output state before writing files. If the re-probe fails, mark the mutation REJECTED in meta.json (triggering T1-EG's cleanup path) and skip the merge. Cost: one extra `mvn compile` per deferred apply, but prevents the T1-DW rollback + T1-EG double-apply cascade.
  - **Alternative — narrow deferred merge TTL:** reject ACCEPTED mutations older than N cycles (default 2) without re-probing. Simpler but less precise; may falsely reject still-valid deferred merges.
  - **Recommended:** Re-probe path — it's already implemented and cheap (CompileProbeService exists for exactly this).
  - **Acceptance:** A deferred merge whose dependencies have changed since evaluation fails the re-probe, gets marked REJECTED, and is never applied to the filesystem. No T1-DW rollback fires for this class of error.
  - **Effort:** ~30 LOC in `MergeAgent` + 1 integration test. Depends on: T1-EC (`CompileProbeService`), T1-EG (meta.json REJECTED write — needed on re-probe failure). Pairs with: T1-EG (the two fixes together eliminate the double-apply + broken-state loop).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-19 after analysing the 00:04 run. `setReturned(boolean)` was the specific missing symbol; the pattern generalises to any deferred merge over a moved-dependency gap.

---

## Tier 2 — Steering quality

- [x] **T2-BA: Redesign `DeterministicScoreHeuristic`** (`DeterministicScoreHeuristic`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-07):** Two independent bugs:
    1. Confidence is hardcoded at 0.55 (always MEDIUM) — the heuristic never reaches HIGH/CRITICAL and cannot win majority vote in `HeuristicNegotiator`.
    2. `mutationState` is always echoed from the LLM (`original.mutationState()`) — the heuristic never forms its own assessment. A CRITICAL confidence simply rubber-stamps whatever the LLM already decided.
  - **Design:** Two independent dimensions must be set correctly:
    - **`mutationState`**: `globalScore ≥ 0.75 → ACCEPTED, else REJECTED`. The 0.75 threshold is the real acceptance boundary used throughout the pipeline (`EvaluationInterpreter`, `EvaluationAgent`). Note: `EvaluationConfigProperties.heuristicThreshold` (0.4) is orphaned — not used by any heuristic — do not use it here.
    - **`confidence`**: Continuous function of evidence quality, theoretically reachable to 1.0 — no artificial cap. Two axes:
      1. **Temporal consistency** — `1 / (1 + |ΔglobalScore| / k)` where k ≈ 0.05. Approaches 1.0 as score stabilises across cycles; 0.5 (neutral) when no prior score.
      2. **Spatial consistency** — `1 / (1 + cv)` where cv = stddev/mean of `perFileScores`. Approaches 1.0 when all files score identically; neutral (1.0 spatial factor) when < 2 files. Coefficient of variation, not raw variance, because it is scale-invariant.
      - Combine: geometric mean of the two factors (or temporal alone × 0.7 when no prior score exists).
  - **Scope:** `DeterministicScoreHeuristic.evaluate()` only — no changes to calling code. Existing test class `DeterministicScoreHeuristicTest` exists but tests the old stepped approach and must be rewritten.
