  - **Root cause (both heuristics):** `lastScore` is a sequential scalar — the global project quality from the last merge, whichever subgoal produced it. When subgoal B starts, `lastScore` holds subgoal A's merge result. `ScoreRegressionHeuristic` compares B's partial-file score against A's complete-implementation baseline → false regression. `ProblemReductionHeuristic` sees no score improvement vs. A's baseline → unjustified-acceptance REJECTED. Both fire on structurally false preconditions because they share the wrong baseline.
  - **Design:** Replace the single `lastScore` scalar with a graph-backed per-subgoal baseline. The graph already stores `Action {type: 'evaluation', status: 'ACCEPTED'}` nodes linked to `Artifact {score}`. A Cypher query scoped to `targetsGoalId = subGoalId` returns the most-recent accepted evaluation score for that subgoal — the correct within-subgoal baseline. No reset logic needed; the graph gives the right answer for any subgoal at any time.
  - **Implementation (4 parts):**
    1. **`AgentGraphService` + `Neo4jAgentGraphService`:** Add `Optional<Double> findLatestAcceptedScoreForSubgoal(String subGoalId)` and `List<Double> findScoreHistoryForSubgoal(String subGoalId, int limit)`. Both traverse `(Action)-[:PRODUCES]->(Artifact)` filtered by `type='evaluation'`, `status='ACCEPTED'`, `targetsGoalId=$subGoalId`, ordered by `a.timestamp DESC`.
    2. **`EvaluationGraphFacade`:** Expose both methods by delegation.
    3. **`CycleContextData`:** Add `Double lastAcceptedSubgoalScore` and `List<Double> subgoalScoreHistory`. Pre-fetched each cycle — no carry-forward required. `lastScore` stays unchanged for backward compatibility (EvaluationInterpreter logging).
    4. **`CycleContextCoordinator`:** Inject `EvaluationGraphFacade`; pre-fetch both fields at cycle start. Null when no accepted evaluation exists for the active subgoal.
  - **Heuristic changes:**
    - `ScoreRegressionHeuristic`: replace `input.lastScore()` with `input.lastAcceptedSubgoalScore()` as the baseline. Null (first mutation) → abstain (existing null-guard already handles this).
    - `ProblemReductionHeuristic`: (a) replace `input.lastScore()` with `input.lastAcceptedSubgoalScore()` in `scoreImproved` check; (b) add first-shot leniency: `if (input.lastAcceptedSubgoalScore() == null) return HeuristicOpinion.none(NAME)` before the rejection block — a first mutation has no baseline.
  - **Why graph over reset:** A `ContextLineageTracker` reset (`lastScore = null` on subgoal change) would fix the immediate problem but lose history for re-visited or replanned subgoals and requires synchronised clearing logic. The graph answer is always correct, cross-session-safe, and gives `subgoalScoreHistory` for trend analysis (meta-deliberation can see "0.70 → 0.68 → 0.65, declining").
  - **Observed impact (2026-04-16 02:18 session):** POST /loans and POST /loans/{id}/return would both have passed heuristic negotiation with these fixes. LoanController.java references (Loan, LoanService, BookService, MemberService) would have had their dependencies merged correctly.
  - **Verified (2026-04-16 03:47 session):** T2-CE/CF were active. `ScoreRegressionHeuristic` and `ProblemReductionHeuristic` do NOT appear in any heuristic opinion for the session — they correctly abstain when `lastAcceptedSubgoalScore` is null. Mutations for POST /loans and GET /members/{id} are ACCEPTED. However, the accepted mutations were never merged to output (see T1-CT and T1-CU). The heuristic fix is confirmed working; the remaining problems are in the merge pipeline.
  - **Depends on:** T2-AJ (counterpart — widens tolerance for multi-file mutations; T2-CE/CF remove the cross-subgoal contamination that T2-AJ thresholds cannot fix).

- [x] **T2-CG: ROLE_EVOLUTION signal grows unboundedly — drowns other signal priorities** (`CycleStartSignalSeeder`, `Signal`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-16 03:47 session):** `ROLE_EVOLUTION` signal grew from 1.80 (cycle 1) to 8.55 (cycle 9) over 9 cycles. `EVOLUTION_FINGERPRINT_CHANGED` grew to 5.90. `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` grew to 5.80. Meanwhile `ROLE_EVALUATION` decayed to 0.08 (below the 0.10 threshold — blocked evaluation canExecute in one cycle). `ROLE_MERGE` remained at ~1.15–1.25 but was overwhelmed in relative priority.
  - **Root cause:** `CycleStartSignalSeeder` emits a fresh `ROLE_EVOLUTION` signal every cycle. `Signal.reinforce()` accumulates strength via addition: `1.0 + 1.0 = 2.0 → 3.0 → …`. The decay is subtracted each cycle, but the reinforcement outpaces the decay. With `decayPerCycle=0.25`, effective growth per cycle is `1.0 - 0.25 = 0.75`. Over 9 cycles: `1.8 + (8 × 0.75) = 7.8`, consistent with the observed 8.55 (slightly higher due to the initial `reinforce` setting 1.8 instead of 1.0).
  - **Effect:** Agent fitness ranking becomes dominated by evolution — other agents (merge, evaluation, planner) are relatively deprioritized. This is not immediately harmful (the meta-step system selects agents explicitly), but it distorts the signal-driven coordinator fallback and makes signal-based diagnostics unreliable.
  - **Fix:** Cap `Signal.reinforce()` at a maximum strength. Suggested: `this.strength = Math.min(this.strength + other.strength, 3.0)`. Alternatively, use a non-additive reinforcement: `this.strength = Math.max(this.strength, other.strength)` — takes the stronger of the two rather than accumulating. Apply the same cap to `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` and `EVOLUTION_FINGERPRINT_CHANGED`.
  - **Changes:** `Signal.java` — add strength cap to `reinforce()` (~1 line). OR `CycleStartSignalSeeder.java` — don't reinforce if signal already above threshold.
  - **Depends on:** None (standalone quality fix).

- [x] **T2-CH: Replacement subgoals under GIVEN_UP parents trigger premature root auto-completion** (`GoalPlannerService`) *(formerly T1-CV)*
  - **Observed (2026-04-21 00:27 session):** Planner injected 2 ACTIVE replacement subgoals under GIVEN_UP `goal-77b73723` (GET /loans?memberId). Immediately after, `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` fired: 6/8 first-wave ratio = 0.75 > 0.5 → root marked COMPLETED. The 2 ACTIVE replacements were orphaned and never worked on. Root goal halted with GET /loans?memberId and GET /books/{id} unimplemented.
  - **Root cause:** `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` only checked whether direct children are terminal — it did NOT check whether any GIVEN_UP child had ACTIVE descendants (T1-CP replacement subgoals). Those descendants are invisible to the ratio calculation (not first-wave) but represent queued work.
  - **Fix (2026-04-21):** Added `hasActiveDescendantsOf(parentId)` helper + T2-CH guard inside `if (allTerminal)` (before the ratio check). If any GIVEN_UP first-wave child has ACTIVE descendants, auto-completion is deferred and the method returns early. Fires after all replacements have become terminal.
  - **Changes:** `GoalPlannerService.java` — `hasActiveDescendantsOf()` helper + 6-line guard in `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied()`. Tests: 2 new tests in `GoalPlannerServiceTest`.

- [ ] **T2-CI: Merge-regression guard — reject mutations that revert established invariants** (`EvaluationHeuristic`, `CycleContextData`, post-merge snapshot)
  - **Observed (2026-04-22 00:50 session, run 20260422_2):** After 6 successful merges all using `jakarta.validation.*` imports (the project's established framework convention), a later cycle proposed a mutation that switched a controller back to `javax.validation.*`. The evaluator scored that cycle's narrow criteria in isolation, did not see the established convention, and accepted the mutation. The merge then propagated a regression that broke compilation of every previously-merged sibling controller. Each evaluation cycle is criterion-local; cross-cycle invariants are invisible.
  - **Root cause:** No mechanism captures "what has stabilised in the codebase across successful merges." Heuristics evaluate the current diff against per-subgoal criteria; they cannot reject "this diff reverts a convention that 6 prior accepted mutations established." The evaluator has no concept of repository-level invariants.
  - **Fix — invariant snapshot + REJECT heuristic:**
    1. After every accepted merge, sample a small set of cheap "invariants" from the merged file(s) and persist them to `CycleContextData` (or to the graph alongside merge records). Suggested invariants (all string-set membership, no AST):
       - **Import-prefix space:** the set of distinct top-level import prefixes seen across accepted files (e.g. `{jakarta.validation, jakarta.persistence, org.springframework}`).
       - **Base package:** the dominant `package` declaration prefix (e.g. `com.firestarter.bookloans`).
       - **Framework version markers:** presence of `@Valid`, `@Validated`, `@RestController`, `@SpringBootApplication` — the stereotype set.
       Sample only after the codebase has crossed a stability threshold (suggested: ≥ `INVARIANT_ACTIVATION_MERGES = 3` accepted merges) so we don't lock in early experimentation.
    2. Add `MergeInvariantHeuristic`: for each candidate diff, compute the same invariant set on the post-mutation file(s). If any locked-in invariant is contradicted (e.g. `jakarta.validation` was in the established prefix space, the diff replaces it with `javax.validation`), opinion=REJECTED, confidence=HIGH, rationale: "Mutation introduces import prefix `javax.validation` that conflicts with established convention `jakarta.validation` (locked in across N prior merges). Conventions established by accepted merges should not be reverted."
    3. Allow override via planner-stated intent (e.g. a subgoal whose description explicitly names "migrate from javax to jakarta" — match against subgoal text). If the diff is part of an explicit migration goal, abstain rather than reject.
  - **Changes:** New `MergeInvariantHeuristic.java` under `agent/evaluation/heuristic/`. Add invariant sampling hook in `MergeAgent` after successful merge — write to `CycleContextData.mergeInvariants` (new field) or to a `MergeInvariantsGraphFacade`. Register heuristic in `HeuristicNegotiator`. Unit tests: (a) `jakarta` established → diff using `javax` → REJECTED; (b) novel prefix not in invariant set → abstain; (c) subgoal description names migration → abstain.
  - **Depends on:** None (orthogonal to existing heuristics — slots in alongside `IsolatedStateHeuristic`, `ScoreRegressionHeuristic`, etc.).
  - **Why T2 and not T1:** This is a steering heuristic, not a state-machine bug. The evaluator's per-subgoal scoring is correct in isolation; what's missing is a cross-cycle invariant signal. T4-AN-1 (compile gate) is the harder backstop — T2-CI is the cheaper preventative check that fires before compile.
  - **Origin:** Run 20260422_2 javax-after-jakarta finding.

- [x] **T2-CJ: `DeterministicScoreHeuristic` near-miss veto blocks merges in the 0.70–0.75 band** (`DeterministicScoreHeuristic`, `HeuristicNegotiator`, `EvaluationInterpreter`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-29 22:24 run):** 22 mutations on `LoanService.java` and 9 on `MemberController.java` were proposed; only 1 of each was merged. Across the final 8 cycles the system was stuck on `member loan limit validation in LoanService` with `scoreNow=0.7825, globalScore=0.715` repeatedly. Every cycle recorded `CRITICAL: Heuristic 'DeterministicScoreHeuristic' overruled (1.00): Deterministic score from ScoringService.globalScore=0.715 confidence=1.00 → REJECTED`. Five additional cycles in the same run had ACCEPTED LLM outcomes that were structurally orphaned (T1-DC pre-fix), but the dominant pattern was rejection at the band edge: real, non-trivial code changes that scored just under 0.75 and were therefore vetoed at maximum confidence.
  - **Root cause:** T2-BA redesigned the heuristic with a binary boundary: `globalScore ≥ 0.75 → ACCEPTED, else REJECTED`. Confidence is then a continuous function of temporal/spatial consistency — at high consistency it reaches 1.0 (CRITICAL), giving `HeuristicNegotiator` a near-monopoly on the final mutationState. When the deterministic score is in `[0.70, 0.75)` and the heuristic is highly consistent, the heuristic ships REJECTED at confidence 1.0 and overrides the LLM's ACCEPTED. The threshold is exactly the merge-acceptance floor used elsewhere (`EvaluationInterpreter`); there is no soft band where evidence quality nudges the boundary.
  - **Why this is steering, not correctness:** The heuristic is doing what it was designed to do; the system policy is wrong. The 0.715 score on a 60-line LoanService change with banked criteria progress and no regression is a borderline acceptance, not a confident rejection. T2-AE (deferred) addresses the LLM-side anchoring at 0.85; T2-CJ addresses the heuristic-side veto at 0.75.
  - **Fix — primary (soft veto in 0.70–0.75 band):** Change the binary in `DeterministicScoreHeuristic.evaluate()`:
    - `globalScore ≥ 0.75` → ACCEPTED, confidence as today.
    - `globalScore ∈ [0.70, 0.75)` → emit ACCEPTED at MEDIUM confidence (≤ 0.65) **only when** the cycle's evidence supports it: (a) `noNetChange = false` (i.e. the diff is structurally meaningful — leverage `NoMeaningfulChangeHeuristic`'s computation or expose it on the context); (b) no problem regression (`ProblemContext.criticalProblems()` did not grow vs `previousProblemContext`); (c) `lastAcceptedSubgoalScore == null` OR `globalScore ≥ lastAcceptedSubgoalScore - 0.02` (no regression vs prior accepted state for this subgoal). If any condition fails, emit REJECTED at the existing confidence — but cap REJECTED confidence at HIGH (0.75), not CRITICAL, so other heuristics retain real influence.
    - `globalScore < 0.70` → REJECTED, confidence as today (CRITICAL when consistent).
  - **Fix — secondary (per-subgoal acceptance floor learned from history):** Drop the absolute `0.75` constant in favour of a per-subgoal floor: `max(0.70, lastAcceptedSubgoalScore - 0.05)`. First merge in a subgoal uses the absolute floor; subsequent merges use the within-subgoal trajectory. Prevents a subgoal that has already merged at 0.78 from accepting a 0.72 regression, while letting a subgoal that has never merged squeeze through at 0.71. Builds on T2-CE/T2-CF infrastructure (graph-backed `lastAcceptedSubgoalScore`).
  - **Fix — tertiary (calibration audit):** Run a one-off `ScoreCalibrationReport` (new) over recent merge history: compute the distribution of `globalScore` for accepted-and-merged mutations vs accepted-and-orphaned vs rejected mutations. If the boundary is genuinely 0.75, the merged distribution should be sharply right-skewed there. If it's not (suspect: it's bimodal around 0.71 and 0.83), use the empirical valley as the threshold. Inform T4-J (TuningParameterStore) so this becomes a tunable rather than a literal.
  - **Changes:** `DeterministicScoreHeuristic.java` — replace single threshold check with three-band logic + helper for `noNetChange`/`regression` checks (~30 lines, mostly already computed elsewhere). `EvaluationInterpreter.java` — keep the absolute 0.75 floor for the `success` boolean; the heuristic's MEDIUM-ACCEPTED in 0.70-0.75 will still let `HeuristicNegotiator` lift mutationState to ACCEPTED via majority vote with the LLM. Tests: (a) score=0.72, no regression, meaningful diff → ACCEPTED MEDIUM; (b) score=0.72, regression → REJECTED HIGH (not CRITICAL); (c) score=0.69 → REJECTED CRITICAL (unchanged); (d) score=0.78 → ACCEPTED CRITICAL (unchanged). New test class `DeterministicScoreHeuristicBandEdgeTest`.
  - **Depends on:** T2-AT (persistent heuristic warnings for consistent-CRITICAL streaks — useful diagnostics for this work). T2-CE/T2-CF (per-subgoal score baseline for the secondary fix).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-AE (un-park if implementing alongside) — both contribute to the same band, on opposite sides of the LLM/heuristic split. T2-CM (orphan-aware bias) is multiplicative — reducing band-edge rejections compounds with structural-hole detection.
  - **Risk:** Loosening the heuristic could allow lower-quality merges through. Mitigated by (a) the regression conditions, (b) the existing `MergeInvariantHeuristic` (T2-CI) catching cross-cycle invariant breaks, (c) T4-AN-1 compile gate when it lands. Track merge-then-revert rate before/after to validate.

- [ ] **T2-CM: Orphan-aware acceptance bias — reward consumer wiring, penalise new orphans** (new `StructuralIntegrityHeuristic`, `EvaluationHeuristic`, `OutputStructureAnalyzer`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-29 22:24 run):** End-of-run output contains `CreateLoanRequest.java` with no consumers. The file was merged in cycle-1777494898061 (alongside `CreateBookRequest.java`) when `LoanController` accepted DTO arguments via the merged update. The follow-up `LoanController`/`LoanService` updates that *use* `CreateLoanRequest` were proposed many times (LoanController 8x, LoanService 22x) and rejected — leaving the new type unreferenced. Symmetrically, `MemberService.java` references `BookService` (line 18: `private BookService bookService;`) but `BookService.java` is not in output — every `BookService` proposal was rejected (3 proposals, 0 merges). The codebase has a broken edge in both directions: a type with no consumers and a consumer with no type. The system has zero awareness of this; nothing in the evolution prompt or evaluation heuristics surfaces structural-integrity holes.
  - **Root cause:** Heuristics evaluate per-diff scoring quality, not whole-output structural integrity. The evaluation prompt sees the per-file scores but not the cross-file reference graph. T2-CI introduces import-prefix invariants (one form of cross-cycle awareness) but does not look at *types referenced but not declared* or *types declared but not referenced* — those are the two most common and most visible structural holes a partially-completed codebase can have.
  - **Why this is steering:** Like T2-CI, this is a heuristic that reads cross-cycle/cross-file signals the per-cycle pipeline is blind to. Not a state-machine bug.
  - **Fix — primary (`StructuralIntegrityHeuristic`):** Compute, before evaluation runs:
    - `unusedTypes(output)`: classes/records/enums declared in any output `.java` whose simple name has zero textual occurrences in any other output file (regex on identifier boundaries — no AST). Include only types with ≥ 1 declaration site to avoid false positives on package-private helpers.
    - `danglingReferences(output)`: identifiers that look like type references (PascalCase with at least one capital after the first character, used as a field type / parameter / return type / `new X()` / `extends X` / `implements X`) where no `class|record|enum X` exists in any output file or in the JDK/Spring stdlib whitelist (pre-baked list of `String|Integer|List|Map|...` and `org.springframework.*` annotations).
    - `passiveOnlyTypes(output)`: types declared in output where every reference is *passive* — `class` literals (`X.class`), annotation arguments (`@Annot(X.class)`, `@Annot(value = X.class)`), generic type parameters (`Class<X>`, `List<X>` only as a type-token, not as a constructed/iterated value), or `import` statements. **Active** sites are `new X(...)`, `throw new X(...)`, method invocations on an `X` value, casts to `X`, instance-of checks. A type with only passive references is "registered but never used" — common pattern: an exception type with an `@ExceptionHandler(X.class)` registration but no `throw new X(...)` site anywhere. Distinct from `unusedTypes` (which has zero references at all) and worth penalising separately because the LLM otherwise sees it as "in use" and never thinks to wire it.
    Then on each candidate diff:
    - **Reward (+0.05 to suggestedScore, HIGH confidence):** if the diff *consumes* any name in `unusedTypes(prevOutput)`, *declares* any name in `danglingReferences(prevOutput)`, or *introduces an active site* (`new X(...)`, `throw new X(...)`, method invocation) for any name in `passiveOnlyTypes(prevOutput)`, opinion=ACCEPTED with rationale `"resolves structural hole: introduces consumer for previously-orphan type 'X'"`, `"resolves structural hole: declares previously-undefined type 'Y'"`, or `"resolves structural hole: activates passive-only type 'Z' (was registered but never used)"`.
    - **Penalty (–0.05 to suggestedScore, MEDIUM confidence):** if the diff *adds* a new declaration that has no consumer in either the prior output or the diff itself, OR *adds* a new declaration whose only references in the diff are passive (annotation args, class literals), opinion=REJECTED with rationale `"introduces orphan type 'Z' — no consumer in this diff or existing output"` or `"introduces passive-only type 'W' — registered but no active call/throw site"`. Override allowed when the subgoal description explicitly names the type as a deliverable (regex match on subgoal description).
    - Confidence MEDIUM to allow the LLM and other heuristics to override; the heuristic should nudge, not dictate.
  - **Fix — secondary (surface in evolution prompt — see T3-BS):** A `--- STRUCTURAL HOLES ---` block in the evolution prompt enumerating `unusedTypes` and `danglingReferences` lets the LLM target them directly. Without T3-BS the heuristic only fires post-hoc; with it, the LLM has the information at generation time and the heuristic becomes a confirmation rather than a corrector.
  - **Changes:** New `analysis/structure/OutputStructureAnalyzer.java` (regex-based, ~80 lines + tests). New `analysis/heuristic/StructuralIntegrityHeuristic.java` (~60 lines). Cache `OutputStructureAnalyzer` results on `CycleContextData.outputStructure` (new field) so the heuristic and the prompt reuse one computation. Register heuristic in `HeuristicNegotiator`. Tests: `StructuralIntegrityHeuristicTest` covering reward, penalty, override-on-explicit-mention, and the `previousOutput` had-orphan / `currentDiff` resolves-orphan join.
  - **Depends on:** Nothing structurally; pairs with T3-BS (analyzer feeds both heuristic and prompt).
  - **Enables:** Build-test gate (T4-AN-1) becomes more meaningful once references resolve. Combined with T2-CJ (band-edge soft veto), the LoanController-consumes-CreateLoanRequest mutation in the analysed run would have moved from REJECTED at 0.715 to ACCEPTED at 0.715+0.05=0.765 — over the floor.
  - **Risk:** Regex-based reference detection produces false positives on identifiers that happen to be PascalCase (e.g. `JsonProcessingException`). Mitigation: whitelist of common stdlib/framework names, plus the heuristic's MEDIUM confidence (other heuristics can override). Long term, replace with the AST that already exists in `analysis/diff/` if compute cost matters.

- [ ] **T2-CN: Registration-collision heuristic — flag annotation-key duplicates (framework-neutral)** (new `RegistrationCollisionHeuristic`, `OutputStructureAnalyzer`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-30 01:00 run):** `firestarter/output` ended with two `@RestControllerAdvice` beans — `GlobalExceptionHandler` and `BookControllerAdvice` — both declaring `@ExceptionHandler(BookNotFoundException.class)`. Spring's resolver picks one non-deterministically based on bean registration order, so the 404 contract for `GET /books/{id}` becomes ordering-dependent. `BookControllerAdvice` *also* declares `@ExceptionHandler(Exception.class)` — a catch-all that, depending on advice ordering, can swallow `MethodArgumentNotValidException` / `DuplicateEmailException` / `MaxLoansExceededException` before `GlobalExceptionHandler` sees them, breaking every 400/409/422 contract in the goal. The system has zero awareness: neither heuristic fired, neither was visible in any prompt, and the LLM's per-file evaluation never compared the two advice classes.
  - **Why this is steering, not correctness:** The system *can* produce coherent output; it just lacks a cross-file signal that two registrations claim the same key. Like T2-CM and T2-CI, this is a structural awareness gap, not a state-machine bug.
  - **Why framework-neutral, not Spring-aware:** The same pattern recurs across frameworks — JAX-RS `@Path("/x")` on two resources, Quarkus `@Observes` for the same event type on two beans, JUnit 5 `@TestInstance(Lifecycle.PER_CLASS)` declared with conflicting modes, Micronaut `@Replaces`, Jackson `@JsonTypeName` collisions, etc. The detection should ignore framework names and look at the structural pattern: an annotation invocation whose first argument is a `Class<?>` literal or a string literal, used twice on different declarations.
  - **Fix — primary (`RegistrationCollisionHeuristic`):** Reuse / extend `OutputStructureAnalyzer` (T3-BS) to compute one new list:
    - `registrationCollisions(output)`: scan every annotation invocation in output. Extract `(annotationSimpleName, keyArg)` where `keyArg` is the first argument and is either a `<Identifier>.class` literal or a quoted string. Bucket by `(annotationSimpleName, keyArg)`. Buckets with ≥ 2 distinct *enclosing types* are reported. Each entry: `{annotation, key, declaringTypes: [Foo, Bar], locations: [Foo.java:14, Bar.java:22]}`.
    - **Penalty (–0.05 to suggestedScore, MEDIUM confidence):** if the diff *introduces* a new collision (a (annotationName, keyArg) bucket whose count went from 1 to 2 between `prevOutput` and `currentOutput`), opinion=REJECTED with rationale `"introduces registration collision: @ExceptionHandler(BookNotFoundException.class) now declared in both GlobalExceptionHandler and BookControllerAdvice — Spring resolution becomes ordering-dependent"`. Override allowed when the second declaration carries an explicit precedence annotation (`@Order`, `@Priority`, `@Primary`).
    - **Reward (+0.05, HIGH confidence):** if the diff *resolves* an existing collision (deletes one of the two declarations, or adds a precedence annotation to one), opinion=ACCEPTED with rationale `"resolves registration collision for (@ExceptionHandler, BookNotFoundException.class)"`.
    - Confidence MEDIUM on the penalty (some collisions are intentional — e.g. `@Order`-distinguished advices); HIGH on the resolution reward.
  - **Fix — secondary (surface in evolution prompt):** Extend the `--- STRUCTURAL HOLES ---` block from T3-BS with a fourth section when non-empty:
    ```
    Registration collisions (multiple declarations claiming the same key):
    - @ExceptionHandler(BookNotFoundException.class) declared in GlobalExceptionHandler.java:27 and BookControllerAdvice.java:14 — resolution is ordering-dependent
    - @ExceptionHandler(Exception.class) in BookControllerAdvice.java:21 may shadow more specific handlers
    ```
    The catch-all detection (`Exception.class` / `Throwable.class` / a string `"*"` / regex `.*`) is a separate but cheap line emitted alongside collisions.
  - **Fix — tertiary (false-positive throttle):** Maintain a small list of annotation-name + key-arg patterns where collision *is* the intended pattern (`@Test`, `@ParameterizedTest`, `@RepeatedTest`, `@Bean` methods returning the same type intentionally for profile selection, `@EventListener` for the same event type). The list is short (≤ 20 entries) and lives next to the analyzer; framework-specific entries can be added without changing the core logic.
  - **Changes:** Extend `analysis/structure/OutputStructureAnalyzer.java` with `registrationCollisions(...)` returning `List<RegistrationCollision>`. New record `RegistrationCollision(String annotation, String key, List<String> declaringTypes, List<String> locations)`. New `analysis/heuristic/RegistrationCollisionHeuristic.java` (~60 lines). Cache on `CycleContextData.outputStructure` (same field that T2-CM and T3-BS use). Register in `HeuristicNegotiator`. Tests: `RegistrationCollisionAnalyzerTest` (single-bean = no collision; two beans same key = collision; collision with `@Order` distinction = no collision; catch-all `Exception.class` always reported); `RegistrationCollisionHeuristicTest` (introduce / resolve / no-op diffs).
  - **Depends on:** T3-BS (analyzer + cache point). Pairs with T2-CM (both heuristics share the analyzer cache).
  - **Enables:** Eliminates an entire class of "tests pass in isolation, fail end-to-end" failures the LLM cannot diagnose from per-file context. T4-AN-1 (compile gate) doesn't catch this — it's a runtime resolution issue, not a compile error.
  - **Risk:** False positives on intentionally-overlapping handlers without explicit precedence (e.g. test annotations). Mitigation: the false-positive throttle list, plus MEDIUM confidence so other heuristics can override. Real risk is *under*-detection — annotations where the collision key isn't the first argument (e.g. `@RequestMapping(method = ..., path = "/x")`). Acceptable for v1; revisit if a specific framework needs second-arg key extraction.

- [ ] **T2-CO: Spec-literal presence audit — extract literals from goal text, verify in output (framework-neutral)** (new `SpecLiteralExtractor`, `SpecLiteralPresenceHeuristic`, `EvolutionProtocolContract`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-30 01:00 run):** The root goal explicitly mandates `Invalid input returns HTTP 400 with JSON body {"message": "<reason>"}`. The output's `GlobalExceptionHandler.handleValidationExceptions` returns a body of shape `Map<fieldName, errorMessage>` instead — a flat `{"title":"required","isbn":"required"}` shape. Every 400 response in the project violates the contract. The literal string `"message"` does not appear in any handler's return-value construction in output, despite being explicitly named in the goal. The system has no signal: each individual file is fine on its own; only goal-vs-output cross-reference would flag it. Same pattern with HTTP status codes — the goal lists `409`, `422`, `404`, `400` explicitly; nobody checks that the output's handlers actually use those codes (they happen to here, but the check would catch a regression).
  - **Why this is steering:** Like T2-CI / T2-CM / T2-CN, this is a cross-file structural signal the per-mutation pipeline is blind to. The goal text is the source of truth; nothing currently reads it for *literal* contracts at evaluation time.
  - **Why framework-neutral:** No knowledge of Spring's `ResponseEntity`, JAX-RS `Response`, gRPC `Status`, etc. is required — the audit is purely "did the goal mention a literal that doesn't appear in any output file?". Works equally for Java/Kotlin/Python/Go output.
  - **Fix — primary (`SpecLiteralExtractor` + `SpecLiteralPresenceHeuristic`):** Run once per cycle in `CycleContextCoordinator` after the goal text is loaded. Extract the following literal categories from the *goal description* (root + current subgoal):
    - **Quoted string literals**: any `"..."` substring of length ≥ 3, excluding common English connectors (whitelist: `the|a|an|and|or|...`).
    - **JSON skeletons**: regex `\{[^{}]*"[^"]+"[^{}]*\}` — captures inline JSON like `{"message": "<reason>"}`. Extract every key inside.
    - **HTTP status codes**: regex `\b(HTTP\s*)?[1-5]\d{2}\b` (200…599).
    - **Regex literals**: any sequence inside a `\` or after the word "match" / "regex" / "pattern", e.g. `\d{13}`.
    - **Numeric thresholds**: regex `\b(at most|max(?:imum)?|up to|no more than|≤|<=)\s+(\d+)\b` and the inverse for minima — capture the verb and the value.
    For each extracted literal, `presence(literal, output)` = number of output files containing the literal as a substring (case-sensitive for JSON keys / regex / camelCase strings, case-insensitive for status codes / English nouns). Build `SpecLiteralAudit(missingLiterals, weaklyPresentLiterals)` where:
    - `missingLiterals`: literals with `presence == 0`. Highest signal.
    - `weaklyPresentLiterals`: present in tests only (`src/test/...`) but not in main code. Secondary signal — implementation forgot the contract but tests caught it.
    Heuristic vote on the diff:
    - **Reward (+0.04, HIGH confidence):** the diff introduces a previously-missing literal in main code. Rationale `"diff supplies missing spec literal '{"message": ...}' from goal"`.
    - **Penalty (–0.04, MEDIUM confidence):** the diff modifies a file containing a previously-present spec literal in a way that removes it. Rationale `"diff removes spec literal 'HTTP 422' from MaxLoansExceededException handler"`.
    Confidence asymmetric (reward HIGH, penalty MEDIUM) because additions are unambiguous wins; removals can be intentional refactors.
  - **Fix — secondary (surface in evolution prompt):** Extend the `--- STRUCTURAL HOLES ---` block (or add a sibling block `--- SPEC LITERALS ---`) when `missingLiterals` is non-empty:
    ```
    --- SPEC LITERALS ---
    Goal mentions the following literals that do not appear in any output file:
    - "message" (JSON key from {"message": "<reason>"} — goal line 17)
    - HTTP 422 (Members loan limit — goal line 12)
    - \d{13} (ISBN regex — goal line 4) [present, only in Book.java]
    Prefer mutations that introduce a missing literal where the spec dictates it.
    ```
  - **Fix — tertiary (also in evaluation prompt):** Append a one-line summary: `Spec-literal coverage: 7 of 9 literals from goal present in output (missing: "message", HTTP 422)`. Lightweight evidence the evaluator can use as a tiebreaker.
  - **Changes:** New `analysis/spec/SpecLiteralExtractor.java` (~140 lines + tests). New `analysis/spec/SpecLiteralAudit.java` record. New `analysis/heuristic/SpecLiteralPresenceHeuristic.java` (~60 lines). `CycleContextData` — add `specLiteralAudit` field. `CycleContextCoordinator` — call extractor after goal text + previousOutput loaded. `EvolutionProtocolContract.buildPromptBody` — append `specLiteralsBlock(ctx.specLiteralAudit())`. Tests: `SpecLiteralExtractorTest` covering each category (quoted strings, JSON keys, HTTP codes, regex, thresholds); `SpecLiteralPresenceHeuristicTest` covering reward/penalty/no-op.
  - **Depends on:** Nothing structurally; orthogonal to T3-BS but should land afterward (extractor / audit fit naturally next to `OutputStructureAnalyzer`).
  - **Enables:** Catches contract drift at evaluation time without requiring a runtime test gate. Complements T2-CM (which checks *type* references) by checking *literal* references — together they cover the two main classes of cross-file structural drift.
  - **Risk:** Over-extraction noise — quoted English phrases in the goal create false missing-literal alerts. Mitigation: minimum length 3, English-connector whitelist, MEDIUM confidence on penalties. Iteration risk: the extractor might miss multi-line JSON skeletons. Acceptable for v1; revisit if real goals have nested JSON spec sections.

- [ ] **T2-CP: Per-cycle structural audit driver — bundle T2-CM/T2-CN/T2-CO outputs into one evaluator-side signal** (new `StructuralAuditService`, `EvaluationProtocolContract`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-30 analysis methodology):** The end-of-run audit that produced the T1-DD/T2-CJ/T2-CN/T2-CO findings was a manual cross-reference: read the full output, build a mental type graph, compare every declaration against every reference, compare every contract literal against every implementation. The agentic loop performs none of this — its evaluation is per-mutation, scoped to the current diff, and never asks "is the entire artifact set internally coherent?". T2-CM, T2-CN, and T2-CO each fix one slice; this ticket bundles their outputs into a single per-cycle audit that the evaluator sees as one structured input.
  - **Why this is steering:** The individual heuristics already exist (T2-CM/CN/CO). Without a driver they each vote separately; the LLM evaluator sees a flat score adjustment without context. The audit makes the *pattern* visible — "5 unused types + 4 passive-only types + 1 collision + 2 missing spec literals" is a different signal from any single heuristic's vote.
  - **Fix — primary (`StructuralAuditService`):** Bundle the analyzer outputs into one record:
    ```java
    record StructuralAudit(
        List<UnusedType> unusedTypes,
        List<DanglingReference> danglingReferences,
        List<PassiveOnlyType> passiveOnlyTypes,
        List<RegistrationCollision> registrationCollisions,
        List<MissingSpecLiteral> missingLiterals,
        int totalDebtScore  // sum of weighted counts: unused×1 + dangling×2 + passive×1 + collision×3 + missing×2
    ) {}
    ```
    Run once per cycle in `CycleContextCoordinator`, cache on `CycleContextData.structuralAudit`. Surface in the evaluator prompt as a `--- STRUCTURAL AUDIT ---` block with the bullet count summary. The evaluator uses it as one input among many; the heuristics (T2-CM/CN/CO) continue to vote independently on the diff — the audit is for the *LLM evaluator's* awareness, not a separate vote.
  - **Fix — secondary (debt-trend signal):** Track `structuralAudit.totalDebtScore` across cycles. If debt strictly increases for ≥ 3 consecutive cycles without a merge resolving any item, emit a `STRUCTURAL_DEBT_RISING` signal. The signal has no built-in action; the deliberation agent can react (typically: route to `analysis` to propose a debt-reduction subgoal, or to `planner` for re-decomposition).
  - **Fix — tertiary (operator-visible status line):** Add the debt counts to the `/autobuilder/status` REST response and the SSE cycle-end event so the operator UI can show "Output debt: 12 items (5 unused + 4 passive + 1 collision + 2 missing literals)".
  - **Changes:** New `analysis/structure/StructuralAuditService.java` (~80 lines, mostly delegation to the four analyzers). New record `StructuralAudit`. `CycleContextData` — add `structuralAudit` field (replaces `outputStructure` from T3-BS — the wider record subsumes it). `CycleContextCoordinator` — single call point. `EvaluationProtocolContract.buildPromptBody` — append the audit block. Pattern analyzer addition — `StructuralDebtTrendAnalyzer` for the rising-debt signal. SSE event extension. Tests: `StructuralAuditServiceTest` covering bundle composition; `StructuralDebtTrendAnalyzerTest` covering rising / stable / improving cases.
  - **Depends on:** T2-CM, T2-CN, T2-CO, T3-BS. Lands last in the structural-awareness stack.
  - **Enables:** Closes the gap between "per-mutation heuristics" and "whole-output coherence". Provides the operator-visible debt counter that turns a vague "is this run going well?" into a concrete trend line. Is the agentic equivalent of the manual end-of-run audit pass — running automatically every cycle.
  - **Risk:** Debt-score weighting (1/2/1/3/2) is arbitrary. Mitigation: ship with weights as configuration (`autobuilder.audit.weights.*`) so they can be tuned without code changes. The trend signal might over-fire on legitimate planned-incrementally builds (early cycles always add unused types that get wired later); mitigation: require ≥ 3 consecutive *non-merging* cycles, not just 3 consecutive cycles, for the rising-debt signal.

- [x] **T2-BO: GIVEN_UP retry limit + stagnation circuit-breaker empty-backlog bypass** (`PlannerAgent`, `GoalSimilarityService`, `GoalGraphService`, `Neo4jGoalGraphService`)
  - **Problem A — infinite re-injection of unachievable subgoals:** `GoalSimilarityService.isDuplicate()` only blocked re-injection of goals matching an ACTIVE or COMPLETED description. A subgoal that was repeatedly attempted and GIVEN_UP could be re-proposed by the planner indefinitely, burning cycles on a goal that has already proven unachievable.
  - **Problem B — stagnation circuit breaker seizes the loop when backlog is empty:** T1-AH's prune-only mode suppressed subgoal injection unconditionally. If `stagnationCircuitBreakerActive` was set while the backlog was empty (no `currentSubGoalId`), all agents were blocked on a missing subgoal and the loop could not recover without a restart.
  - **Fix A:** Add `GoalSimilarityService.countGivenUpMatches(candidate)` — counts GIVEN_UP goals with similarity ≥ threshold. `PlannerAgent` calls this in both `inject()` and `planInto()` loops: if count ≥ `GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT = 2`, the proposal is skipped and logged as unachievable. `Neo4jGoalGraphService.findDescriptionsByStatus(status, limit)` added to support the query. `findAllDescriptions` now excludes GIVEN_UP goals from the active-dedup check (they are handled separately).
  - **Fix B:** `PlannerAgent`: stagnation circuit breaker prune-only mode now only applies when `!backlogEmpty`. When the backlog is already empty, injection proceeds normally so the loop can recover.
  - **Changes:** `PlannerAgent.java` (retry limit constant + guard in inject/planInto loops + backlog-empty bypass), `GoalSimilarityService.java` (`countGivenUpMatches`), `GoalGraphService.java` (interface addition), `Neo4jGoalGraphService.java` (`findDescriptionsByStatus` + GIVEN_UP exclusion from `findAllDescriptions`).
  - **Observed:** 2026-04-10 (identified alongside T2-BL / T3-BK work).

- [x] **T2-CQ: Harden evaluation rubric against approximate satisfaction** — add a strictness instruction to `EvaluationProtocolContract` telling the LLM to mark a criterion FAIL unless the implementation provably satisfies it exactly as stated, not just in spirit. Domain-neutral — no technology-specific wording.
  - **Observed:** 2026-05-01 run — evaluator scored ✓ on criteria that weren't literally met. Scores of 0.85–0.92 accepted without heuristic override.
  - **Why T2 not T4:** Prompt/rubric improvement to an existing component. Ground-truth execution (T4-AN-1/AN-2) is the deeper fix; this is the cheap steering-level guard in front of it.
  - **Risk:** May become overly conservative. Mitigation: scope the strictness instruction to provably measurable properties rather than semantic intent.

- [x] **T2-CT: Stderr-classified `BUILD_FIX` criteria — name the actual missing artifact** (new `core/build/BuildErrorClassifier`, `MergeAgent`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04):** `MergeAgent.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` (lines 233–236) hardcodes generic acceptance criteria *"Project compiles without errors (mvn build exits 0)"* and *"Compile error reported in the previous build is resolved"* regardless of which Maven error fired. These criteria contain only common English words — `compiles`, `errors`, `project`, `build`, `previous`, `resolved` — that match unrelated Java files via the T1-CS substring scan, contributing to the false `#NoDiff` trust path. Worse, they give the evolver no concrete instruction: the BUILD_FIX subgoal description correctly says *"Fix compile error: The goal you specified requires a project to execute but there is no POM in this directory…"* but the criteria don't mention `pom.xml` at all.
  - **Fix:** new `BuildErrorClassifier` under `core/build/` with enum `Kind { MISSING_POM, CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL, PACKAGE_DOES_NOT_EXIST, UNKNOWN }`, returns `Classification(kind, criteria)`. Pattern-match on stderr (after ANSI stripping — reuse the regex shape from `MergeAgent.ANSI_ESCAPE:256`):
    - `"no POM in this directory"` / `"could not find a POM"` → `MISSING_POM` → criteria mention `pom.xml at the project root` and `mvn compile exits 0`.
    - `symbol:\s*(class|variable|method)\s+(\S+)` → `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL` → criteria name the missing symbol verbatim.
    - `package\s+(\S+)\s+does not exist` → `PACKAGE_DOES_NOT_EXIST` → criteria name the missing package verbatim.
    - default → `UNKNOWN` with today's generic criteria preserved exactly (zero behavioural regression for unobserved errors).
  - **Wiring:** in `MergeAgent.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` replace the hardcoded `setAcceptanceCriteria(...)` call with `classification.criteria()`. Also store `classification.kind().name()` under metadata key `buildErrorKind` so future agents/heuristics can dispatch on it. Consolidate the two `metadata.put(...)` calls into one `saveGoalMetadata` write (current code calls save once after blockerType; just put both keys before the single save).
  - **Tests:** new `BuildErrorClassifierTest` — eight cases: each Kind with realistic Maven stderr (use the captured fixture from the failing 2026-05-04 run for MISSING_POM); ANSI-coloured stderr; alternate Maven phrasings (`could not find a POM`); cannot-find-symbol with leading `[INFO]` lines; package-does-not-exist symbol extraction; null/empty stderr → UNKNOWN; unrecognised stderr → UNKNOWN. Extend `MergeAgentTest.mergeCompletedBranch_T1DM_extractsErrorLineFromMavenBannerStderr` to also assert criteria contain `pom.xml` and `project root` and metadata contains `buildErrorKind=MISSING_POM`. New `MergeAgentTest.mergeCompletedBranch_T1DM_unrecognisedError_keepsGenericCriteria` for UNKNOWN fallback.
  - **Edge cases:** ANSI-coloured stderr — strip first, pattern-match plain text. Symbol with type parameters (`List<String>`) — `\S+` captures angle brackets, LLM interprets fine. Multi-error stderr — classifier picks the first match; the full diagnostic still appears in T4-AN-3's BUILD RESULT block. UNKNOWN preserves today's behaviour exactly.
  - **Risks:** classifier regex set will need extending; UNKNOWN is the safety net, additions are additive (one regex + one criteria template + one test per kind). Don't preemptively add patterns we haven't observed. The criteria text *"mvn compile exits 0"* leaks the build-tool assumption — acceptable today (only Maven supported via `application.yml:48`); generalise to `${command}` once a second tool is supported.
  - **Acceptance:** for the failing-run stderr, the injected BUILD_FIX subgoal has criteria mentioning `pom.xml` and metadata `buildErrorKind=MISSING_POM`. The T1-CS substring guard naturally fails-evidence on the new criteria (no Java file contains the literal `pom.xml`). Subgoal description preserved as today.
  - **Depends on:** None. Pairs with T1-DN (T1-DN's tests assert pom-mentioning criteria — if T2-CT slips, T1-DN ships with generic-criteria fixtures).

- [ ] **T2-CR: Criteria-preserving refinement when REPLAN fires from evaluator-repeat-cap** (`PlannerAgent`, `PlannerProtocolContract`, `GoalPlannerService`, `CycleContextData`, `BasicSignals`)
  - **Origin (2026-05-02):** T3-BR ships a `REPLAN_REQUESTED` emission when the evaluator hits the repeat-cap on a fingerprint with unbanked criteria. The signal correctly hands control to the planner, but carries no payload — the planner re-decomposes blindly. Three failure modes follow if the planner replaces or splits the stagnating subgoal without awareness of which criteria were already banked:
    1. **Redundant re-work.** Subgoal had criteria [A, B, C, D]; banked A and B; can't bank C and D. Planner re-plans into a fresh subgoal that re-states A and B as new criteria. Subsequent cycles re-do work that was already correct.
    2. **Score regression on the new subgoal.** Each new subgoal starts with `lastAcceptedSubgoalScore = null` (per T2-CJ secondary fix). The deterministic-score band edge (0.70–0.75) is unforgiving for first merges; re-decomposing late in a run pushes good code through the band again.
    3. **Banked criteria erased.** T1-BG persists pass-status on the *original* subgoal entity. If REPLAN demotes/replaces the subgoal, the bank is lost — even though the evidence on disk is unchanged.
  - **Fix — primary (B+A in the option matrix):** When REPLAN fires from `evaluator-repeat-cap` (detect via signal source string), the planner should:
    - **Demote-and-narrow rather than replace.** Convert the stagnating subgoal from leaf → branch (`GoalPlannerService.demoteToBranch` style); inject one child subgoal whose acceptance criteria are exactly the unbanked subset (computed from `cumulativeCriteriaStatus`). Parent retains its banked status (A, B); child carries only C, D. Banked work is preserved on the parent's record; child evaluation only needs to bank the residual.
    - **Surface unbanked criteria in the planner prompt.** Add a `--- STAGNATING SUBGOAL: UNMET CRITERIA ---` block listing the parent description and only the unbanked criterion text, when the deliberation context shows the cap-source REPLAN. Steers the LLM toward narrow refinement rather than fresh decomposition.
  - **Fix — secondary (option C):** In `EvaluationProtocolContract`, filter the `--- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ---` block to omit already-banked entries on subsequent cycles for the same subgoal. Reduces evaluator workload and noise but doesn't fix the redundancy-on-replan path. Useful as a paired efficiency win.
  - **Fix — tertiary (option D, follow-up only):** Replace the bare `REPLAN_REQUESTED` emission with a structured signal `CRITERIA_REFINEMENT_REQUESTED` carrying `{ subGoalId, unbankedIndices }` payload. Most principled but requires extending the signal type to carry structured data — design first, ship later.
  - **Test plan:** (a) cap fires with banked=[A,B], unbanked=[C,D] → planner injects child with criteria=[C,D] only, parent retains banked status; (b) child banks C, D in its own cycles → child promotes to FULFILLED, parent auto-completes; (c) cap fires with no banked criteria → planner falls through to existing replace-or-split behaviour (no degradation); (d) prompt block omitted when REPLAN source ≠ `evaluator-repeat-cap` (don't pollute every replan with stagnation context).
  - **Depends on:** T3-BR (cap-emission infrastructure — shipped). T1-BG (`cumulativeCriteriaStatus` — shipped). T2-BY (criteria-span-independence rule — informs when narrowing is safe).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CJ secondary fix (per-subgoal acceptance floor) — preserving the parent's `lastAcceptedSubgoalScore` on the demote path keeps the regression-tolerance baseline intact for the new child.
  - **Gating — do not ship before observing cap-fire in a live session.** T3-BR's cap is a fallback; most stagnating subgoals are caught earlier by T2-BL / T1-CF / exhaustion machinery. If `evaluator-repeat-cap` REPLANs are rare in real runs, the redundancy concern is theoretical and this ticket would add complexity for a corner case. Re-evaluate after the next full-run analysis confirms the cap fires.
  - **Gate falsifier shipped 2026-05-19:** `BehaviourEvent.EvaluatorRepeatCapFired` + `BehaviourLogger.evaluatorRepeatCapFired()` emit on the cap-fire path in `EvaluationAgent` with payload `{subGoalId, retries, cap, fingerprint, bankedCriteria, unbankedCriteria}`. Search subsequent `behaviour-*.ndjson` for `event=evaluator_repeat_cap_fired` to decide whether the gate has been met. 1092/1092 green.
  - **Observation (2026-05-02):** Analyzed runs `behaviour-2026-05-02_23-34-07.ndjson` and `behaviour-2026-05-02_22-11-23.ndjson`. Cap did **not** fire in either run. Stagnating subgoals escaped via `criteria_banked_early_exit` (all criteria eventually banked) before hitting the retry limit of 3. T3-BR's re-evaluation path (force LLM call on unbanked fingerprint repeat) was confirmed working, but the cap-triggered `REPLAN_REQUESTED` path remains unexercised. Gate still applies.
  - **Risk:** Demote-to-branch changes goal-tree shape mid-cycle and interacts with `autoCompleteRootIfSatisfied` ratio computation (cf. T1-CQ first-wave-ratio rules). Plan: lift the demoted node's `planningRunId` so the ratio-filter exemption from T1-CQ applies correctly.

- [x] **T2-CU: Planner BUILD STATE awareness + `BUILD_FIRST` rule** (`PlannerProtocolContract`, `protocol/build/BuildResultPromptBlock`)
  - **Problem:** `PlannerProtocolContract.getUserPrompt` shows goals, completed/abandoned lists, problem context, interpretation feedback — nothing about build state. Observed in T1-DP: planner restructured a BUILD_FIX subgoal into a plain "Add pom.xml" subgoal mid-recovery, dropping the `metadata[blockerType]` tag. The planner is making restructuring decisions blind to whether the build is currently red.
  - **Fix:**
    1. **Render a BUILD STATE block** in the planner prompt by extending `BuildResultPromptBlock.render` with a third `Audience.PLANNER` variant. The closing directive is planner-specific: *"While the build is COMPILE_ERROR, your only valid #SubGoalAppend is build-fix work. Do NOT remove a subgoal that carries `[BUILD_FIX]` metadata until the build passes."*
    2. **Add a `BUILD_FIRST` contract rule** to `PlannerProtocolContract.rules()`: "When the BUILD STATE block shows COMPILE_ERROR, do NOT add unrelated feature subgoals. If no subgoal tagged `[BUILD_FIX]` is active, append exactly one BUILD_FIX subgoal naming the diagnostic. Do NOT remove or restructure an active BUILD_FIX subgoal until COMPILE_OK is observed."
    3. **Render `[BUILD_FIX]` tag** in the goals listing helper (`formatGoalInfoList` or equivalent) when `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX`, so the LLM can see which subgoal protects the build invariant.
  - **Why T2:** the planner is currently dumb (not broken) — it makes structurally-plausible decisions that are wrong because it lacks a piece of context. Steering quality gap, not a state-machine bug.
  - **Depends on:** T4-AN-3 (`BuildResultPromptBlock` formatter — shipped). Pairs with T1-DT (gate keeps execution honest; rule keeps planning honest).
  - **Acceptance:** in a session with `COMPILE_ERROR` carried forward, planner prompts include a `--- BUILD STATE ---` block; planner does not propose `#SubGoalRemove` against a `[BUILD_FIX]`-tagged goal until a `COMPILE_OK` cycle is observed.

- [x] **T2-CV: Signal-driven build-fix escalation — replan → analyze** (`BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`, `AnalysisAgent`, `AnalysisProtocolContract`, new `MetaIntention.ANALYZE_BUILD_BLOCKER`, `CycleContextData.problemContext`, `GoalGraphService`)
  - **Problem:** `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` abstains after `MAX_BUILD_FIX_ATTEMPTS=3` — a private counter that duplicates information already expressed by the signal system (`REPETITION_DETECTED`, `BUILD_BROKEN.age`) and the graph (planner action history per subgoal). The counter is also blind to *why* attempts failed and cannot distinguish "making slow progress" from "genuinely stuck."
  - **Fix — signal/graph-driven escalation (absorbs former T1-DV, re-tiered to T2 2026-05-14; stage 3 dissolved into T1-EB):**
    1. **Stage 1 — replan:** `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` emits `REPLAN_REQUESTED` (source: `buildFixStuck`) when `REPETITION_DETECTED >= threshold` OR when `BUILD_BROKEN.age >= N` with no increase in the current subgoal's `mergeCount` since the last check. No private attempt counter — both conditions read shared state the system already maintains. The planner runs with the BUILD STATE block visible (T2-CU) and restructures around the blocker.
    2. **Stage 2 — analyze:** After stage 1, `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` checks the graph: has the planner already acted on this BUILD_FIX subgoal (planner-action count for `currentSubGoalId` > 0 via `GoalGraphService`)? If yes and `BUILD_BROKEN` is still active, invoke `AnalysisAgent` with intention `ANALYZE_BUILD_BLOCKER`. Analysis prompt receives: `BuildOutcome.stderrTail` (T4-AN-1), classifier output (T2-CT / T4-AN-4), build-tool diagnostic block (T4-AN-5), list of build-config mutations attempted on this subgoal (from `GoalEntity.metadata[mergedFiles]`). Directive: *"Enumerate at least three structurally distinct hypotheses for why the proposed fixes have not resolved the error."* Output injected as a problem-context block on the active BUILD_FIX subgoal; optionally appends refinement subgoals via T3-BE.
    3. **Stage 3 — pause:** dissolved. T1-EB's zero-work-cycle breaker is the backstop: if stages 1 and 2 haven't broken the loop and no agent is doing useful work, T1-EB halts the session. No redundant fixed cap needed.
  - **Why T2:** using shared signal/graph state rather than a private counter makes escalation coherent with the rest of the system's observability. The correctness backstop (halt on intractable state) is T1-EB's job.
  - **Depends on:** T2-CU (planner BUILD STATE block makes stage 1 useful), T4-AN-4 (classifier output for stage 2 prompt), T4-AN-5 (diagnostic block for stage 2 prompt).
  - **Pairs with:** T1-EB (zero-work-cycle halt — the stage 3 that T2-CV's proactive escalation is designed to make unnecessary), T3-BE (analysis-injected subgoals carry criteria — already done), T5-N (per-attempt behaviour events keep escalation observable).

- [ ] **T2-CW: Data-drive build-config file detection from `BuildToolProbe`** (`BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`, `FileVariantCollector`, `MergeAgent.touchesBuildConfig`, `BuildToolProbe`)
  - **Problem:** Three separate hardcoded build-config file lists exist:
    - `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic.BUILD_CONFIG_FILES` (5 entries)
    - `FileVariantCollector.BUILD_CONFIG_FILES` (5 entries — duplicate)
    - `MergeAgent.touchesBuildConfig()` inline literals (`pom.xml`, `build.gradle*`, `settings.gradle*`)
  - All three need updating by hand for each new build system (Cargo `Cargo.toml`, npm `package.json`, sbt `build.sbt`, Bazel `BUILD` / `MODULE.bazel`, etc.). Same generality concern as `BuildErrorClassifier` had pre-T4-AN-4.
  - **Fix:** `BuildToolProbe` (T4-AN-5) exposes `Set<String> configFilenames(toolName)` from the same yaml registry that defines diagnostic commands. All three callers consult it. For multi-tool repos (e.g. monorepo with both `pom.xml` and `package.json`), the union of detected tools' config files is used.
  - **Acceptance:** zero hardcoded build-config filename literals outside `BuildToolProbe.java` or `application.yml`. Adding a new build system is a yaml-only change.
  - **Depends on:** T4-AN-5 (`BuildToolProbe` registry). Small ticket — ~3 callers, ~30 LOC + tests.

- [x] **T2-CX: `summariseStderr` extracts informative compile-error line, not just first `[ERROR]`** (`BlockerSubgoalInjector.summariseStderr`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 02:33 run, behaviour-2026-05-07_02-33-08.ndjson):** With T1-DZ shipped, the system successfully merged `pom.xml` (cycle 4). Cycles 5-6 then attempted Java mutations that hit real compile errors and rolled back with `kind=UNKNOWN, stderrSummary="COMPILATION ERROR :"`. The classifier returned UNKNOWN because the summary is just the Maven banner text — the actual error pattern (`cannot find symbol`, `package … does not exist`) was on the next line. Result: BUILD_FIX subgoal injected with description `"Fix compile error: COMPILATION ERROR :"` and generic UNKNOWN criteria. The LLM has no concrete repair target.
  - **Root cause:** `BlockerSubgoalInjector.summariseStderr` picks the first informative `[ERROR]`-prefixed line, treating "informative" as "non-empty and not framing." Maven compile-error stderr has this typical shape:
    ```
    [INFO] BUILD FAILURE
    [INFO] ------------------------------------------------------------------------
    [ERROR] COMPILATION ERROR :
    [ERROR] /path/to/Foo.java:[12,5] cannot find symbol
    [ERROR]   symbol:   class Bar
    [ERROR]   location: class Foo
    [ERROR] -> [Help 1]
    ```
    The current summariser stops at `"COMPILATION ERROR :"` (first `[ERROR]` line that isn't bare `[ERROR]` or `-> [Help]`). The classifier's substring patterns then miss the diagnostic which is two lines down.
  - **Fix — within `summariseStderr`:**
    1. After ANSI strip, prefer lines that match a *diagnostic* shape rather than just the first informative line. Diagnostic indicators (in priority order):
       - `cannot find symbol` / `package … does not exist` / `; expected` / `class … is public, should be declared in a file named` (Java compiler diagnostics)
       - `no POM in this directory` / `could not find a POM` (Maven invocation)
       - File-coordinate prefix `[ERROR] /path/to/Foo.java:[N,M]` followed by a colon-introduced diagnostic on the same line
    2. **Skip the banner shapes**: bare `[ERROR]`, `-> [Help N]`, and `COMPILATION ERROR :` (the literal Maven section header).
    3. **Fallback chain**: if no diagnostic-shape line is found, fall back to the first informative `[ERROR]` line (current behaviour) so the change is non-regressive for already-handled cases.
    4. **Also include the file-coordinate prefix** when present — the LLM benefits from knowing which file+line broke. Concatenate: `<path>:<line> <diagnostic>` (truncate to 200 chars).
  - **Why T2:** the system still works, just with worse guidance. Steering quality gap. (T1 would be if the system silently advanced or deadlocked — T1-DZ already prevents that.)
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. For Maven stderr containing `[ERROR] /path/to/Foo.java:[12,5] cannot find symbol\n[ERROR]   symbol:   class Bar`, the summary contains `"cannot find symbol"` AND `"Bar"` (or at least one of those). The current behaviour returns `"COMPILATION ERROR :"` — the new behaviour MUST return something the classifier can match.
    2. `BuildErrorClassifier.classify(newSummary).kind() != UNKNOWN` for the most common Java compile errors (cannot-find-symbol, package-does-not-exist).
    3. Existing T1-DM tests for MISSING_POM continue to pass — the summariser must still extract `"no POM in this directory"` from Maven's missing-pom stderr.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Cascading errors.** Maven shows multiple compile errors when several files fail. Pick the FIRST diagnostic — fixing it often resolves cascades anyway. Subsequent rollbacks will surface later errors.
    - **Long file paths.** Path + line + diagnostic may exceed 200 chars. Truncate the path component first (keep the filename + immediate parent dir), then the diagnostic.
    - **Non-Maven build tools.** The diagnostic patterns above are Maven/javac-flavoured. For Gradle/sbt the patterns differ (e.g. Gradle's `error: cannot find symbol` is similar but framed differently). Out of scope for T2-CX — the patterns added here apply broadly enough to javac output regardless of invoker. T4-AN-4's adaptive LLM classifier is the long-term answer.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Pattern over-fit to a specific Maven version.** Maven's banner shape is stable enough that this is low-risk; if a future Maven changes the framing, the fallback to current behaviour preserves the previous-state behaviour.
    - **Classifier still UNKNOWN.** Even with a better summary, errors outside the three known patterns (MISSING_POM / CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL / PACKAGE_DOES_NOT_EXIST) still classify UNKNOWN. Adding more patterns is a follow-up — but at least the description text is now informative for the LLM even when the classifier is generic.
  - **Depends on:** None.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CY (which uses the file path the summariser now extracts to populate the BUILD_FIX subgoal description), T4-AN-4 (replaces the regex classifier with an adaptive LLM-driven one — eventually subsumes the per-error-shape pattern set).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after analysing the 2026-05-07 02:33 run, the first session that successfully merged `pom.xml` past T1-DZ. Three cycles later the system hit a Java compile error that was misclassified as UNKNOWN due to this gap — pure protocol-quality issue, not a correctness defect.

- [x] **T2-CY: BUILD_FIX subgoal description names the actually-failed file paths** (`BlockerSubgoalInjector`, `BuildErrorClassifier`, `RolledBackEvidence`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 02:33 run):** After T1-DZ shipped, BUILD_FIX subgoals injected from rollback evidence had descriptions like `"Fix compile error: COMPILATION ERROR :"` (T2-CX addresses the source-line content) — but even with a better summary, the description doesn't tell the LLM *which file* broke compilation. The rollback evidence carries `restoredPaths` (the mutation's file list) and the stderr summary contains the file:line prefix once T2-CX lands. Both are dropped on the floor when constructing the subgoal description.
  - **Why this matters:** When the BUILD_FIX subgoal becomes current, the evolver's prompt shows the subgoal description and a few other context blocks. Without the failed file path, the evolver has to guess which file's compile error it should attack. With the path explicit (e.g. `"Fix compile error: src/main/java/com/example/library/BookController.java — cannot find symbol: class Book"`) the evolver knows exactly where to focus and can either fix the existing file or recognise it was never persisted (rolled-back state) and propose a fresh implementation.
  - **Fix:**
    1. Extend `BlockerSubgoalInjector.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` to extract a file path from the stderr summary when present (the file-coordinate prefix that T2-CX preserves: `"path/to/Foo.java:[12,5]"`). Fall back to the first non-build-config path from `restoredPaths` (passed as a new optional parameter or a synthetic field on the source `BuildOutcome`).
    2. Description format: `"Fix compile error in <relativePath>: <diagnostic>"` — keeps the existing `"Fix compile error:"` prefix anchor for downstream consumers but adds the path.
    3. Acceptance criteria gain an explicit named-path criterion: `"<relativePath> compiles without errors"` in addition to the generic `"mvn compile exits 0 with no errors"`.
    4. **Synthetic outcomes from rollback evidence:** `BuildBlockGate.effectiveBuildOutcome` (T1-DZ) currently passes `cycleId` from the evidence. Extend to thread `restoredPaths` into the synthetic outcome so the injector can also pull from there when stderr lacks a file prefix. Either add a new field to `BuildOutcome` (`List<String> contextPaths`) or use a parallel param to the injector. New field is cleaner.
  - **Why T2:** steering quality — narrows the LLM's attention to the actual repair target. Same justification as T2-CT (which made the criteria name the missing artifact rather than say "Project compiles").
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. BUILD_FIX subgoals injected from a stderr containing `[ERROR] /path/to/Foo.java:[12,5] cannot find symbol` have descriptions starting with `"Fix compile error in path/to/Foo.java"` (or similar — path normalised relative to `output/`).
    2. BUILD_FIX subgoals injected from rollback evidence with `restoredPaths=[Book.java, BookController.java]` and stderr lacking a file prefix have descriptions referencing one of the restored paths.
    3. T1-DM existing tests continue to pass for MISSING_POM — that case has no per-file diagnostic, so the description stays generic (`"Fix compile error: no POM in this directory"`) and the criteria still call out `"pom.xml exists at project root"`.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Multiple files in the rollback set.** Pick the first that appears in the stderr; if none, pick the first non-build-config path. Cascading errors are handled by subsequent rollbacks.
    - **No path information at all.** Fall back to the existing description shape (no file). Don't synthesize a fake path.
    - **Cross-package errors.** A file may fail to compile because of an issue in another file (e.g. missing dependency). The named path is still useful as the *symptom* location even when the *cause* is elsewhere — narrows the LLM's attention.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Path extraction misparses the file-coordinate prefix.** Maven's format is consistent (`/abs/path/to/Foo.java:[N,M]`). Use a tight regex; fall back to no path if it doesn't match.
    - **Absolute paths leak into the description.** Strip the `output/` prefix (or the configured `file.base-path`) so the description shows `src/main/java/com/example/Foo.java`, not `/home/user/.../firestarter/output/src/main/java/com/example/Foo.java`.
  - **Depends on:** T2-CX (provides the file-coordinate prefix in the summary). Small ticket — ~30 LOC + tests.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CT (the criteria-naming sibling — same theme of "name the actual artifact").
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 alongside T2-CX. Both target the BUILD_FIX subgoal description quality observed as inadequate in the 2026-05-07 02:33 run.

- [x] **T2-CZ: Interpretation must treat rolled-back cycles as no-progress, not "healthy"** (`InterpretationService` / `DefaultInterpretationAgent`, `InterpretationProtocolContract`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 02:04 run, autobuilder.log:1489 onwards; persists in 02:33 run):** The interpretation block rendered in subsequent cycles' prompts after a rolled-back cycle reads `"Summary: No notable patterns detected; cycle history shows healthy progress with a clear evolution direction and accepted mutations."` — even when every recent cycle ended `result=ROLLED_BACK, merged=False`. The interpretation is reading rolled-back cycles as healthy because:
    1. Pre-T1-DX, the cycle context retained `mutationState=ACCEPTED` and a high score after rollback. Interpretation reads those and concludes "we're accepting good mutations."
    2. Post-T1-DX, those fields are cleared on the rolled-back cycle's outgoing context — but the interpretation history stored in graph/snapshot may still contain pre-T1-DX cycles, and even fresh post-T1-DX cycles surface only as `STAGNATING` or `CONTINUE` in some paths, never as `ROLLED_BACK` to the interpreter.
    3. The interpretation agent doesn't have a concept of "rolled-back cycle" — its prompt and signal logic treat any non-error cycle as a normal data point.
  - **Why this matters:** The interpretation block is part of the planner's and evolver's prompts. When it falsely says `"healthy progress"`, it directly contradicts the BUILD_FIRST nudge from T2-CU and the LAST CYCLE ROLLED BACK block from T1-DX, giving the LLM ambiguous signals. The system has the right data to know it's not making progress; the interpretation layer fails to surface it.
  - **Fix — three-part:**
    1. **`InterpretationService` / `DefaultInterpretationAgent`** consults `cycleResultState=ROLLED_BACK` from the recent-cycle history (graph snapshot or context lineage). When the most recent cycle was rolled back, the interpretation summary MUST include `"Recent cycles include rollbacks — apparent progress may be phantom"` or equivalent, and MUST NOT use language like `"healthy progress"`, `"accepted mutations"`, or `"clear evolution direction"` for cycles that rolled back.
    2. **Recent-cycle scoring change**: rolled-back cycles count as zero-progress regardless of their pre-rollback `mutationState=ACCEPTED` flag. Today the interpretation may average scores or count "accepted" mutations; rolled-back cycles must be excluded or counted as failures in any such aggregation.
    3. **Prompt directive in `InterpretationProtocolContract`**: explicit rule — "If recent cycles include `result=ROLLED_BACK`, the system has NOT made progress in those cycles. Do NOT describe them as healthy or progressing. Surface the rollback explicitly in the summary."
  - **Why T2 not T1:** the interpretation block is steering input, not a state-machine decision. It misleads the LLM but doesn't cause the system to silently advance the goal tree (T1-DY already fixed that). Still: misleading the LLM into proposing the same broken Java files cycle after cycle is a steering-quality defect with downstream correctness impact.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. After a session containing rolled-back cycles, the interpretation block in the next planner / evolver prompt must include the word `"rolled back"` (or `"rollback"`) and must NOT contain the phrase `"healthy progress"` or `"accepted mutations"` describing those cycles.
    2. Rolled-back cycles must be counted as zero-progress in any score-averaging or accepted-mutation-counting performed by the interpretation agent.
    3. Existing interpretation tests continue to pass for sessions without rollbacks (no false positives).
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Interpretation can't access `cycleResultState`.** Today it may only see scores and signals. Add `cycleResultState` to the recent-cycle history surface (graph snapshot ActionEvidence or `CycleContextData.recentResultStates` field). Wider change but necessary if the interpretation is to know.
    - **Rolled-back cycle followed by a successful BUILD_FIX merge cycle.** The interpretation should describe this as "recovered" not "broken" — the rollback was followed by a corrective merge. Heuristic: if the most recent cycle's `cycleResultState != ROLLED_BACK` and the active subgoal is no longer BUILD_FIX, the interpretation can return to normal language even if older cycles were rolled back.
    - **Long history of rolled-back cycles.** When a session is stuck (many rollbacks), the interpretation should escalate language ("repeated rollbacks indicate the build cannot self-repair") to guide planner/analyser response. Could pair with T2-CV (escalation infrastructure).
  - **Risks:**
    - **Existing interpretation prompt regressions.** The agent's prompt is calibrated to specific score thresholds (T2-BB pending). Adding rollback-awareness may interact with those thresholds — verify the new directive doesn't fire on cycles that ended STAGNATING but didn't actually roll back.
    - **History retrieval performance.** If consulting `cycleResultState` requires a graph query per cycle, batch it with the existing snapshot fetch.
  - **Depends on:** T1-DX (defines the `ROLLED_BACK` cycle result that this ticket consumes). Pairs with T2-BB (live-threshold injection — both ride on the interpretation prompt contract).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CV (escalation language for sustained rollback sessions), T2-CU (planner BUILD_FIRST directive — both protect against ambiguous LLM steering when build is broken).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after the 2026-05-07 02:33 run, where the interpretation block was directly observed contradicting the LAST CYCLE ROLLED BACK and BUILD_FIX-subgoal-active state. Symptom across multiple runs in this session — pre-existing defect that became more visible after T1-DZ enabled progress past the pom.xml gate.

- [x] **T2-DA: Evolution protocol contract must teach the LLM to emit configuration files** (`EvolutionProtocolContract`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 20:16 run, autobuilder-2026-05-07_20-16-33.log):** First two cycles produced 13 Java files but no `pom.xml`. The build failed both times, both rolled back. Only after a BUILD_FIX subgoal explicitly named `pom.xml` did the LLM produce one (cycle 3). Looking at the contract: every `STATIC_RULES` example uses `java [src/main/java/...]` — `RETURN_CHANGED_FILES`, `MARK_DELETED_FILES`, `PROBLEM_TARGETING`, `BLOCKERS_ONLY`, `KEYWORD_ANCHORING` all show only Java code blocks. The LLM has no concrete pattern for emitting `pom.xml`, `application.properties`, or any other config artifact. The line at the bottom of `getUserPrompt` saying `"Or one or more configuration files if necessary (e.g, pom.xml)"` is a weak tail-of-prompt hint with no example body — the LLM follows the rule examples, not the closing paragraph.
  - **Fix:**
    1. **New rule `CONFIG_FILES_WHEN_NEEDED`** in `STATIC_RULES` with an explicit `xml [pom.xml]` example body (full minimal Spring Boot Maven POM, ~30 lines). The rule text: *"When the build requires a configuration file (e.g. `pom.xml`, `build.gradle`, `application.properties`), emit it as a fenced block with the appropriate language tag and the file's relative path in square brackets, exactly like a code file. Do NOT skip a config file because it is not a `.java` source — the build needs it to compile your code."*
    2. **Update `RETURN_CHANGED_FILES` example** to mention that the same shape applies to non-Java files: keep the `java` example and add a one-liner that the same `[path]`-in-brackets pattern works for `xml`, `properties`, `yml`, `gradle`, etc.
    3. **Promote the tail-of-prompt line** into the rule body where the LLM actually reads format guidance — so it's at the top of contract text alongside the other rules, not buried at the response-instructions tail.
  - **Why T2:** the LLM is *capable* of producing `pom.xml` (cycle 3 proved it once explicitly told to). The defect is steering quality — the protocol doesn't tell the LLM that config files are first-class output. Pure prompt-contract issue.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. The rendered contract text contains at least one `pom.xml` example as a fenced `xml [pom.xml]` block.
    2. `EvolutionProtocolContract.isValidResponse` continues to accept config-file blocks (the existing `FILE_CODE_BLOCK` regex `(?s)\`\`\`[^\\n\`]*\\[[^\\]\\n]+\\]\\s*\\n.*?\\n\`\`\`` is already language-agnostic — verified, no parser change needed).
    3. Existing tests for `RETURN_CHANGED_FILES`, `BLOCKERS_ONLY`, `PROBLEM_TARGETING` continue to pass.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Multiple config files in one mutation.** The LLM may emit `pom.xml`, `application.properties`, and several Java files in the same response. Already supported by the parser.
    - **LLM emits a config file the project doesn't need.** Caller logic (acceptance, merge, build) is the truth — the contract just makes config-file output legible.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Prompt length growth.** A 30-line `pom.xml` example adds ~600 tokens to every evolution prompt. Acceptable: it's the difference between the system bootstrapping a Maven project in 1 cycle vs 3.
    - **LLM over-emitting `pom.xml`.** Possible — the rule should phrase "when needed" so the LLM doesn't reflexively re-emit pom.xml on every cycle. Mitigated by the existing `Diff Summary` and `previousOutput` blocks that show what already exists.
  - **Depends on:** None. Self-contained protocol change.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DB (the merge guard prevents wasted work when the LLM still fails to emit pom.xml — together they bound the cost of the LLM forgetting).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after analysing the 20:16 run — the unstaged tail-of-prompt one-liner added during T2-CY work was insufficient. ~30 LOC + tests.

- [x] **T2-DB: Skip per-file merges when prior cycle rolled back and mutation doesn't address the rollback target** (`MergeAgent`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 20:11 run, autobuilder-2026-05-07_20-11-56.log:4432-5189):** After a successful `pom.xml` merge (20:15:14, COMPILE_OK), the next cycle attempted a 13-path Java-file mutation. `MergeProcessor` made **5 per-file LLM merge calls** (GlobalExceptionHandler, Book, BookService, LibraryApplication, BookController — ~11 seconds of LLM time) before the trailing build returned `COMPILE_ERROR` and `T1-DW` rolled all 13 paths back. The same pattern repeated multiple times across the session. Each chain of rollback cycles pays N LLM merge calls per cycle, all of which feed into a state guaranteed to be discarded.
  - **Root cause:** Merges run unconditionally regardless of cross-cycle evidence that the project is in a broken state. `ctx.lastRollback()` (set by T1-DX when the previous cycle rolled back) is read by `BuildBlockGate` and `RolledBackPromptBlock` but not by `MergeAgent` — so the same cross-file Java mutations get fully re-merged + re-built + re-rolled-back each cycle.
  - **Fix — early-skip guard at top of `MergeAgent.mergeCompletedBranch`:**
    1. After the existing `currentSubGoalId` null-guard, check `ctx.lastRollback()`. If null, proceed normally (no behaviour change for healthy paths).
    2. If non-null AND none of the mutation's file paths matches a path in `lastRollback.restoredPaths()` AND none of the mutation's paths matches the BUILD_FIX target derived from `lastRollback.errorKind()` (e.g. `pom.xml` for `MISSING_POM` — reuse `BuildFixTargets.preferredRepairTarget`), return early. Result: no per-file merges, no build run, no rollback noise. Cycle ends as a no-op merge with `merged=false` and the existing BUILD_FIX subgoal carries forward.
    3. Log line: `T2-DB: skipping merges — prior cycle rolled back and mutation does not address rollback target ({targetPath}/{kind})`.
  - **Why T2, not T1:** today the system *can* recover from the rollback chain (T1-DZ injected a BUILD_FIX subgoal, evolution eventually produces the right artifact), it just burns LLM time getting there. Steering-quality cost, not a correctness defect.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. When `ctx.lastRollback()` is non-null and the mutation does NOT include any file from `restoredPaths` or the kind-derived target, `MergeAgent` returns early without invoking `MergeProcessor.process` for any path.
    2. When `ctx.lastRollback()` is null OR the mutation DOES include a relevant repair file, all paths are merged as before.
    3. New behaviour event `merge_skipped_due_to_rollback` (or a log-only signal — TBD during implementation) records the skip with `cycleId`, `restoredPaths`, and the reason.
  - **Edge cases:**
