    - **Mutation includes pom.xml AND unrelated Java files after MISSING_POM rollback.** The fix file is present → guard does NOT fire → all paths merge. Correct.
    - **Mutation includes ONE of the restored paths but not all.** Guard does NOT fire. The presence of any rollback-related file is enough evidence the LLM is attempting to address the issue.
    - **First cycle (no prior rollback).** `ctx.lastRollback()` null → guard does NOT fire. We still pay N LLM calls in cycle 1; this fix bounds the COST of subsequent cycles in the rollback chain, not the first cycle.
    - **Successful BUILD_FIX merge clears `lastRollback`.** T1-DX clears it (one-cycle TTL). Guard returns to inactive. Normal flow resumes.
  - **Risks:**
    - **False positive — guard fires on a legitimate non-fix-targeted mutation.** Acceptable: the cycle is a no-op merge, BUILD_FIX subgoal stays current, next cycle's evolution gets a stronger signal to address the BUILD_FIX target. No data loss, no rollback noise.
    - **`restoredPaths` matching is path-string-based.** Use suffix matching with normalisation (strip leading `/output/` etc., the same `normaliseRelativePath` shape used in `BlockerSubgoalInjector.resolveFailedPath`).
  - **Depends on:** T1-DX (provides `ctx.lastRollback()` evidence and TTL semantics), T1-DZ (the wider Chain-H wiring this guard pairs with).
  - **Pairs with:** T1-EA (evaluation under BUILD_FIX — together they make the rollback-chain recovery fast: T1-EA closes the fix in 1 cycle, T2-DB cuts the wasted LLM work in failed bridging cycles).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after the 20:11 run showed 5 per-file merge LLM calls per cycle being thrown away. ~30 LOC + tests in `MergeAgent`. Subgoal: future iteration may extend to a per-merge build check, but per current "compatibility and stability" decision (2026-05-07) we keep the simpler skip-on-evidence variant.

- [x] **T2-DC: Symbol-existence verification on analysis/planner output — kill hallucinated-symbol loops at the source** (`AnalysisAgent`, `PlannerAgent`, `AnalysisResponseParser`, `PlannerResponseParser`, new `core/grounding/SymbolGroundingService`) — shipped 2026-05-08
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 23:51 run, `behaviour-2026-05-07_23-51-14.ndjson` + `autobuilder-2026-05-07_23-51-14.log`):** 14 cycles, 0 merges, 7 ROLLED_BACK with identical stderr `symbol: class BookAlreadyLoanedException` / `errorKind=CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL` on `src/main/java/com/example/library/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java`. The class does **not exist** on disk (`firestarter/output/.../exception/` contains only `GlobalExceptionHandler.java`), but analysis-agent output stated `BookAlreadyLoanedException is defined :: src/main/java/com/example/library/exception/BookAlreadyLoanedException.java` and `LoanController has logic to throw BookAlreadyLoanedException :: src/main/java/com/example/library/controller/LoanController.java` — neither file is present. The planner trusted the cited paths verbatim and built a 5-deep subgoal chain on top of them.
  - **Root cause:** analysis/planner protocol contracts allow the LLM to cite file paths and symbol names without grounding them. There is no post-parse pass that verifies cited paths exist or cited classes/symbols are actually defined. Cited paths flow into `acceptanceCriteria`, `subgoalDescription`, and the evolution prompt as if they were facts — so every downstream agent reasons against fabricated state.
  - **Fix:** New `core/grounding/SymbolGroundingService` with two operations: `verifyPathExists(String relPath)` (filesystem check under `file.base-path/output`) and `verifySymbolDefined(String simpleOrFqn)` (filesystem grep for `class X` / `interface X` / `record X` / `enum X` plus, when Neo4j is populated, a graph lookup). After parsing analysis/planner LLM output, walk every cited path and every cited Java identifier through the service. For each unresolved citation, either (a) auto-rewrite the citation to flag it as "MISSING — needs creation" so downstream subgoals are framed as creation tasks, or (b) drop the citation and emit a `GROUNDING_FAILED` signal that suppresses subgoal injection that depends on the missing artifact.
  - **Why T2, not T1:** orchestration is correct — T1-DW caught every bad merge, no wrong state stuck. The failure is steering: the loop is blind to the fact that an LLM-cited symbol doesn't exist, and re-uses the fabricated citation across cycles. Canonical T2 ("agents repeat mistakes they've been penalized for, orchestration is blind to its own history"). Cost is high (14 wasted cycles in one observed run) but the "wastes significant resources" T1 clause would otherwise swallow most of T2; held to the stricter reading.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. When analysis or planner output cites a path that does not exist under `file.base-path/output`, the citation is rewritten to `<path> [NOT YET PRESENT]` or removed before it reaches subgoal injection / evolution prompt assembly.
    2. When analysis or planner output cites a Java identifier that has no `class|interface|record|enum X` declaration anywhere under `output/`, the identifier is annotated `<symbol> [UNDEFINED]` and a `MISSING_SYMBOL` signal is emitted naming the symbol.
    3. Regression test: with a fixture project missing `BookAlreadyLoanedException.java`, an analysis output that cites it produces a subgoal whose description names the symbol as undefined and whose first acceptance criterion is "Create `BookAlreadyLoanedException.java`" — not "Add import for `BookAlreadyLoanedException`".
    4. Existing analysis/planner tests with valid citations are unaffected (grounding service returns "verified" → no rewrite).
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Symbol exists in a JDK or third-party JAR** (e.g. `IllegalArgumentException`). Filesystem grep returns no match but the symbol is real. Mitigation: maintain a small allowlist of `java.*` / `javax.*` / `org.springframework.*` package prefixes that skip the existence check. Long-term replace with a proper classpath-aware resolver.
    - **Symbol cited as fully-qualified name vs simple name.** Service must check both — `com.example.library.exception.BookAlreadyLoanedException` and `BookAlreadyLoanedException` should both resolve to the same filesystem search.
    - **Citation in free-form prose ("the LoanController throws…")**. Out of scope — only cited identifiers that the parser already extracts as structured fields (`acceptanceCriteria`, `subGoalAppend.description`, problem-context file paths) are checked.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Over-rewriting suppresses legitimate forward-looking citations** (e.g. analysis correctly proposes "create `LoanService` with this signature"). Mitigation: the rewrite annotates ("NOT YET PRESENT") rather than deletes, so the planner can still treat it as a creation target — exactly the framing we want for divergence (see T2-DD).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DD (when grounding fails post-merge — i.e. build rolled back on `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL` — divergence escalation forces "create the missing class" rather than re-editing the file that references it).
  - **Depends on:** none. Self-contained.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-08 after the 2026-05-07 23:51 run analysis. Estimated ~1 day: ~150 LOC for `SymbolGroundingService`, ~50 LOC parser hooks, ~5 tests. Symbol allowlist starts minimal (`java.lang`, `java.util`, `org.springframework.*`); broaden as false positives surface.

- [x] **T2-DD: BUILD_FIX divergence escalation on repeated identical rollbacks — force the planner off the same file** (`BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic`, `BlockerSubgoalInjector`, `PlannerAgent`, `PlannerProtocolContract`, `MergeRollbackService`, new `RollbackStreakTracker`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 23:51 run):** 7 consecutive cycles, every rollback identical: `errorKind=CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL`, `restoredPaths=[src/main/java/com/example/library/exception/GlobalExceptionHandler.java]`, `stderrSummary="symbol:   class BookAlreadyLoanedException"`. The active BUILD_FIX subgoal `goal-5e40242a` had `subgoalSignals.REPETITION_DETECTED=3.0` and `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST=3.0` — but the planner kept generating sibling subgoals (`Add missing import for BookAlreadyLoanedException`, `Ensure the BookAlreadyLoanedException is correctly imported and used`) all targeting the same `GlobalExceptionHandler.java`. The right move — "create `BookAlreadyLoanedException.java`" or "revert the GlobalExceptionHandler edit that introduced the dangling reference" — was never proposed.
  - **Root cause:** Chain H invariants stop the bad merge from sticking, but they don't actively redirect the planner's next subgoal. `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic` has a consecutive-failure dampener (T1-DQ, cap=3) that abstains, but abstain-after-cap leaves the planner unconstrained — it picks the same locus by default because `BlockerSubgoalInjector` re-injects a BUILD_FIX subgoal naming the same file (T2-CY does the path naming, but the path *is* always the file that referenced the missing symbol, never the missing symbol's own file).
  - **Fix:** Three-part change.
    - **A — Track rollback streaks per `(errorKind, primaryRolledBackPath)` key.** New `core/build/RollbackStreakTracker` (Spring bean, lives on `ContextLineageTracker` like other build state). Increments on every rollback; resets when a cycle ends with a different `(errorKind, path)` or with a successful merge. Streak ≥2 fires `BUILD_FIX_LOCUS_STUCK` signal.
    - **B — When `BUILD_FIX_LOCUS_STUCK` fires, `BlockerSubgoalInjector` produces a divergence subgoal instead of a same-locus retry.** For `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL` specifically: subgoal description becomes `Create the missing symbol <Name> at <expected-path> OR remove the reference from <rolled-back-path>` with criteria `[either] (a) <expected-path> exists and defines <Name>, or (b) <Name> no longer appears as a free identifier in <rolled-back-path>`. For other error kinds, fall back to a generic divergence prompt that names the streak count and forbids the same locus.
    - **C — Planner protocol contract gains a `BUILD_FIX_DIVERGENCE_REQUIRED` rule** that fires when `BUILD_FIX_LOCUS_STUCK` is in context: "The build has failed N consecutive times on the same file with the same error. Do NOT propose another subgoal that edits this file. Either propose creating the missing artifact named in the error, or propose reverting the change that introduced the bad reference."
  - **Why T2, not T1:** the rollback machinery (T1-DW) is doing its job — no bad state persists. The failure is purely steering: REPETITION_DETECTED=3.0 and DIVERGENCE_REQUEST=3.0 were already in the subgoal's signal set, but the planner had no mechanism to act on them. "Signals don't flow where they should" is the textbook T2 description.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. After two consecutive cycles end ROLLED_BACK with identical `(errorKind, primaryRolledBackPath)`, the next `BlockerSubgoalInjector` invocation produces a divergence subgoal whose description does NOT lead with the same file path that just rolled back.
    2. For `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL` specifically, the divergence subgoal's acceptance criteria explicitly offer two paths: create the missing artifact, or remove the dangling reference.
    3. Streak resets when a cycle merges successfully OR when the next rollback has a different `(errorKind, path)` tuple. Verified by a regression test that alternates rollback files and confirms no spurious divergence trigger.
    4. Behaviour event `build_fix_locus_stuck` emitted on signal fire, payload `{streak: N, errorKind, path}`, so this is observable in `behaviour-*.ndjson`.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Two different rollback files in alternating cycles** (A → B → A → B). Streak tracks `(errorKind, path)` tuple, so A and B each reach streak=1 at most before resetting. Correct: this is not the stuck pattern T2-DD targets.
    - **Same file but different `errorKind`** (e.g. `CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL` → `INCOMPATIBLE_TYPES`). Different tuple → streak resets. Correct: progress was made on the previous error, even if a new one surfaced.
    - **Streak hits the cap, divergence subgoal injected, but planner ignores the divergence rule and re-proposes same-file edit.** The `BUILD_FIX_DIVERGENCE_REQUIRED` rule alone is advisory; pair with `MergeRollbackService` rejection at injection time — if the next mutation's primary file matches the locked file path, refuse to merge with `LOCUS_STUCK_REJECT` and let the loop force the planner to try again.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Divergence subgoal proposes "create `BookAlreadyLoanedException.java`" when the right answer was "remove the reference"** (or vice versa). Mitigation: the divergence subgoal offers BOTH options as alternative acceptance criteria; the LLM picks. If wrong direction is picked first, T2-DD still fires next cycle to flip.
    - **Streak threshold of 2 is too aggressive — punishes a planner that genuinely needed two cycles to get a fix right.** Mitigation: thresholds configurable; default 2 because the current observed pathology is so cheap to detect (every rollback is identical down to the byte). Raise to 3 if false positives surface.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DC (grounding kills the hallucination upstream so this divergence rarely fires; T2-DD catches the case where grounding misses).
  - **Depends on:** T1-DW (rollback machinery), T1-DZ (rollback evidence wiring), T2-CY (BUILD_FIX subgoal path naming — T2-DD reuses the path field this populates).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-08 after the 2026-05-07 23:51 run analysis. Estimated ~1.5 days: ~80 LOC for `RollbackStreakTracker`, ~120 LOC `BlockerSubgoalInjector` divergence path, ~30 LOC planner protocol rule, ~8 tests covering streak math, divergence subgoal shape, and the alternating-file edge case.

- [x] **T2-DE: Audit T1-DT analysis eligibility under BUILD_BROKEN — analysis must remain executable while build is red** (`BuildBlockGate`, `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.fitnessFor`, `BasicSignals`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-08 20:02 run, behaviour-2026-05-08_20-02-30.ndjson):** Chain H's design intent (T1-DT/T1-DU/T2-CU) was that while the build is red, **analysis remains executable** so it can diagnose the blocker and inject corrective subgoals. The 20:02 run's META LEARNINGS block contradicts this: `mode=NOOP role=analysis reason=CAN_EXECUTE_FALSE count=21`. Analysis was being skipped 21 times despite `ROLE_ANALYSIS=3.00` (capped strength) being the *only* live role-signal across the deadlock window. With analysis blocked, the system has no mechanism to break out of the build-blocked state — every other role is correctly gated by T1-DT, but the one role meant to be the escape hatch is also gated.
  - **Investigation entry points:**
    1. `BuildBlockGate.isEligible("analysis", ctx)` — confirm it returns `true` while `BUILD_BROKEN` is active. Compare the gate's implementation against the original T1-DT design ticket.
    2. `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer` — what `canExecute=false` reason was actually emitted for analysis? `META LEARNINGS` records the count but not the explanation. Add per-role-eligibility behaviour event (or extend `agent_skipped`) to capture the *specific* gate that fired (BuildBlockGate? signal threshold? scope mismatch? already executed?).
    3. `AdaptiveExecutionEngine.fitnessFor("analysis")` — even with the gate passing, fitness may be too low for analysis to be selected. Check whether the analysis agent's signal-strength calculation correctly weights `ROLE_ANALYSIS` against the cycle's other signals when build is broken.
    4. Analysis-specific gates: any role-internal `canExecute()` check on `AnalysisAgent` (e.g. requires non-null subgoal, requires open problems, scope guards) may be the one that's failing — not the global gate.
  - **Hypotheses (in observed-likelihood order):**
    1. **Already-executed guard.** Analysis ran once early in the deadlock window, set `ROLE_ALREADY_EXECUTED` in the cycle, and the meta-deliberation tries to re-invoke it but is blocked. The fact that subsequent cycles still show `ROLE_ANALYSIS=3.00` while analysis is skipped points here. Yet analysis hasn't actually produced anything new — probably because the `AnalysisAgent` returned an empty outcome on its single execution and the system never invokes it again.
    2. **Scope guard.** The active subgoal during the deadlock is a `[BUILD_FIX]` subgoal whose description names a specific source file. Analysis's scope guard may consider analysis "out of scope" relative to a build-fix subgoal. Worth checking `DefaultSubgoalScopeGuard` and `BuildBlockGate` interaction.
    3. **Signal-driven fitness underweighting.** Analysis's priority constant is 1.5 (between planner and evaluation). With BUILD_BROKEN suppressing planner/evaluation (T1-DU), analysis should win — but only if its signal-derived fitness exceeds zero. If `getSupportedSignals()` doesn't include `BUILD_BROKEN` (which it shouldn't, structurally — analysis isn't a build-fix role), the signal contributes nothing to its fitness, and `ROLE_ANALYSIS=3.00` alone may not clear whatever threshold the engine uses.
  - **Fix shape (depends on root cause):**
    - If hypothesis 1: add a re-execution clause for analysis when `BUILD_BROKEN` is active and no progress has been made for N cycles — analysis is the only escape hatch, so it should be allowed to re-run despite `ROLE_ALREADY_EXECUTED`.
    - If hypothesis 2: add an explicit exception in the scope guard for analysis-under-BUILD_FIX-subgoal — analysis examining a build-broken file IS in scope by definition.
    - If hypothesis 3: add `BUILD_BROKEN` to `ANALYSIS_SIGNALS` so the analysis agent's fitness picks it up; or add a separate `BUILD_BLOCKED_ANALYSIS_REQUEST` signal that's emitted when no other role is eligible and `BUILD_BROKEN` persists.
  - **Why T2:** the system runs (no infinite loop yet — though T1-EB now catches that), but it's blind to its own most important escape hatch. Steering quality gap.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. `agent_skipped` (or new behaviour event) records the specific gate that blocked analysis when `canExecute=false` — replaces the current `META LEARNINGS` aggregate count.
    2. In a session where every non-analysis role is gated by `BUILD_BROKEN`, analysis runs at least once per N cycles to attempt diagnosis.
    3. Replaying the 20:02 run's deadlock window with the fix produces non-zero analysis activity in the affected cycles.
  - **Pairs with:** T1-EB (deadlock breaker — triggers when this fix proves insufficient as final safety net), T2-DF (meta role pre-validation — meta should also know analysis is the only viable role and route to it).
  - **Depends on:** None — investigation-first ticket.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-08 after the 20:02 run analysis. Investigation step is mandatory before the fix; root cause is unknown today.

- [x] **T2-DF: Meta-deliberation must pre-validate proposed roles against eligibility — un-park T2-AS Fix 2** (`MetaDeliberationAgent`, `MetaDeliberationResponseParser`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `DeliberationExecutionGate`, `BuildBlockGate`)
  - **Origin (2026-05-08):** un-parks T2-AS Fix 2 from T4-AI's deferred slate. The 20:02 run produced empirical evidence that the parking decision should be revisited.
  - **Observed (2026-05-08 20:02 run):** the LLM's last logged meta-deliberation plan was `[1] EVOLUTION → [2] EVALUATION → [3] PLANNER → [4] EVOLUTION → [5] EVALUATION` while every one of those roles had `canExecute=false`. The engine logged `Meta step 1 skipped: canExecute=false for role=evolution`, `step 2 skipped: ... role=evaluation`, `step 3 skipped: ... role=planner`, then `Meta steps present but none executable → falling back to signal-driven ordering`. This pattern repeated every cycle in the deadlock window — at ~25 cycles, that's ~25 wasted meta-deliberation LLM calls, each producing a 5-step plan that's silently discarded.
  - **Why T2-AS was parked:** T2-AS noted the fix in a parked state because the entire meta mechanism was being redesigned in T4-AI. Two arguments for un-parking now: (a) T4-AI hasn't started and isn't on the immediate roadmap, (b) the parking note explicitly said "Fix 2 (role eligibility guard) is straightforward in isolation" — the only objection was bundling, not viability.
  - **Fix:**
    1. **Pre-LLM constraint injection.** Before the meta-deliberation LLM call in `MetaDeliberationAgent.decide`, compute the set of currently-eligible roles via the same `canExecute` machinery the engine uses (`BuildBlockGate`, `DeliberationExecutionGate`, `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer`). Inject the result as a `--- ELIGIBLE ROLES ---` block in the meta prompt: `"Only these roles can execute this cycle: ANALYSIS. PLANNER, EVOLUTION, EVALUATION, MERGE are blocked by build_blocked."`. The current implementation already has a `buildEligibilityBlock()` helper (T2-AS Fix 1 territory) — extend it to enumerate ineligible roles with their gating reason.
    2. **Post-LLM validation.** In `MetaDeliberationResponseParser` (or the meta-step executor), validate each proposed step's `TargetRole` against the eligible set. Steps targeting ineligible roles are dropped with a soft warning logged at WARN. If the entire plan is dropped, fall through to signal-driven ordering (existing behaviour) without burning the next cycle's meta call.
    3. **Behaviour event.** New `meta_step_filtered { cycleId, step, originalRole, reason }` so the rate of LLM-proposed-but-filtered steps is observable. If the rate is high, the prompt's eligibility-block instruction needs strengthening.
  - **Why T2:** the system runs and produces work in non-deadlock cycles. The defect is wasted LLM credit on every cycle where meta proposes ineligible roles — a steering-quality / cost-of-operation issue, not a correctness defect.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. After the fix, in a deadlock cycle where only ANALYSIS is eligible, the meta-deliberation prompt contains an `--- ELIGIBLE ROLES ---` block listing only ANALYSIS, and the LLM's proposed plan does not contain steps targeting EVOLUTION/EVALUATION/PLANNER/MERGE.
    2. If the LLM ignores the constraint and proposes ineligible roles anyway, the parser drops them with a logged warning before reaching the engine.
    3. `meta_step_filtered` behaviour events appear in `behaviour-*.ndjson` whenever filtering occurs, so the steering-prompt effectiveness is measurable.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **All proposed roles ineligible** (the 20:02 case). Plan is fully dropped; engine falls through to signal-driven ordering (which will also produce nothing, but T1-EB now catches that termination state).
    - **Eligible roles change mid-cycle** (e.g. evolution becomes eligible after analysis runs and clears `BUILD_BROKEN`). T4-AI-4 (re-evaluate eligibility dynamically) is the principled fix; for T2-DF, accept that subsequent steps may stall — they'll be picked up in the next cycle's plan.
    - **LLM emits a step with no `TargetRole`** (malformed). Already handled by existing parser; no change.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Over-constraining the LLM** so it cannot propose recovery actions. Mitigation: the eligibility block is descriptive ("only these roles can execute"), not prescriptive ("only propose these steps") — the LLM can still suggest meta-NOOP / analysis as a strategic pivot, just not propose ineligible execution steps.
    - **Drops legitimate steps when eligibility computation is wrong.** Pair with T2-DE (analysis eligibility audit) — both fixes have to land for the deadlock window to actually recover.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-DE (analysis eligibility audit — the right ELIGIBLE_ROLES block depends on analysis being correctly classified as eligible when build is red), T1-EB (deadlock breaker — final safety net even when meta proposes the right thing).
  - **Depends on:** None hard. T4-AI-1 (typed `MetaStep` model) would make the parser-side filter cleaner, but the current pipe-delimited serialisation is workable.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-08 alongside T2-DE and T1-EB. Together they form a small remediation cluster for the build-blocked deadlock observed in the 20:02 run. Estimated ~80 LOC + ~5 tests. Un-parks T2-AS Fix 2.

- [ ] **T2-DG: Rollback-recurrence escalation — force planner when same subgoal + same error rolls back N≥2 times** (`CycleContextData`, `AutobuilderService`, `BasicSignals`, `PlannerProtocolContract`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-09 04:14 run, cycles 42–58):** The same subgoal (`goal-2189aa0609c5` and successors) caused compile-error rollbacks in cycles 42, 44, 47, 49, 50, 52, 56, 57, 58 — 9 consecutive cycles on the same `ErrorResponse`/`BookController` compile error cluster. Each cycle the meta-deliberation directed evolution to "produce a non-repetitive mutation", evolution re-generated similar code, the LLM scored ACCEPTED (no compile ground truth — see T1-EC), and the rollback repeated. The system had all the information needed to escalate but never acted on it.
  - **Root cause:** No counter for compile-error rollbacks per subgoal. `REPETITION_DETECTED` / `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST` signals (T2-DD) fire on `(errorKind, file)` pairs for BUILD_FIX subgoals only, not for general feature subgoals that repeatedly fail compilation. `RolledBackEvidence` carries `stderrSummary` and `errorKind` forward one cycle — the data is available but nothing acts on it beyond prompting evolution to "be non-repetitive".
  - **Counter design — keyed on `(subgoalId, errorKind)`:**
    - Subgoal-only keying would fire when the error changes between rollbacks (e.g. fixed "symbol not found" but introduced "incompatible types") — that's genuine progress; escalating then would interrupt recovery prematurely.
    - `(subgoalId, errorKind)` keying is precise enough: `BuildResultHeuristic` already classifies rollbacks as COMPILE_ERROR / TIMEOUT / etc., and `errorKind` (from `BuildErrorClassifier`) narrows within COMPILE_ERROR (e.g. MISSING_SYMBOL vs TYPE_MISMATCH). Only escalate when the system is genuinely stuck on the same class of error.
    - `stderrSummary` exact-match would be overly strict — minor line-number variation in the same error would reset the counter, preventing escalation. `errorKind` is the right granularity.
  - **Fix:**
    1. Add `consecutiveCompileRollbackCount` (int), `compileRollbackSubgoalId` (String), and `compileRollbackErrorKind` (String, the `errorKind` from `RolledBackEvidence`) to `CycleContextData`. After a ROLLED_BACK cycle where `rolledBackEvidence.buildResult() == COMPILE_ERROR`: if `currentSubGoalId == compileRollbackSubgoalId && rolledBackEvidence.errorKind().equals(compileRollbackErrorKind)`, increment; otherwise reset to 1 and update both tracking fields. Reset to 0 on any successful merge.
    2. In `AutobuilderService` post-cycle hook: when `consecutiveCompileRollbackCount >= 2`, emit `COMPILE_FIX_NEEDED` (new signal in `BasicSignals`, decay=0.20, strength=2.0) and `REPLAN_REQUESTED`. Forces the planner into the next cycle's meta-step.
    3. In `PlannerProtocolContract`, add a `--- COMPILE ERROR ---` block (populated from `context.lastRollback.stderrSummary()` — always the most recent stderr, regardless of which cycle it came from) when `COMPILE_FIX_NEEDED` is active. The planner sees the exact error message and creates a targeted subgoal (e.g. "Fix generics type error in BookController.java line 53").
    4. After the planner runs under `COMPILE_FIX_NEEDED`, reset `consecutiveCompileRollbackCount` to 0.
  - **Why T2, not T1:** The system terminates via T1-EB or the stagnation breaker — it does not loop forever. But 9 wasted cycles on a detectable repeated error class is a steering failure. Analogous to T2-DD (same pattern for BUILD_FIX subgoals, keyed on `(errorKind, file)`).
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. When the same `(subgoal, errorKind)` combination rolls back in 2 consecutive cycles, `COMPILE_FIX_NEEDED` is emitted and the planner runs next cycle with the most recent stderr in its prompt.
    2. When the error *kind* changes between rollbacks (different `errorKind`), the counter resets — no premature escalation.
    3. The planner produces a narrowly-scoped subgoal targeting the specific error.
    4. A successful merge on any cycle resets the counter.
  - **Depends on:** T4-AN-1 (`buildOutcome`, `RolledBackEvidence.errorKind()` on `CycleContextData`). Check whether `BuildErrorClassifier` already populates `errorKind` on `RolledBackEvidence` — if not, that population must be added as part of this ticket.
  - **Pairs with:** T1-EC (primary fix — probe compile eliminates most rollbacks before they occur; T2-DG is the circuit breaker for the rest), T2-DD (analogous mechanism for BUILD_FIX subgoals).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-09 after the 04:14 run analysis. Chain I safety net. ~60 LOC + ~4 tests.

- [x] **T2-DH: `evolutionRanThisCycle` is overloaded — three distinct meanings collapsed into one boolean** (`CycleContextData`, `EvolutionAgent`, `PatternSignalAnalyzer`, `CyclePersistenceService`, `RuleBasedDeliberationProposer`, `CycleExecutor`, `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer`, `MutationSnapshotService`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-14 22:20 run, `behaviour-2026-05-14_22-20-49.ndjson`, 29 cycles, 1 merge, 5 ACCEPTED, 4 stuck):** Cycles 2, 6, 13, 23 produced ACCEPTED mutations but each one failed merge with `can_execute_false`. All four took the `#NoDiff` early-return path in `EvolutionAgent.runEvolution` (`EvolutionAgent.java:127–172`). That path sets `withEvolutionRanThisCycle(true)` but returns *before* `applyRegularMutations` runs — so no mutation files land in `mutations/<cycleId>/` and no `meta.json` is written. T1-ED's fallback create then wrote a "ghost" `meta.json` claiming `mutationState=ACCEPTED`, but `FileVariantCollector` couldn't find any variant files on disk and silently skipped the cycle. Hotfix patched `MutationSnapshotService.persistMutationState` to require `evolutionOutcome.addedOrChangedFiles` to be non-empty before falling back, but that's a per-reader workaround for the same underlying overload.
  - **Root cause:** The flag conflates three per-cycle states:
    - **(A) Agent executed** — `EvolutionAgent.execute()` ran the LLM (whether the LLM produced changes or `#NoDiff`).
    - **(B) Wrote files** — `applyRegularMutations` succeeded; `mutations/<cycleId>/` is populated and `meta.json` exists.
    - **(C) Fresh diff** — `computeProjectDiff` ran this cycle; `ctx.projectDiff()` reflects this cycle's mutation, not a stale prior one.
    - When the normal path runs all three are true. On any of the four `#NoDiff` early-returns only (A) is true.
  - **Setters (5, all set true):**
    - `EvolutionAgent:136` — `#NoDiff` on active BUILD_FIX subgoal. (A) only.
    - `EvolutionAgent:153` — `#NoDiff` + no output evidence (T1-CS). (A) only.
    - `EvolutionAgent:159` — `#NoDiff` + accepted/null prior status (T2-CC deferred-merge / evolution-noop). (A) only.
    - `EvolutionAgent:168` — `#NoDiff` + prior REJECTED. (A) only.
    - `EvolutionAgent:183` — normal path with `changed=true`. (A), (B), (C) all true.
  - **Readers (6) — what each one actually wants:**
    | File:line | Code | Wants | Behaviour on `#NoDiff` |
    |---|---|---|---|
    | `PatternSignalAnalyzer:56` | empty-diff stagnation guard | (C) | **Wrong** — runs check on stale diff |
    | `CyclePersistenceService:33` | sets `mutationPath` on context | (B) | **Wrong** — sets path to empty folder |
    | `RuleBasedDeliberationProposer:261` | "hasCandidate" for evaluation | (A) | Correct |
    | `CycleExecutor:457` | `changedThisCycle` — gates merge invocation | (B) | **Wrong** — invokes merge with no variant on disk |
    | `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer:166` | "evolution must run before evaluation" guard | (A) | Correct |
    | `MutationSnapshotService:94` | gates fallback meta write (T1-ED) | (B) | Was wrong; T1-ED hotfix #2 patched with `hasMutationContent` |
  - **Options considered:**
    1. **Per-reader patches** (status quo + hotfix #2 style). Smallest delta, but spreads "what I actually meant" logic across the codebase and the next reader added will hit the same trap.
    2. **Narrow the flag to mean (B)** — remove the four `#NoDiff` setters; readers that wanted (A) lean on `lastEvolutionOutcome != null` or the existing `EVOLUTION_NOOP` signal. Matches the flag's original intent per the T5-AZ comment in `CycleExecutor` ("explicit per-cycle flag instead of EVOLUTION_OCCURRED signal" — that comment is about merge gating, which is (B)).
    3. **Three explicit flags** — `evolutionAgentExecutedThisCycle` (A) / `evolutionWroteFilesThisCycle` (B) / `evolutionProducedFreshDiffThisCycle` (C). Most explicit but more touch points.
    4. **Hybrid** — keep one flag for (A), derive (B) from `mutations/<cycleId>/` filesystem check or `evolutionOutcome.addedOrChangedFiles`, add a small flag for (C). Less new state than option 3 but similar touch points.
  - **Recommended approach — Option 2 (narrow to meaning (B)):**
    1. In `EvolutionAgent.runEvolution` (lines 127–172), remove `withEvolutionRanThisCycle(true)` from all four `#NoDiff` early-return paths. Keep only the setter at line 183 (post-`applyRegularMutations`).
    2. Audit the two (A)-readers:
       - `RuleBasedDeliberationProposer:261` — change `|| context.evolutionRanThisCycle()` to `|| context.lastEvolutionOutcome() != null` (covers both noOp and normal paths). Or remove entirely; the `pendingFingerprint` check above already covers normal paths, and noOp cycles should NOT be re-evaluating anyway (the `EVOLUTION_NOOP` path is designed to mark FULFILLED directly via `CycleExecutor.applyCompletion`'s bypass).
       - `ExecutionEligibilityExplainer:166` — change the guard to `if (!ctx.evolutionRanThisCycle() && !ctx.hasSignal(BasicSignals.EVOLUTION_NOOP))`. NoOp cycles are evaluating existing output/, which is a legitimate evaluation case.
    3. Remove the `hasMutationContent` helper from `MutationSnapshotService.persistMutationState` (T1-ED hotfix #2 becomes unnecessary because the flag itself now means (B)).
    4. No change needed in `PatternSignalAnalyzer`, `CyclePersistenceService`, `CycleExecutor.applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation` — their existing usage is correct under meaning (B).
  - **Why T2, not T1:** The system still terminates (no infinite loop); the bug manifests as silent merge skips, not stuck state. The hotfix #2 patch already prevents the cascade. This ticket is the structural cleanup — same severity tier as T2-DA (protocol-contract clarification) or T2-CW (consolidate hardcoded file lists).
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. `evolutionRanThisCycle == true` IMPLIES `mutations/<ctx.cycleName()>/meta.json` exists on disk (when evolution wasn't interrupted by IOException).
    2. A `#NoDiff` cycle reports `evolutionRanThisCycle == false` and `hasSignal(EVOLUTION_NOOP) == true`.
    3. Evaluation still runs on `#NoDiff` cycles where output/ has evidence (eligibility test).
    4. `MutationSnapshotService.persistMutationState` no longer needs `hasMutationContent` — remove the helper and its dedicated test; the cycle-2 fallback test still passes with the flag itself acting as the gate.
    5. The `T2-DH-NoOpDoesNotSetFlag` regression test asserts: after `runEvolution` returns from any of the four `#NoDiff` branches, `ctx.evolutionRanThisCycle() == false`.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **`#NoDiff` + BUILD_FIX subgoal (line 134–139):** also emits `REPETITION_DETECTED` / `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST`. Those signals carry the "evolution agent did engage" signal forward; no caller needs the flag for this path.
    - **`#NoDiff` + no output evidence (line 150–154):** emits `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST`. Same — signal carries the meaning.
    - **`#NoDiff` + deferred-merge / evolution-noop (line 155–162):** emits `EVOLUTION_NOOP`. The eligibility explainer change in step 2 covers this case explicitly.
    - **`#NoDiff` + prior REJECTED (line 163–171):** emits `REPETITION_DETECTED` / `DIVERGENCE_REQUEST`. Same as BUILD_FIX path.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Hidden (A)-reader I missed.** Mitigation: full grep before merge; the audit table above lists 6 readers and each one is justified. Adding a `T2-DH-flag-semantics-pinned` invariant test that constructs a `#NoDiff` context and asserts the flag stays false catches regressions.
    - **Downstream code expects `mutationPath` to be set after evolution.** Per the `CyclePersistenceService` reader audit, `mutationPath` is only set when `evolutionRanThisCycle == true`. After Option 2, noOp cycles won't set `mutationPath` — but they also don't write a mutation folder, so nothing should be reading it. Spot-check `SmsCycleIngestor` and any other `mutationPath` reader for safety.
  - **Depends on:** None. Cleanup of the existing flag.
  - **Pairs with:** T1-ED (made the bug visible by surfacing meta-persist failures); subgoal-runaway tickets (T2-DD, T2-DG) — orthogonal but reduce the frequency of noOp cycles in practice.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-14 after analysis of 22:20 run revealed the structural overload. Estimated ~30 LOC + 2 tests; small ticket, mechanical change. Single PR. The T1-ED `hasMutationContent` patch in `MutationSnapshotService` can be reverted as part of this ticket since the flag will carry the same guarantee.

- [x] **T2-DI: Dataflow store coherence — flag entities with parallel stores across classes** (extension of `analysis/structure/OutputStructureAnalyzer`, new `analysis/heuristic/StoreCoherenceHeuristic`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-18 20:35:35 run):** Output compiles cleanly, all 17 files exist, all references resolve. Yet `BookController.java:16` and `BookService.java:13` both declare `private static final List<Book> books = new ArrayList<>();`. POST /books writes to the controller's list; `LoanService.createLoan()` calls `bookService.getBookById(bookId)` which reads from the service's empty list. Every POST /loans returns 404 in practice. The system fulfilled all 8 subgoals as COMPLETED. The bug is statically obvious to a careful reader, but invisible to every existing heuristic: T2-CM detects orphans/dangling refs/passive-only types (none of those apply — both classes are used); T1-EC's compile gate sees a clean build; the LLM evaluator never compares the two classes against each other.
  - **Root cause:** Existing structural-awareness heuristics (T2-CM family) check **file-level** integrity. None check **dataflow-level** coherence — whether the structural pieces form a working dataflow when read together. Two parallel stores for the same entity is a coherence bug invisible at the file level.
  - **Why T2:** structural awareness gap, not a state-machine bug. Slots into the existing `OutputStructureAnalyzer` family alongside T2-CM/T2-CN/T2-CO.
  - **Fix — primary (`StoreCoherenceHeuristic`):** Extend `OutputStructureAnalyzer` with `parallelStores(output)`:
    1. Enumerate **entity types** — PascalCase types declared in output with ≥1 mutable instance field or setter (the POJO pattern). Filter out controllers/services/exceptions by suffix.
    2. For each entity type E, enumerate **store fields** — instance/static fields whose declared type is a collection of E: `List<E>`, `Map<?, E>`, `Set<E>`, `Collection<E>`, `Queue<E>`, `E[]`. Use the existing `JavaStructureParser` AST (regex fallback per T5-BC) to resolve generics.
    3. Group by entity type. Any entity with stores in ≥2 distinct enclosing types is flagged: `ParallelStores(entity=Book, owners=[BookController, BookService], locations=[BookController.java:16, BookService.java:13])`.
    4. **Penalty (–0.10 to suggestedScore, HIGH confidence):** if `parallelStores(currentOutput)` is non-empty AND the diff *introduces* the violation (newly-added store for an entity that already has one elsewhere), opinion=REJECTED with rationale `"parallel store for entity 'Book' — declared in BookController.java:16 and BookService.java:13; writes to one are invisible to the other"`.
    5. **Reward (+0.05 to suggestedScore, MEDIUM confidence):** if the diff *resolves* an existing parallel-store violation (removes a store, replaces a local store with a field reference to another class's store), opinion=ACCEPTED with rationale `"resolves parallel-store violation for entity 'Book'"`.
  - **Fix — secondary (surface in evolution prompt — see T3-BS):** Add a "Parallel stores" section to the `--- STRUCTURAL HOLES ---` block when non-empty, enumerating each violation with locations. Lets the LLM target the fix directly at generation time.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. The 20:35:35 Book parallel-store scenario fires `StoreCoherenceHeuristic` with REJECTED opinion.
    2. A diff that removes `BookController.books` and routes through `BookService` fires `StoreCoherenceHeuristic` with ACCEPTED opinion.
    3. False positives on legitimate "two stores intentional" patterns (e.g. a cache + primary store) are accepted as a known limitation in v1.
    4. Detection is framework-neutral.
  - **Depends on:** Nothing structurally. Reuses `OutputStructureAnalyzer` cache from T2-CM if/when both ship.
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CM (different layer of structural awareness — file-level vs dataflow-level); T2-CP (per-cycle structural audit driver — natural place to bundle coherence findings alongside T2-CM/CN/CO outputs); T4-AV (LLM coherence reviewer — deferred placeholder for patterns this static scan misses).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-18 after the 20:35:35 fulfilled-but-broken run made the gap visible. Initial scope: parallel-store detection only. If a second coherence pattern surfaces in production that this scan doesn't catch, un-park T4-AV.

- [x] **T2-DJ: Meta-step `executedRoles` poisoning — blocked steps pollute the signal-driven execution guard** (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine.executeMetaSteps`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-18 22:59:57 run, 16 cycles, 1 merge):** Deliberation returned `mode=NOOP` with evolution (and sometimes planner) as proposed meta steps in every cycle after the first. `executeMetaSteps` added the role to `executedRoles` (line 464) _before_ checking `canExecute`. When `canExecute=false` blocked the step, the role was already in `executedRoles`, so the signal-driven phase skipped it with `already_executed_this_cycle`. Evolution ran exactly once (cycle 1 bootstrap merge) and was then unreachable for all 15 subsequent cycles. With evolution blocked, evaluation re-probed the same compile-failing mutation 13× (12 "no POM" + 1 real type error). Zero additional merges.
  - **Root cause:** Line 464 `executedRoles.add(step.targetRole())` fires unconditionally at the top of the meta-step loop. Subsequent gates (`deliberationGate.allows`, `canExecute`) continue without removing the role. The signal-driven phase's identical `executedRoles.add(role)` guard at line 172 then returns false and emits `already_executed_this_cycle`.
  - **Fix:** Move `executedRoles.add(role)` to just before `agent.execute()` — only a role that _actually ran_ should block the signal-driven phase. Changed `!executedRoles.add(step.targetRole())` guard to `executedRoles.contains(step.targetRole())` so the check still works without the side effect.
  - **Shipped 2026-05-18.** 1067/1067 green. Companion finding to same session's `no POM` probe failures — the two together explain the 0-progress loop.

- [ ] **T2-DK: Rename `DeliberationMode.NOOP` → `DeliberationMode.UNDIRECTED`** (pure rename across production + test code)
  - **Motivation:** `NOOP` is a computing term meaning "do nothing", which actively misleads readers into thinking the mode suppresses execution or LLM calls. The actual semantics are "no specific directed mode — all roles remain eligible, signal-driven fitness pass steers execution." `UNDIRECTED` names that intent precisely. Confirmed by audit 2026-05-19.
  - **Scope:** Mechanical find-and-replace of `DeliberationMode.NOOP` → `DeliberationMode.UNDIRECTED` plus:
    - `DeliberationMode.java` — rename enum constant
    - All production `.java` files referencing `DeliberationMode.NOOP` (~15 sites across `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `DeliberationExecutionGate`, `DeliberationService`, `DefaultDeliberationValidator`, `RuleBasedDeliberationProposer`, `MetaDeliberationResponseParser`, `MetaDeliberationAgent`, `DeliberationResult`)
    - All test `.java` files (~25 sites across `AdaptiveExecutionEngineOrderingTest`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngineEvolutionIntegrationTest`, `DeliberationServiceTest`, `DeliberationServiceMetaControlContractTest`, `RuleBasedDeliberationProposerTest`, `MetaDeliberationAgentTest`, `DeliberationExecutionGateTest`)
    - Inline string literals `"NOOP"` used as mode name (e.g. `mode.name()` fallback strings in `AdaptiveExecutionEngine:644`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine:662`, `DeliberationService:74`) — these auto-update via `Enum.name()` but any hardcoded string fallbacks need a manual sweep
    - Comments referencing "NOOP" in the deliberation sense (not the interpretation-protocol sense — see below)
  - **Out of scope:** The interpretation protocol uses `Status: NOOP` / `Status: APPLIED` as a completely separate LLM response token in `InterpretationProtocolContract` and `InterpretationOutcome`. That NOOP is not a `DeliberationMode` and should NOT be renamed.
  - **Why T2:** naming clarity directly reduces misread risk when reasoning about deliberation flow. The T2-DJ post-mortem would have been faster if the mode were named `UNDIRECTED`.
  - **Effort:** ~45 min. No logic changes — pure rename + test green check.
  - **Depends on:** None.

---

## Tier 3 — Feature completion

- [x] **T3-AW: Planner-generated acceptance criteria per subgoal** (`PlannerProtocolContract`, `PlannerResponseParser`, `PlannerOutcome`, `PlannerAgent`, `GoalEntity`, `GoalPlannerService`, `Neo4jGoalGraphService`, `EvaluationProtocolContract`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Motivation:** Local LLMs score mutations overconfidently (0.82+) without checking whether the implementation actually meets the subgoal's stated requirements. OpenAI does this implicitly; weaker models need an explicit checklist.
  - **Approach:** The planner generates 1–4 short "done when" criteria alongside each subgoal at planning time. Stored with the subgoal (via `GoalEntity.acceptanceCriteria`, persisted to Neo4j via the existing `metadata` map under key `"acceptanceCriteria"`). Hydrated into `CycleContextData.currentSubGoalCriteria` at context-refresh time and rendered as a `--- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ---` block in the evaluation prompt.
  - **Key changes:**
    - `PlannerProtocolContract`: add `ACCEPTANCE_CRITERIA` rule — optional `Criteria:` sub-block (1–4 `*`-prefixed lines) under each `#SubGoalAppend` entry.
    - `PlannerResponseParser`: parse `Criteria:` sub-blocks into `Map<String, List<String>> goalCriteria`; pass into `PlannerOutcome`.
    - `PlannerAgent.injectOutcomeGoals()` and `planInto()`: call `goalPlannerService.storeAcceptanceCriteria(id, criteria)` after each `addSubGoal` call.
    - `GoalPlannerService`: add `storeAcceptanceCriteria(goalId, criteria)` and `getAcceptanceCriteria(goalId)`.
    - `GoalEntity`: add `List<String> acceptanceCriteria` field.
    - `Neo4jGoalGraphService.mapRowToGoalEntity()`: populate `acceptanceCriteria` from metadata on load.
    - `CycleContextData`: add `List<String> currentSubGoalCriteria`; populated in `PlannerAgent.refreshGoalState()`.
    - `EvaluationProtocolContract.getUserPrompt()`: add `--- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ---` block.
  - **Relationship to T3-B:** T3-B's `expectedArtifacts` is the programmatic/heuristic counterpart (file existence assertions). Acceptance criteria are the LLM-verifiable counterpart (observable code properties). Both can coexist.
  - **Backward compatibility:** Goals without criteria get empty criteria; the evaluation block renders `(none defined)`.

- [x] **T3-AX: Full file content in evaluation prompt** (`EvaluationProtocolContract`)
  - **Motivation:** The evaluation prompt currently shows only changed file *names* (`diff.changedFiles().keySet()`). The evaluator has no access to the actual code and must reason from a diff summary alone.
  - **Fix:** Add a `--- CURRENT FILE CONTENT ---` section to `EvaluationProtocolContract.getUserPrompt()` that renders the actual content of each changed file from `context.projectDiff().changedFiles()`. Truncate per-file content at a configurable limit (default: 150 lines or 4000 chars, whichever is shorter). Add `evaluation.fileContentMaxLines` (default 150) to `application.yml`.
  - **Note:** Pairs naturally with T3-AW — the evaluator can check each acceptance criterion against the actual code rather than inferring from a diff summary.
  - **Future:** Consider method-level truncation instead of raw line-based truncation — extract each method's signature + body separately (using JavaParser) so the evaluator sees semantically meaningful units rather than an arbitrary top-N lines. Falls back to raw for non-Java files. Significantly more complex.

- [x] **T3-AY: Make planner acceptance criteria mandatory** (`PlannerProtocolContract`, `PlannerAgent`, `EvaluationProtocolContract`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-07):** Evaluation prompt shows `ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA: (none defined)` — the evaluator operates without a checklist. T3-AW made `Criteria:` optional for backward compatibility; in practice the planner omits them routinely when not required.
  - **Fix:**
    1. `PlannerProtocolContract`: promote `Criteria:` from optional to required. Rule: "Every `#SubGoalAppend` entry must include a `Criteria:` sub-block with 1–4 `*`-prefixed done-when conditions. If no observable output criterion applies, write `* (subgoal is structural — no observable output criterion)`."
    2. `EvaluationProtocolContract`: replace the silent `(none defined)` render with a visible warning line: `"⚠ No acceptance criteria were defined for this subgoal. Evaluate against subgoal description only."` Makes the gap observable in logs.
    3. `PlannerAgent`: log a WARNING whenever a goal is injected with zero criteria (metric-trackable, no logic change).
  - **Relationship to T3-AW:** T3-AW built the infrastructure; T3-AY enforces its use.
  - **Observed (2026-04-08 session):** Rule took effect immediately — all 5 injected subgoals had criteria populated; all evaluation prompts showed real criteria. However the evaluator still produces vague per-criterion prose rather than structured assessments, and the fulfillment heuristic is blind to unmet criteria → see T3-AZ and T3-BA.

- [x] **T3-AZ: Per-criterion pass/fail assessment in evaluation response** (`EvaluationProtocolContract`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08):** The evaluator reads the `--- ACCEPTANCE CRITERIA ---` block but produces a single vague message sentence rather than checking each criterion individually. One run flagged an unmet criterion in prose ("no changes were made to include previously created books") but gave 0.75/ACCEPTED/PROGRESSING with no machine-readable signal. You cannot tell from the log which criteria passed and which failed.
  - **Fix:** Add a `CRITERIA_ASSESSMENT` rule to `EvaluationProtocolContract` requiring a `CriteriaCheck:` block in the response when criteria are present. Format: `[N] ✓ <criterion>` or `[N] ✗ <criterion> — <why not met>`. Insert `CriteriaCheck:` into the response format template (between `Score:` and `Message:`). Update `isValidResponse` to not require it (criteria may be absent — don't gate on it).
  - **Depends on:** T3-AY (criteria must be populated for this to fire).
  - **Enables:** T3-BA (the heuristic veto needs a machine-readable unmet signal).
  - **Done (2026-04-08):** `CRITERIA_ASSESSMENT` rule added; `CriteriaCheck:` block in response format; `EvaluationResponseParser.parseCriteriaAssessments()` extracts `[N] ✓/✗` lines; `ParsedEvaluation` wrapper carries them from adapter to agent; `CycleContextData.criteriaAssessments` field added.
  - **Live session finding (2026-04-08):** LLM partial compliance observed — in one evaluation with 2 criteria, the LLM assessed only criterion 1 (`[1] ✓`) and silently dropped criterion 2 despite acknowledging its failure in prose. The `CriteriaCheck:` format rule alone is insufficient; the LLM treats omission as acceptable. → T3-BC addresses this with a missing-criterion safety fallback.

- [x] **T3-BA: `SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic` vetoes FULFILLED when a criterion is unmet** (`SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic`, `CycleExecutor` or `CycleContextData`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08):** A subgoal (`GET /books`) was closed as `FULFILLED` with `rationale=score-based closure + meaningful diff` even though the evaluator had explicitly flagged criterion 2 ("Includes all previously created books") as unmet. The fulfillment path (`SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic` → `CycleExecutor`) never consults `currentSubGoalCriteria` or the evaluator's criterion assessments.
  - **Fix:** Parse `CriteriaCheck:` lines from the evaluator response (stored on `CycleContextData` as `criteriaAssessments`). In `SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic.decide()`: if any assessment is `✗`, return PROGRESSING (suppress FULFILLED). If all assessments are `✓` and score threshold is met, allow FULFILLED.
  - **Depends on:** T3-AZ (needs machine-readable `[N] ✗` markers).
  - **Done (2026-04-08):** `hasUnmetCriterion()` check inserted before both the `interpreter=FULFILLED` and score-based closure paths; 4 new tests added.
  - **Live session finding (2026-04-08):** Veto did not fire in either evaluation cycle. Two compounding causes: (1) LLM dropped criterion 2 entirely from `CriteriaCheck:` (no `[2] ✗` emitted) so `criteriaAssessments` contained no `✗` lines — the veto had nothing to act on; (2) criteria for subgoal 1 (POST /books) never reached the evaluator at all due to a hydration timing issue (see T3-BC and the investigation note below). The GET /books subgoal was incorrectly marked `GOAL_FULFILLED` despite a broken implementation (`BookResource.books` disconnected from `BookController.books`, always returning empty).
  - **Pattern note — chain-dependency fragility:** T3-AW → T3-AY → T3-AZ → T3-BA is a chain where each link assumes the previous delivered correctly. Both live breaks were silent: no log line indicated that criteria were absent from the prompt or that the `CriteriaCheck:` block was incomplete. Future chain-dependent features should include a per-link observability log (e.g. `"Criteria delivered to evaluator: N"`, `"CriteriaCheck lines parsed: M of N"`) so breaks are visible immediately rather than requiring post-hoc log analysis.

- [x] **T3-BC: Missing-criterion safety fallback — treat unassessed criteria as unmet** (`SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic`, `EvaluationResponseParser` or `EvaluationAgent`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08):** LLM partial compliance with T3-AZ: the evaluator produced `[1] ✓` for 2 criteria and silently omitted `[2]`. Because no `[N] ✗` line was emitted, T3-BA's veto never fired and the subgoal closed FULFILLED despite criterion 2 being materially unmet.
  - **Fix:** In `SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic.decide()` (or at parse time in `EvaluationAgent`), compare `currentSubGoalCriteria.size()` against the number of indexed lines in `criteriaAssessments`. If assessed count < defined count, treat the gap as unmet — i.e. suppress FULFILLED as if a `✗` line were present. This makes T3-BA robust regardless of LLM compliance level.
  - **Implementation note:** The index set of assessed criteria can be extracted from `criteriaAssessments` by parsing the `[N]` prefix. Any index N in `[1..criteriaCount]` absent from that set is an implicit fail. Alternatively, a simpler check: if `criteriaAssessments.size() < currentSubGoalCriteria.size()`, veto.
  - **Depends on:** T3-AZ (source of assessments), T3-BA (veto path to hook into).
  - **Done (2026-04-08):** `hasUnmetCriterion()` count-comparison added; `EvaluationAgent` logs WARN on mismatch.
  - **Additional fix (2026-04-08):** `EvaluationProtocolContract.buildCriteriaCheckFormat()` replaces the static `[1]`/`[2]` placeholder in the response format template with a dynamically generated fill-in-the-blank list pre-populated with each actual criterion text. This anchors the model to emit exactly N lines rather than producing from scratch. `CycleExecutor.applyCompletion()` now logs the `SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic` rationale when fulfillment is denied (previously only "Completion signal not met" was visible). Criteria storage logs elevated from DEBUG to INFO in `PlannerAgent` and `AnalysisAgent`.
  - **Live session verification (2026-04-08 23:36 run):** Veto confirmed firing correctly — score ≥ 0.75 cycles with 1/4 criteria assessed produced `promoted=false` as expected. Dynamic format template not yet validated end-to-end (applied after that session).

- [x] **T3-BD: Investigate criteria hydration timing in bootstrap cycle** (`CycleContextCoordinator` / `PlannerAgent` / `GoalPlannerService`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08):** Criteria defined by the planner for subgoal 1 (POST /books) were present in the planner's `#SubGoalAppend` output but absent from the evaluation prompt (`"No acceptance criteria were defined for this subgoal."`). The planner and evaluator ran in the same cycle (bootstrap), suggesting that criteria are written to `GoalEntity` after the evaluation context is already built for that cycle.
  - **Investigation:** Check the execution order in `CycleContextCoordinator.refreshGoalState()` (or equivalent) relative to `PlannerAgent.injectOutcomeGoals()`. If criteria are written by the planner mid-cycle and the context snapshot was taken before the planner ran, the evaluator will always see stale (empty) criteria for any subgoal created in the same cycle it is evaluated.
  - **Fix:** `PlannerAgent.refreshGoalState()` reads `acceptanceCriteria` from current subgoal metadata and calls `withCurrentSubGoalCriteria()` after every planner run — so the evaluator in the same cycle picks up freshly injected criteria rather than the stale empty list from cycle start.
  - **Done (2026-04-08):** Fix confirmed working in live session (23:36 run) — evaluator received 4 criteria for the POST /books subgoal injected in the same bootstrap cycle.
  - **Depends on:** T3-AW (criteria infrastructure), T3-AZ/T3-BA (the feature being protected).

- [x] **T3-BE: Analysis-injected subgoals have no acceptance criteria** (`AnalysisAgent`, `AnalysisResponseParser`, `AnalysisProtocolContract`, `AnalysisOutcome`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08):** `AnalysisAgent.autoInjectExecutableSubGoals()` called `goalPlannerService.addSubGoal()` but never `setAcceptanceCriteria()`. Subgoals injected by analysis (e.g. "Implement validation for the title field…") always had 0 criteria, causing `EvaluationAgent` to warn "hydration may have failed" every cycle for those goals — even though criteria were never attached in the first place.
  - **Fix (2026-04-08):** Added optional `Criteria:` / `* criterion` sub-blocks to `AnalysisProtocolContract` response format (with a MUST requirement). Added `goalCriteria: Map<String, List<String>>` to `AnalysisOutcome`. Updated `AnalysisResponseParser` to parse criteria sub-blocks under `ProposedExecutableSubGoals`. `AnalysisAgent` now calls `setAcceptanceCriteria()` after `addSubGoal()` and logs WARN when criteria are absent.
  - **Done (2026-04-08):** 4 new parser tests, full compile green.

- [x] **T3-BF: `EvaluationResponseParser` misses criterion on inline `CriteriaCheck:` header line** (`EvaluationResponseParser`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08):** LLM wrote `CriteriaCheck: [1] ✓ criterion text` (header + first criterion on same line). `CRITERIA_LINE_PATTERN` anchors to `^`, so after trimming the line starts with `CriteriaCheck:`, not `[`, and the match fails. System logged "0 of 2 expected (complete LLM omission)" — but 1 criterion was actually present.
  - **Fix (2026-04-08):** In `parseCriteriaAssessments()`, added `flatMap` step: lines starting with `CriteriaCheck:` are replaced by their suffix (stripped of the header prefix) before pattern filtering. Empty suffix emits nothing; non-empty suffix is evaluated normally. 3 new tests added.
  - **Done (2026-04-08):** All tests green.

- [x] **T3-BG: Enrich planner prompt with subgoal health and project state** (`PlannerProtocolContract`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08 23:52 run):** When a subgoal is stagnating and REPLAN fires, the planner is repeatedly invoked but generates proposals that are (a) redundant with work already attempted and (b) blocked by the backlog guard. The planner's prompt contains only goal text, evaluation feedback summary, and interpretation context — it has no visibility into how many cycles the current subgoal has been stagnating, which acceptance criteria passed vs. failed, what files currently exist in the project, or persistent heuristic warnings. As a result, each planner invocation starts essentially blind to the actual problem.
  - **Observed failure pattern:** In the 23:52 run, a single broad subgoal ("Establish a Spring Boot application...") stagnated for 4 cycles. The planner was invoked 3 times during REPLAN — each time generating reasonable but context-free proposals that were immediately blocked by "skipping injection — 1 active subgoals already in backlog." All three proposals were discarded. The subgoal eventually exhausted and the session terminated.
  - **Mechanical note:** `PlannerAgent.processRemovals()` runs BEFORE the backlog guard. So if the planner proposes `#SubGoalRemove <stuck-goal-id>` + `#SubGoalAppend <focused replacements>`, the removal empties the backlog before the injection check — the decompose pattern already works mechanically. The planner just needs to know it should use it and when.
  - **Fix:** Add three new sections to `PlannerProtocolContract.getUserPrompt()` (all data already available on `CycleContextData`):
    1. `--- CURRENT SUBGOAL STATUS ---`: stagnating cycle count (`cumulativeStagnatingCycles`), last score + delta, criteria with pass/fail from last evaluation (`currentSubGoalCriteria` × `criteriaAssessments`), persistent heuristic warnings (`persistentHeuristicWarnings`). Shown only when a current subgoal exists.
    2. Decompose coaching note (shown only when `cumulativeStagnatingCycles >= 3`): "⚠ This subgoal has been stagnating for N cycles. Consider decomposing: use `#SubGoalRemove <id>` to remove it, then `#SubGoalAppend` with narrower, file-level replacements. Removal is processed before injection — the backlog will be empty in time for the new subgoals."
    3. `--- PROJECT STRUCTURE ---`: `ctx.projectSummary()` truncated to ~30 lines, so the planner can anchor proposed subgoals to concrete existing file paths.
  - **No logic change outside the prompt.** All data is on `CycleContextData`; the coaching note makes the existing `#SubGoalRemove + #SubGoalAppend` decompose pattern discoverable by the LLM.
  - **Expected outcome:** When a subgoal stagnates past the threshold, the planner proposes `#SubGoalRemove` + focused `#SubGoalAppend` rather than proposals that get blocked. The stuck subgoal is given up voluntarily and replaced with a more granular plan before exhaustion forces termination.
  - **Depends on:** T3-AY (criteria populated), T3-BC (unmet criteria surfaced on context).

- [x] **T3-BH: Plan rationale and sibling coordination context** (`PlannerProtocolContract`, `PlannerResponseParser`, `PlannerOutcome`, `GoalEntity.metadata`, `EvolutionProtocolContract`, `EvaluationProtocolContract`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-08 runs):** When the planner proposes N subgoals as a batch, they share no common rationale. Each subgoal is a standalone description with standalone criteria. Evolution working on subgoal K doesn't know subgoals K+1…N exist — it may implement their scope. Evaluation working on subgoal K penalizes for missing everything that K+1…N are supposed to cover, creating excessive score pressure and forcing each subgoal to over-implement. Concretely: the first plan injected a single broad subgoal covering the entire API, because there was no mechanism to signal that a batch of smaller, ordered, coordinated subgoals is preferred.
  - **Root cause:** The planner response format has no way to express coordination intent across a batch of proposed subgoals. The agents that execute each subgoal have no visibility into the plan's overall structure.
  - **Fix:**
    1. **Planner emits `PlanRationale:`** — a 1–2 sentence field (required when ≥ 2 subgoals are proposed) describing what the batch collectively achieves and how the subgoals relate to each other. Example: "Subgoals 1–3 implement Books, Members, and Loans as separate self-contained resource groups in that order. Each subgoal should be evaluated only for its own resource group — do not penalise for absent sibling groups."
    2. **`PlannerResponseParser`** parses `PlanRationale:` → new field on `PlannerOutcome`.
    3. **`PlannerAgent.injectOutcomeGoals()` / `planInto()`** stores the rationale in parent `GoalEntity.metadata["planRationale"]` when subgoals are injected.
    4. **Evolution prompt** gains `--- PLAN CONTEXT ---` block: plan rationale (from parent metadata) + list of completed siblings (same `parentId`, `COMPLETED` status) + list of upcoming siblings (same `parentId`, `ACTIVE` status, excluding current). Instruction: "Focus ONLY on what this subgoal requires. Do not implement functionality covered by upcoming siblings."
    5. **Evaluation prompt** gains the same `--- PLAN CONTEXT ---` block with an explicit scoring note: "Do not penalise for absent functionality that will be handled by upcoming sibling subgoals (listed above)."
  - **Sibling data source:** `goalPlannerService.getAllGoals()` filtered by `parentId == current.parentId`. Split into completed vs. remaining (excluding current). Already available via `GoalPlannerService`.
  - **PlanRationale in prompt contract rule:** Add a `PLAN_RATIONALE` rule to `PlannerProtocolContract` (alongside the existing `Criteria:` requirement): "When proposing 2 or more subgoals, include `PlanRationale:` explaining how they collectively advance the root goal and what each covers. This rationale will be shown to the agents executing each subgoal."
  - **Depends on:** T3-BG (produces better-scoped subgoal batches for the rationale to coordinate).
  - **Note:** Sibling filtering by `parentId` requires that subgoals produced by the planner in the same batch share a common parent. This is already the case — `injectOutcomeGoals()` passes `context.currentSubGoalId()` as `parentGoalId` for all subgoals in a batch.

- [x] **T3-BB: Persist per-evaluation `CriterionResult` nodes to Neo4j** (`EvaluationActionRegistrar`, `EvaluationGraphFacade`)
  - **Problem:** `criteriaAssessments` from T3-AZ are carried on `CycleContextData` for the current cycle only; they are not persisted and are invisible to the orchestration snapshot, retrospective analysis, and any heuristic wanting to trend per-criterion failure rate across cycles.
  - **Fix:** In `EvaluationActionRegistrar.registerEvaluation()`, write each parsed `[N] ✓/✗` line as a `CriterionResult` node linked from the evaluation action node:
    ```
    (:EvaluationAction)-[:HAS_CRITERION]->(:CriterionResult {
      index: int, text: String, passed: Boolean,
      reason: String,          // ✗ failure reason if present
      cycleId: String, subgoalId: String
    })
    ```
    This schema enables per-criterion queries: "how many times did criterion 2 fail for this subgoal?" and "which criteria have never passed?".
  - **Distinct from T3-BI** (which persists *cumulative* pass status — the rolled-up view per subgoal). T3-BB is the raw event log; T3-BI is the derived summary.
  - **Depends on:** T3-AZ (source of the assessments). T3-BI and T3-BJ can read from this event log.

- [x] **T3-BI: Persist cumulative criteria status to `GoalEntity` / Neo4j** (`GoalPlannerService`, `Neo4jGoalGraphService`, `CycleContextCoordinator`, `GoalEntity`)
  - **Problem:** T1-BG's `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` lives entirely in `CycleContextData` and is lost on restart. If the system restarts mid-subgoal, all accumulated criterion passes are forgotten and the subgoal must re-earn them from scratch — cycling criteria that had already been satisfied.
  - **Fix:** Mirror the `mergeCount` pattern (T2-BI hotfix): store cumulative pass status in `GoalEntity.metadata` under key `"criteriaPassStatus"` as a JSON-serialised `Map<Integer, Boolean>`. Three integration points:
    1. **Write:** `GoalPlannerService.markCriterionPassed(goalId, criterionIndex)` — loads current metadata map, sets `criteriaPassStatus[index] = true`, saves to graph. Called from `EvaluationAgent` after each `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` update (same location as T1-BG's in-memory update).
    2. **Read on startup/sync:** `GoalPlannerService` (or `CycleContextCoordinator`) loads `criteriaPassStatus` from the active subgoal's metadata on context preparation and populates `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` on the context. This restores the accumulated state transparently after a restart.
    3. **Reset:** When a subgoal transitions to FULFILLED or GIVEN_UP, the metadata entry is no longer needed (the goal is closed). No explicit cleanup required.
  - **Graph note:** Metadata is already stored as a JSON blob on the `Goal` node; no schema change is needed. The same pattern is used for `mergeCount`, `cumulativeStagnatingCycles`, `acceptanceCriteria`.
  - **Depends on:** T1-BG (defines `cumulativeCriteriaStatus`), T2-BI (establishes the metadata persistence pattern).
  - **Enables:** T3-BJ (snapshot service reads from here rather than querying T3-BB's event log for the rolled-up view).

- [x] **T3-BJ: Surface criteria progress in orchestration snapshot** (`OrchestrationSnapshotService`, `OrchestrationSnapshotFormatter`)
  - **Problem:** The meta-deliberation agent and interpretation agent currently have no visibility into *which* criteria have been satisfied and which have persistently failed for the current subgoal. They can observe that the subgoal is PROGRESSING, but they cannot distinguish "3 of 4 criteria satisfied — one stubborn criterion remaining" from "0 of 4 criteria ever passed — subgoal is fundamentally stuck." These require completely different steering responses (targeted evolution vs. replanning), but the snapshot gives the agents no data to reason from.
  - **Fix:** In `OrchestrationSnapshotService`, for the active subgoal:
    1. Load `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` from `GoalEntity.metadata["criteriaPassStatus"]` (T3-BI) — the rolled-up per-criterion pass/fail across all cycles.
    2. Query the N most recent `CriterionResult` nodes for this subgoal (T3-BB) to determine *how long* each unmet criterion has been failing (streak count).
    3. Build a `criteriaProgressBlock`: per-criterion summary formatted as `[✓/✗/–] <text> (passed N cycles ago | failed M consecutive cycles | never assessed)`.
  - **Surface in `OrchestrationSnapshotFormatter`** under `--- CRITERIA PROGRESS ---`, between the evaluation summary and the signal list. Visible to the meta-deliberation agent, interpretation agent, and the planner's `CURRENT SUBGOAL STATUS` block.
  - **Expected steering impact:** Meta-agent proposes targeted evolution when 3/4 criteria pass (one remaining). Interpretation agent emits `REPLAN_REQUESTED` when a criterion has failed 5+ consecutive cycles with no improvement. Planner's coaching note becomes criterion-specific ("criterion 2 has never passed — the implementation of X is missing").
  - **Depends on:** T3-BB (event log for streak counts), T3-BI (cumulative status for rolled-up view). Can be partially implemented with T3-BI alone (no streak counts) and upgraded to full streaks when T3-BB is ready.

- [x] **T3-BK: Partial graduation — force-fulfill at high criteria pass rate** (`SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic`, `GoalPlannerService`, `PlannerAgent`)
  - **Problem:** A single stubborn criterion can hold an otherwise-complete subgoal hostage indefinitely. If 3 of 4 criteria have passed across multiple cycles but criterion 4 has failed N consecutive times (likely due to a structural gap in evolution rather than a solvable incremental problem), the subgoal will exhaust and be GIVEN_UP — discarding the real progress made on the other three.
  - **Fix:** In `SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic.decide()`, after the normal fulfillment path fails (unmet criterion veto), check graduation: if `passedCount / totalCount >= GRADUATION_RATIO` (default 0.80, configurable via `autobuilder.criteria.graduation-ratio`) AND the remaining unmet criteria have each failed for at least `GRADUATION_MIN_FAIL_STREAK` cycles (default 3, read from `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` + `lastCriteriaPassCount` history), return `FulfillmentDecision.yes(score, "graduation: M/N criteria satisfied, remaining injected as micro-subgoal")`.
  - **On graduation:** `CycleExecutor.applyCompletion()` detects the "graduation" rationale and calls `PlannerAgent` (or directly `GoalPlannerService`) to inject one micro-subgoal per unmet criterion, each with a single-criterion `Criteria:` block. The parent subgoal is then marked FULFILLED.
  - **Effect:** Progress is banked; the system moves forward rather than exhausting. The micro-subgoal is a focused, smaller target that evolution can hit in fewer cycles.
  - **Configurable:** `autobuilder.criteria.graduation-ratio: 0.80`, `autobuilder.criteria.graduation-min-fail-streak: 3`.
  - **Depends on:** T1-BG (`cumulativeCriteriaStatus`), T3-BI (streak history), T2-BL (exhaustion suppression should fire before graduation is needed — graduation is the last resort).

- [x] **T3-BP: Capture goal/subgoal completion and give-up rationale**

  - **Problem:** When a subgoal transitions to COMPLETED or GIVEN_UP, no reason is recorded. The graph shows the final state but not why — was it fulfilled by criteria? Abandoned after N stagnating cycles? Given up because the planner replaced it? This information is available at transition time but currently discarded.
  - **Fix:**
    1. Add a resolution string field to GoalEntity (Neo4j) and the JSON goal stack. Populated at transition time via GoalPlannerService.
    2. In promoteCurrentSubGoal / wherever COMPLETED / GIVEN_UP is written, pass a short rationale string. Call sites to cover:                                                                       
       - COMPLETED via SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic → e.g. "all criteria passed, score 0.88"                         
       - GIVEN_UP via stagnation exhaustion → e.g. "given up after N stagnating cycles"
       - GIVEN_UP via planner replacement → e.g. "superseded by replanning"
       - GIVEN_UP via GOAL_EXHAUSTED signal
    3. Surface resolution in OrchestrationSnapshotFormatter under the goal/subgoal header so the LLM meta-agent can see it.
  - **Why:** Without this, post-hoc analysis (and the deliberation agent) can't distinguish a subgoal that cleanly passed from one that was silently abandoned. It also aids debugging when goals are given up prematurely.
  - **Depends on:** None — purely additive metadata.

- [>] **T3-B: Activate `GoalEntity.expectedArtifacts`**
  - **Superseded by the T3-AW/T3-AZ/T3-BA acceptance-criteria chain (2026-04-11 assessment).**
  - Original plan: Define schema (file path + existence check or content assertion), populate from planner LLM output, add artifact check to `SubGoalFulfillmentHeuristic.decide()`.
  - **Why superseded:** The `acceptanceCriteria` system (T3-AW through T3-BC) provides richer, LLM-verifiable "done-when" conditions that subsume what `expectedArtifacts` would have offered. File-existence assertions are a strict subset of criteria (a criterion can assert `* File BookController.java exists with a POST /books endpoint`). Maintaining two parallel fulfillment signals would add complexity with no clear benefit. If file-existence assertions are needed in the future, they are better expressed as a specific criterion type within the existing criteria framework rather than a separate `expectedArtifacts` field.

- [ ] **T3-G: Complexity-adaptive regression tolerance — Phase 2 (lines changed composite + calibration)** (`ScoreRegressionHeuristic`, `Neo4jScoreHistoryService`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Status (2026-04-21):** Split into two sub-tickets. Reassessment found the core infrastructure exists — `Neo4jScoreHistoryService` persists per-file scores, global scores, and per-goal evaluation/acceptance events linked via `Cycle` nodes; `DefaultMutationHeuristicAnalyzer` already computes `linesAdded`/`linesDeleted` per file from `diffReport`. What's missing is the linkage (diff size isn't persisted alongside the cycle's score) and the calibration algorithm (current T2-AJ constants are hand-picked).
  - **Core motivation (unchanged):** File count is a crude proxy. A 1-line change to 4 files is not complex; a 500-line rewrite of 1 file is. Lines changed correlates more directly with LLM evaluation variance.
  - **Design options for composite:** (A) replace file-count breakpoints with line-count breakpoints; (B) composite score combining both; (C) weighted product. Decide based on calibration outcome.

  - [ ] **T3-G1: Persist diff size per cycle** (`Neo4jScoreHistoryService`, `CycleScoreRecorder`, `CycleContextData`)
    - **Shovel-ready.** Extend `Neo4jScoreHistoryService` with `recordMutationSize(cycleId, linesAdded, linesDeleted, fileCount)` — stores the three numbers as properties on the existing `Cycle` node (or on a linked `MutationSize` node if preferred for cleanliness). Call from `CycleScoreRecorder` alongside the existing `recordGoalEvaluation` call when a mutation was produced.
    - Expose `getMutationSize(cycleId)` and `getRecentMutationSizes(goalId, limit)` query methods for Phase 2b's calibration job and any other downstream analysis.
    - Add `lastAcceptedMutationLinesChanged` (and a delta counterpart) to `CycleContextData`, populated on merge, mirroring the existing `lastAcceptedMutationFileCount` pattern from T2-AJ. Available to `ScoreRegressionHeuristic` at the point the next-cycle heuristic runs.
    - **No calibration logic yet.** Purpose is only to start accumulating the corpus needed for T3-G2.
    - **Depends on:** None substantive — uses existing `Neo4jScoreHistoryService` pattern and `DiffReport` line counts.

  - [ ] **T3-G2: Calibrate regression tolerance from observed score history** (`ScoreRegressionHeuristic`, new `RegressionToleranceCalibrator`)
    - Requires sufficient corpus from T3-G1 (estimate: 100+ accepted mutations across varied diff sizes to regress meaningfully). If the corpus is too small, hand-tuned constants stay in place.
    - Query `(score, linesAdded, linesDeleted, fileCount)` tuples from Neo4j. Fit a simple model for expected score variance as a function of diff complexity (start with ordinary least squares on |Δscore| vs. lines changed; consider robust regression if outliers dominate). Replace T2-AJ's `SHIFT_PER_FILE` / `SCALE_REDUCTION_PER_FILE` with coefficients derived from the fit.
    - Validate by back-testing: would the calibrated thresholds have correctly accepted/rejected historical mutations? If back-test accuracy is worse than T2-AJ's hand-tuned version, keep the hand-tuned values and log the divergence for manual review.
    - **Depends on:** T3-G1 (data). Probably also T4-J (tuning parameter store) so calibrated values can be persisted and swapped live without code changes.
