    - Each `*ProtocolContract` exposes a `Map<OrchestrationPattern, PromptTemplate>` keyed lookup; the default template handles legacy/unclassified.
    - Logged via the behaviour NDJSON (T5-H) so we can see which patterns dominate and how often each fires — invaluable for tuning and for spotting unhealthy distributions.
  - **Connection to LLM-judge dedup:** Phase A is the concrete starting point and is independently valuable as a fix for the paraphrased-duplicate problem observed in 2026-04-30 17:34 run (planner re-injected 7 paraphrased duplicates of completed first-wave subgoals; Monge-Elkan @ 0.88 didn't catch any of them). T4-AP solves it cleanly without resorting to domain-specific heuristics like REST endpoint normalisation.
  - **Risks / open questions:**
    - Pattern explosion — if every nuance gets its own ID, we just relocate the problem. Hold to a small canonical set; combine via tags rather than multiplying patterns.
    - LLM-judge cost — the duplicate check fires on every planner injection. Cache aggressively; consider a short-circuit when Monge-Elkan is unambiguous.
    - Migration churn — existing tickets that added conditional blocks (T2-AT et al.) need to be re-homed without behaviour change. Use observability (T5-H) to confirm parity before retiring legacy paths.
  - **Prerequisites:** None hard. T5-H (behaviour logger v1, done) is the observability backbone for measuring impact. T4-J (tuning parameter store) is convenient but not required.
  - **Out of scope:** Replacing `DeliberationMode` itself (that's T4-AI's territory). T4-AP is about *prompt-level* state-tagged routing; T4-AI is about unifying meta-step vs. signal-driven orchestration above that layer. They compose well but should not be merged into one effort.

- [ ] **T4-AQ: Stub-body detection and automatic follow-up injection** (new `StubBodyDetector`, `MergeAgent` post-merge hook, `AnalysisAgent` injection path)
  - **Observed (2026-05-01 run):** Cycle-1 bulk evolution merged `LoanService.createLoan()` with placeholder bodies (`// Validation logic would go here`, `// Add to collection`, no ID assignment, no real persistence). All subsequent Loan subgoals built on top of this non-functional skeleton — evolution couldn't satisfy criteria regardless of how many iterations ran, because the service layer was structurally incomplete. REPETITION_DETECTED accumulated to strength 3.0 with no escape. Root cause was the stub, but no heuristic blocked the merge and no agent identified the stub as the root blocker.
  - **Why not a hard rejection:** A stub is sometimes intentional scaffolding — the correct move is to schedule a follow-up that completes it, not silently block the merge. Rejecting stubs unconditionally would also block legitimate partial-progress merges where a body is intentionally minimal and later cycles fill it in.
  - **Design:** New `StubBodyDetector` component, called from a `MergeAgent` post-merge hook after a successful write. It scans each written file for method bodies whose only content is comments and/or a bare `return null` / `return` (regex-based, framework-neutral). For each stub found it records: class name, method signature, and the placeholder comment text. If any stubs are detected, `MergeAgent` (or the analysis path) injects a corrective child subgoal under the current active subgoal — description generated from the stub signature and comment, e.g. "Implement `LoanService.createLoan(bookId, memberId)` — replace placeholder body with real validation and persistence logic." The subgoal is tagged with a `spawnedBy=stub-detector` metadata flag so it can be tracked separately from analysis-injected children.
  - **Scope:** Only fires on methods that are both (a) non-trivial by name (not getters/setters/constructors) and (b) have bodies consisting solely of comments + optional bare return. Does not fire on abstract methods, interface defaults, or explicitly annotated `@Override` stubs.
  - **Components:**
    - `StubBodyDetector.java` — scans a list of file contents, returns `List<StubMethod>` (className, methodSignature, placeholderText).
    - `MergeAgent.java` — after successful filesystem write, invoke detector; if stubs found, call analysis injection path.
    - Injection path (reuse existing `AnalysisAgent` subgoal injection infrastructure) — create child subgoal(s) with `spawnedBy=stub-detector`.
  - **Prerequisite:** T4-AN-1 (compile gate) would make this more powerful — a stub that compiles cleanly but fails tests is a stronger signal than regex alone. T4-AQ is independently useful without it.
  - **Out of scope:** Detecting stubs in files already present before the current merge (only newly-written files are scanned). Fixing the bulk-evolution quality problem that produced the stubs in the first place (a separate T2 prompt-steering concern).

- [ ] **T4-AR: Mission-control overlay — phase-level event stream, auto drill-down modals, richer narration** (`graph.html`, narration hooks; presentation layer over `BehaviourEventPublisher`)
  - **Motivation (2026-05-03 design discussion):** The live 3D graph (T4-AM) and the structured behaviour event stream (T5-H v1) are both shipped, but they don't talk to each other yet. `graph.html` only subscribes to `/autobuilder/goals/stream` (cycle-boundary granularity); the rich phase-level events from `/events/behaviour` (`agent_selected`, `mutation_proposed`, `heuristic_result`, `mutation_negotiated`, `mutation_merged`, `mutation_rejected`, `deliberation_outcome`, …) currently only land in `behaviour.ndjson`. As a result, both the visual layer and the TTS narration can only react at cycle-end — not when an agent starts running, when a heuristic overrides the LLM, or when a mutation is rejected mid-cycle. Closing this gap on the frontend turns the existing graph into a "mission-control" view of what the loop is doing, in real time.
  - **Three presentation layers (no backend changes for the MVP):**
    1. **Event timeline strip** below the 3D canvas. Subscribes to `GET /events/behaviour` as a second `EventSource`. Renders the last ~30 events as a horizontal scrolling ticker; each event is a coloured chip keyed by event type (agent_selected = blue, mutation_proposed = amber, heuristic_result = grey or red on override, mutation_merged = green, mutation_rejected = red, deliberation_outcome = purple). Click a chip → opens a drill-down modal for that event's payload.
    2. **Auto drill-down ghost modals.** A curated subset of "important" events auto-pop a semi-transparent corner modal (~1.5–3 s lifespan, click to pin / dismiss). Initial trigger set: heuristic override of LLM result, mutation_rejected with reason, deliberation_outcome when mode switches, criteria-check parsed M < N. Modal body shows the payload slice that matters (LLM score → final score, rejection reason, affected files / diff snippet). Stack ≤ 3; older modals slide out. Never auto-pop on cycle_start / agent_skipped / agent_selected — those stay timeline-only.
    3. **Phase-level narration with variable rate.** Extend `TTS` with `announcePhase(event)` keyed by event type. Default `playbackRate = 1.0`; bursts (≥ 3 narration-eligible events within 2 s) transiently lift to 1.4 to keep up without dropping events. Curated speak set only (heuristic override, mutation_merged, mutation_rejected with one-line reason, deliberation_outcome on mode switch). Replace today's binary `speak` checkbox with a three-way verbosity toggle in the header (`silent` / `important` / `chatty`).
  - **Implementation shape (~250 lines added to `graph.html`):**
    - Second `EventSource('/events/behaviour')` connection alongside the existing `/autobuilder/goals/stream` connection. Independent reconnect / status pill.
    - `EventChip` renderer, ring-buffer of last N events, click handler → existing modal infrastructure (reuse `#editor-overlay` patterns).
    - `ModalStack` manager for auto-pop ghost modals (separate visual layer from the existing editor modal — different z-index + styling so they don't collide).
    - `NarrationPolicy` map: event type → `{ speak: bool, priority: 'low'|'high', textBuilder(payload) }`. Burst-rate detection in the TTS module.
  - **Out of scope (MVP):**
    - Persisting the timeline across reloads (sessionStorage at most).
    - A full ledger / replay view — `behaviour.ndjson` + `tail` already covers post-hoc inspection.
    - Editing or filtering by event type — that's a v2 if the strip gets crowded.
    - **Mid-agent progress events** like `evolution_llm_streaming` or "strategy preparing" sub-states — those would require backend instrumentation inside the agents and are a separate T5 ticket (extending T5-I) or a v2 of T5-H.
  - **Design risks:**
    - **Visual noise** — too many chips / modals makes the screen worse, not better. The verbosity toggle is the safety valve; default to `important` and curate the auto-pop set conservatively.
    - **Narration spam** — phase narration must use `priority: 'low'` so existing high-priority node-click and cycle-end narration still cuts through. Burst-rate boost shortens audio but should not stack indefinitely.
    - **SSE multiplexing** — two parallel `EventSource` streams from one browser is fine in practice (HTTP/2 multiplexes), but worth noting; if it becomes a chokepoint later, consolidate behind a single `/events/all` endpoint.
    - **Auto-pop dismissal UX** — modals that vanish before the user can read them are infuriating. Click-to-pin must be obvious; long event payloads truncate with "…" + "click to expand."
  - **Prerequisites:** T4-AM (live 3D graph) — done. T5-H v1 (phase event stream + `/events/behaviour` SSE) — done. No hard prerequisites.
  - **Downstream:** T5-I (behaviour v2 — goal-tree + pattern-detector events) — once shipped, fold those new event types into the timeline / narration mappings with no UI plumbing changes.

- [ ] **T4-AS: Multi-provider LLM fallback — design placeholder** (`LLMClient`, `LlmCircuitBreaker`, new `LLMProviderRegistry`, `application.yml`)
  - **Problem:** `LLMClient` wraps a single provider with a circuit breaker. When the primary provider is slow, rate-limited, or unreachable, the circuit breaker halts the whole loop. No fallback path. Risky for autonomous overnight runs.
  - **Sketch:** registry of provider configurations (OpenAI, Anthropic, Ollama, Azure OpenAI, …); per-role provider preferences (e.g. classifier might run on a smaller/local model — see T4-AN-4 model-selection note); circuit-breaker promotes the fallback when the primary trips. Costs / token-counting (T5-R) needs to attribute per-provider.
  - **Open design questions:**
    - **Model-equivalence assumption.** Switching providers mid-session changes the prompt-compliance distribution. Heuristic negotiator and prompt contracts assume a stable model. Quantify drift before relying on fallback.
    - **State leakage across providers.** Prompt-cache hits become irrelevant if traffic flips to a fresh provider. Cost spike on cutover.
    - **Configuration explosion.** Each provider has its own auth, base URL, timeouts, sampling defaults. Yaml config bloat.
  - **Don't ship:** until a real session has been killed by primary-provider failure. Speculative complexity otherwise.
  - **Status:** design placeholder — file as a `[ ]` with no concrete spec until the failure mode is observed.

- [ ] **T4-AT: Session resume after `SYSTEM_PAUSED` — design placeholder** (`AutobuilderService`, `CycleContextCoordinator`, new `SessionResumeService`)
  - **Problem:** `SYSTEM_PAUSED` halts the scheduler with no documented resume path. Neo4j retains goal state; `output/` retains code; `meta/GoalStack.json` retains the stack — but the in-memory `CycleContextData` carry-forward chain is lost (signals, criteria status, lineage tracking, evaluator-repeat counters, etc.). Restarting the autobuilder reconstructs context partially from Neo4j (T2-BK persistence) but several fields reset.
  - **Sketch:** snapshot `CycleContextData` (or its persistable subset) to `meta/session/last-cycle-context.json` on every cycle end. On startup, if `meta/session/last-cycle-context.json` exists and matches the current Neo4j goal state, hydrate into the first cycle. A human-readable `meta/session/pause-reason.md` companion (populated by T1-EB on halt) explains why the session stopped.
  - **Open design questions:**
    - **Subset vs full serialise.** Some fields (`projectDiff`, `currentOutput`) are derivable from disk on next cycle and shouldn't be persisted. Some (signals, criteria banks, attempt counters) must be persisted. Drawing the line is the actual design work.
    - **Schema evolution.** Resuming a session after upgrading autobuilder may load context with stale shape. Define versioning + migration policy.
    - **Mid-cycle crash recovery.** If autobuilder crashes mid-cycle (between merge and build), resume semantics need to know whether the merge happened. Pairs with T1-DW (rollback) — both need durable cycle-state markers.
  - **Don't ship:** until a real operational pain point is felt. Today's pause is rare; resume complexity is high.
  - **Status:** design placeholder — file as `[ ]` with no concrete spec until ops scenarios are real.

- [ ] **T4-AU: Ecosystem-aware file-role classification — single source of truth** (new `core/build/FileRoleClassifier` or extend `BuildToolProbe`, callers: `BlockerSubgoalInjector.resolveFailedPath`, `MergeAgent.touchesBuildConfig`, `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic.BUILD_CONFIG_FILES`, `FileVariantCollector.BUILD_CONFIG_FILES`)
  - **Motivation:** The codebase already has *four* hardcoded build-config filename lists at the time of writing (T2-CW filed against three of them; T1-DQ added a fourth). T2-CY originally added a *fifth* in `BlockerSubgoalInjector` to filter `contextPaths` (skipping `pom.xml`/`build.gradle*` so the BUILD_FIX subgoal description names a Java file rather than the build descriptor). Removed before merge once we noticed (a) it duplicates yet again, (b) it's actually wrong for the MISSING_POM case where `pom.xml` *is* the right repair target. The pattern reveals a missing abstraction: callers want to ask shaped questions about file roles ("is this a build descriptor?", "is this a source file?", "is this a test?", "is this a generated artifact?") and today they answer those questions with hardcoded filename lists scattered across the codebase.
  - **Today's hardcoded lists, by caller:**
    1. `BuildFixAcceptanceHeuristic.BUILD_CONFIG_FILES` — used to recognise build-config mutations under `COMPILE_ERROR` for force-acceptance.
    2. `FileVariantCollector.BUILD_CONFIG_FILES` — exempts build-config files from subgoal-id scope filter so a `pom.xml` accepted under a `BUILD_FIX` subgoal stays mergeable after the planner replaces it.
    3. `MergeAgent.touchesBuildConfig` — exempts build-config mutations from T1-DW filesystem rollback so build-fix attempts persist on disk for the heuristic to see.
    4. (T1-DS) `target/` exclusion in `FileService.listFiles`, `DefaultProjectStructureSummarizer`, `application.yml.exclude-folders` — three more places that need to know which directories are build-tool output.
  - **Future callers identified:**
    - `BlockerSubgoalInjector.resolveFailedPath` (T2-CY): when stderr has no file:line, fallback to contextPaths needs to know whether each path is "the build descriptor" vs "a source file" — for MISSING_POM, the correct fallback IS `pom.xml`; for CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL, it is NOT (we want the source file). The right answer depends on the *error class*, not the *filename*.
    - Future per-error-class repair-target selection: similarly needs error-class → preferred-file-role mapping.
    - T3-BT (bootstrap-time build-system seeding) needs to know which build descriptor to emit for the seed's apparent ecosystem.
  - **Design approach (not committed — design work is part of the ticket):**
    1. **Single registry yaml** — extend the `BuildToolProbe` registry T2-CW already proposes. Each entry declares `filenames` (the files that DEFINE this build tool — `pom.xml`, `build.gradle`, `Cargo.toml`, etc.) and `outputDirectories` (the directories the tool generates — `target/`, `build/`, `node_modules/`, `dist/`).
    2. **`FileRoleClassifier` interface** — pure Java service that consults the registry:
       - `Set<FileRole> rolesOf(String path)` returning some subset of `{BUILD_DESCRIPTOR, SOURCE, TEST, GENERATED_OUTPUT, RESOURCE, UNKNOWN}`.
       - `Optional<String> preferredRepairTarget(BuildErrorClassifier.Kind errorKind, List<String> candidates)` — error-class-aware preferred-file selection. For MISSING_POM the answer is the candidate matching `BUILD_DESCRIPTOR` role. For CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL the answer is the candidate matching `SOURCE` role, prefer non-test if both exist.
    3. **All four current call sites collapse to `classifier.rolesOf(path).contains(BUILD_DESCRIPTOR)`** (or equivalent role check). T2-CW reduces to a config-file change in this design — its hardcoded lists are now register entries.
  - **Why T4 not T2:** the immediate fix in T2-CW (consolidate the three existing duplicate lists) is small and cheap. T4-AU is the broader layer that makes that consolidation extensible — once the registry exists, adding a new build system (Cargo, npm, sbt, Bazel) is a yaml entry rather than touching N Java files. T2-CW can be the first half of T4-AU's implementation; T4-AU finishes the abstraction.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. Zero hardcoded build-config filename literals in Java code outside the registry implementation.
    2. Adding a new build system is a yaml-only change.
    3. `BlockerSubgoalInjector.resolveFailedPath` consults `preferredRepairTarget(errorKind, candidates)` rather than positional fallback or hardcoded skip lists; MISSING_POM picks the build descriptor, CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL picks the source file.
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **Multi-tool repos.** A monorepo containing both `pom.xml` and `package.json` has TWO `BUILD_DESCRIPTOR` roles. Per-error-class repair-target selection scopes by the kind of build that failed (the build error stderr identifies the tool used).
    - **Ambiguous file roles.** `application.yml` is both `RESOURCE` and (for Spring Boot) effectively a build/config file. Roles are a `Set`, not a single value, so a file can have multiple.
    - **Generated-but-checked-in files.** Some projects check in `target/site/` or generated stubs. Out-of-tree by default; per-project override via configurable patterns.
  - **Risks:**
    - **YAML schema churn.** First implementation pins the schema. Migrations needed if the role taxonomy expands.
    - **Performance.** `rolesOf(path)` called once per merged path is fine; called per-line during stderr scanning would be wasteful. Cache shape decisions accordingly.
    - **Over-engineering.** Today only `BUILD_DESCRIPTOR` is asked about. Don't speculatively add the full role enum until a second role is needed by a real caller.
  - **Depends on:** T2-CW (consolidation of the three existing lists is the natural first step). Pairs with: T4-AN-5 (BuildToolProbe registry shares this yaml), T3-BT (bootstrap seeding needs the same registry to know which descriptor to emit).
  - **Don't ship:** until a real second build system is needed in production OR until the fifth caller surfaces. Premature otherwise. T2-CW is sufficient for now.
  - **Status:** design placeholder — capture the abstraction so future T2-CW-shaped tickets don't recreate the same duplication.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after T2-CY's review surfaced a fifth would-be hardcoded filename list. The "always one more list" pattern is the reason this needs a registry, not another quick consolidation.

- [>] **T4-AV: LLM coherence reviewer — semantic coherence check as a new evaluation dimension** (new `agent/coherence/CoherenceReviewAgent`, new `protocol/coherence/CoherenceReviewContract`, new `BasicSignals.ROLE_COHERENCE` / `INCOHERENCE_DETECTED`, `CycleContextData`)
  - **Status: DEFERRED — do not implement until a coherence pattern is observed in production that T2-DI's deterministic dataflow scan does not catch.**
  - **Idea:** After a merge that compiles successfully, a `CoherenceReviewAgent` invokes the LLM with the full output structure + acceptance criteria and asks "is this code semantically coherent? Are entity stores shared? Do controllers delegate to services? Do exception types map onto the contracts in the goal? Are dependencies layered?" The LLM returns a structured list of `(severity, file:line, description)` findings. High-severity findings emit `INCOHERENCE_DETECTED`, force REJECTED, and become the divergence prompt for the next cycle.
  - **Why this is parked:**
    - The 20:35:35 Book parallel-store bug is catchable purely by reading the code — no LLM coherence reasoning needed. T2-DI catches it deterministically.
    - An LLM coherence call adds ~1 LLM round-trip per merged cycle plus the prompt-engineering work to make the question reliable.
    - The general "what is wrong with this code?" framing is what `AnalysisAgent` already does — overlap with the existing analysis role.
  - **Trigger to un-park:** Production observation of a coherence bug that is *not* a parallel-store / orphan / dangling-ref / registration-collision / spec-literal pattern. Plausible future triggers:
    - Layer-bypass: a controller directly using a repository, skipping the service.
    - Field-mutation contracts: an entity has both setters and `final` fields in incompatible ways.
    - Cross-class invariant violation: one class throws an exception that another class's `@ExceptionHandler` can never reach because the exception's package was changed mid-cycle and the handler points at a stale fully-qualified name.
  - **Why T4, not T2:** Introduces a new agent and a new prompt contract — not just another heuristic on top of existing analysis. Closer in shape to `AnalysisAgent` than to `StructuralIntegrityHeuristic`.
  - **Sketch when un-parked:**
    - `CoherenceReviewAgent implements ExecutionAgent` — fitness-ranked alongside the other agents, gated to fire only post-merge with `BuildOutcome.result()==COMPILE_OK`.
    - `CoherenceReviewContract` prompt template: `<files>… <criteria>… <findings_template>…` — request structured JSON.
    - New signal `INCOHERENCE_DETECTED` consumed by `EvaluationAgent` / `HeuristicNegotiator` as a CRITICAL REJECTED opinion.
    - New role in `BasicSignals` (`ROLE_COHERENCE`).
  - **Depends on:** T2-DI (must ship first; T4-AV is the escalation when T2-DI proves insufficient). T1-EC compile gate (only run coherence review on compilable output).
  - **Pairs with:** T2-CM, T2-CN, T2-CO, T2-DI — all structural-awareness layers feed the LLM's overall picture.
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-18 as a deferred placeholder. The cost-benefit favors the deterministic check (T2-DI) today; this LLM reviewer is the next layer if and only if the static one proves insufficient. Worth re-evaluating each time a new coherence pattern surfaces in a live run.

- [ ] **T4-AW: Output critic SPI — structural verification of analyzer/planner LLM output against project ground truth** (new `critic/Critic` SPI, `critic/CriticVerdict`, `critic/PathCriticv1`, `critic/SymbolCritic`, `critic/EndpointCritic`, `critic/DependencyCritic`, optional `critic/LlmCritic`; wired into `AnalysisAgent` and `PlannerAgent` post-LLM)
  - **Motivation:** Observed in 2026-05-19 20:51 run — `AnalysisAgent` emitted the criterion `Loan class exists in src/main/java/com/example/library/loan/Loan.java` while the project uses `com.example.demo.*`. `Loan.java` was correctly implemented at `com/example/demo/model/Loan.java` and merged twice, but the criterion was structurally unsatisfiable, so the subgoal stayed ACTIVE for 6+ cycles. It was eventually removed by planner discretion (`#SubGoalRemove`), not by any deterministic state transition. The session's prompt-side fix (PACKAGE CONSTRAINT in `AnalysisProtocolContract`, landed 2026-05-19) steers the LLM away from one specific failure mode (`com.example.library.*`) but doesn't prevent other drifts (hallucinated symbols, hallucinated endpoints, deps not in pom.xml, paths under packages that don't exist yet).
  - **Core idea:** A two-layer `Critic` SPI applied to `AnalysisAgent` and `PlannerAgent` output before it enters the goal graph. The first layer is deterministic (cheap, narrow recall); the second is an LLM "second opinion" (sampled, broader recall). Each critic emits a verdict per item it cares about; the agent applies verdicts in order: REJECT drops the item, REWRITE replaces it, OK accepts it. All decisions are logged and emit `subgoal_critic_verdict` behaviour events for falsifiability.
  - **Verdict model:**
    - `CriticVerdict` = `(target: AnalyzerItem, verdict: OK | REJECT | REWRITE, rewritten: Optional<String>, reason: String, critic: String, confidence: double)`.
    - `AnalyzerItem` discriminator: `CRITERION` (path-anchored or symbol-anchored), `SUBGOAL_DESC`, `FINDING`. Initial scope is CRITERION only.
  - **Sub-tasks:**
    - **T4-AW-1: `Critic` SPI + verdict model + `CriticChain` orchestrator.**
      `interface Critic { List<CriticVerdict> review(List<AnalyzerItem> items, CycleContextData ctx); }`. `CriticChain` (Spring-injected `List<Critic>`) walks critics in `@Order`, applies verdicts left-to-right, returns the surviving/rewritten item list. New `BehaviourEvent.SubgoalCriticVerdict` + `BehaviourLogger.subgoalCriticVerdict()`. No critics yet — purely the wiring + tests.
    - **T4-AW-2: `PathCritic` (deterministic) — anchor path criteria to project ground truth.**
      For every criterion mentioning a path under `src/main/java/`: (a) if `establishedBasePackage` is set and the path's package prefix doesn't match, attempt REWRITE by searching the project for a same-named file under the established base and pointing the criterion there; if no match, REJECT with reason `path_off_base_package`. (b) If the path mentions a file that doesn't exist on disk yet but the symbol name matches a known on-disk file in a different location, REWRITE to the existing location. The case that motivated this ticket (`library/loan/Loan.java` → `demo/model/Loan.java`) is a REWRITE.
    - **T4-AW-3: `SymbolCritic` (deterministic) — verify symbol-anchored criteria against project tree.**
      For every criterion of shape `<Name> class exists`/`<Name> enum exists`/`<Name> method exists in <Class>`: grep the project for `class <Name>` / `enum <Name>` / `<Class>` method declarations. If found, REWRITE the criterion to include the actual path; if not found, mark the criterion OK (it correctly describes work yet to do) but stamp `confidence=0.5` so downstream can treat it as soft.
    - **T4-AW-4: `EndpointCritic` (deterministic) — verify endpoint criteria against root goal.**
      For every criterion mentioning an HTTP endpoint (`POST /foo`, `GET /bar/{id}`): check whether the endpoint string appears in `ctx.currentGoalDescription()` or the root goal text. If not, REJECT with reason `endpoint_not_in_root_goal` (this is the ROOT_GOAL_SCOPE rule, but enforced deterministically rather than prompt-only).
    - **T4-AW-5: `DependencyCritic` (deterministic) — verify pom.xml-referenced criteria.**
      For every criterion mentioning a Maven dependency (`pom.xml includes <artifactId>`): if the project pom.xml is readable and the artifact is already present, mark the criterion OK with `precondition=true` and a banner so the LLM knows it's already satisfied. If the artifact name is unknown to `KnownStarterResolver`'s table or Maven Central (later), mark `confidence=0.3` and flag for review.
    - **T4-AW-6: Wire into `AnalysisAgent` and `PlannerAgent`.**
      Post-LLM, before subgoals/criteria are persisted to the goal graph, run `CriticChain.review()`. Apply verdicts. Log a summary line per cycle: `Critics: <N OK, M REJECT, K REWRITE> reasons={path_off_base_package=2, ...}`. Both agents share the same chain — same item types, same critics.
    - **T4-AW-7: `LlmCritic` (sampled, optional).**
      Second LLM call with prompt: `"Given <project package tree>, <pom.xml summary>, <existing classes>, <root goal>, <these analyzer items>, return for each item a verdict {OK, REJECT, REWRITE, REWRITE_AS:<text>} and a one-line reason."`. Config: `autobuilder.critic.llm.enabled=false` (default off), `autobuilder.critic.llm.sample-rate=0.1` (sample 1 in 10 analyzer cycles when on), `autobuilder.critic.llm.confidence-threshold=0.7` (only act on verdicts above threshold). Pays for itself only on the long tail that deterministic critics miss; absent that tail, leaves the system unchanged.
  - **Invariants to preserve:**
    - Never silently drop a criterion — every REJECT emits a behaviour event with reason.
    - Critics never call other critics (no recursion). Each is a pure function from items + context to verdicts.
    - `LlmCritic` must respect its sample-rate even when enabled — never burns tokens on every cycle.
    - Critics see read-only views of context — they cannot mutate state.
  - **Migration path:** T4-AW-1 (SPI + wiring) + T4-AW-2 (PathCritic) are the minimum viable subset; together they fix the bug that motivated this ticket. T4-AW-3 / -4 / -5 are independent and can land in any order. T4-AW-7 (LlmCritic) is gated behind operational signal that the deterministic critics miss cases — until then it stays off.
  - **Downstream / pairs:**
    - **T2-DC** (symbol grounding) — complementary: T2-DC is *inbound* to the LLM (annotates the prompt with `[UNDEFINED]` markers so the LLM avoids hallucinating); T4-AW is *outbound* from the LLM (catches whatever hallucinations slipped through). Both layers needed.
    - **T2-CM family** (structural awareness) — feeds the same project-tree facts T4-AW critics rely on; the `SymbolCritic` and `PathCritic` may reuse `JavaStructureParser` / `SymbolGroundingService`.
    - **T4-AV** (LLM coherence reviewer) — different concern: T4-AV checks post-merge semantics (parallel stores, layer bypass); T4-AW checks pre-merge structural plausibility of criteria. Could share `LlmCritic` infrastructure if both ship.
    - **T4-AI** (unified orchestration) — when T4-AI lands, `CriticChain` becomes a natural place for the meta-agent to inject `proposedSignals` based on cumulative critic verdicts ("if more than 30% of last 5 cycles' criteria were path-off-base, raise PLANNING_RESTRUCTURE").
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-19 after analysis of 20:51 run (Loan subgoal stranded for 6+ cycles due to path hallucination despite correct implementation). Prompt-side fix to `AnalysisProtocolContract` landed same session as the first line of defense; this ticket is the structural backstop and the generalisation to other classes of analyzer hallucination.

- [x] **T4-AX: Deterministic compile-fix injector — auto-patch high-confidence build errors instead of routing through the LLM** (new `core/build/inject/` package: `CompileFixResolver` SPI, `BootstrapPomResolver`, `KnownStarterResolver`, `CompileFixInjector`, `FixProposal`, `InjectorFixRecord`; `CompileProbeService` wiring; `BuildErrorClassifier.extractToken`; `CycleContextData.injectorFixes`; `BehaviourEvent.MutationInjected` + `BehaviourLogger.mutationInjected`; `CompileFixInjectionRegistrar` graph action; `INJECTOR FIXES THIS CYCLE` blocks in evaluator + planner contracts; `EvaluationAgent.foldInjectorFixes`) — shipped 2026-05-19
  - **Motivation:** Pre-injector runs (e.g. 2026-05-19 18:20) burned 5+ cycles bootstrapping a target project because the LLM kept proposing Java sources before any `pom.xml` existed; later cycles burned more cycles on `package jakarta.persistence does not exist` / `package org.springframework.data.repository does not exist` while LLM-driven `[BUILD_FIX]` subgoals chased the same missing-dep error from different code paths. The fixes are deterministic — name the missing package, append a known starter, recompile. There's no learning signal lost by auto-fixing them; the LLM-driven `[BUILD_FIX]` path stays as the safety net for the long tail.
  - **Architecture:**
    - `BuildErrorClassifier.classify(stderr)` (T2-CT, pre-existing) returns `{MISSING_POM, PACKAGE_DOES_NOT_EXIST, CANNOT_FIND_SYMBOL, UNKNOWN}`. New `extractToken(stderr, kind)` returns the missing package / symbol name as a structured `Optional<String>` — resolvers consume that instead of re-parsing stderr.
    - `CompileFixResolver` SPI: `Optional<FixProposal> resolve(Kind, String missingToken, CycleContextData)`. Implementations are Spring `@Component`s with `@Order` — `CompileFixInjector` walks them and returns the first match.
    - `BootstrapPomResolver` (@Order 10): on `MISSING_POM` writes a default Spring Boot 3.3.0 starter pom.xml (parent + Java 21 + starter-web + starter-validation + lombok + spring-boot-maven-plugin).
    - `KnownStarterResolver` (@Order 20): longest-prefix table for `jakarta.persistence` / `javax.persistence` / `jakarta.validation` / `javax.validation` / `org.springframework.data.repository` / `org.springframework.data.jpa` / `org.springframework.web` → appends matching `<dependency>` block to existing `pom.xml`. Stop-gap by design — replaceable by future resolvers (Maven Central search, local `~/.m2` index, symbol resolver) without touching injector or probe.
    - `CompileFixInjector` orchestrator: stateless, takes (stderr, ctx), classifies, walks resolvers, returns first match. One-attempt-per-cycle guarantee lives at the call site (`CompileProbeService`), not here.
  - **Wiring:**
    - `CompileProbeService.probe()` now calls injector on `COMPILE_ERROR`: writes fix to `output/` + `mutations/<cycleId>/`, re-probes once. New `probe(ctx, allowInjection)` overload; `MergeAgent` passes `false` on T1-EH deferred re-probe so a deferred mutation's stale state cannot be masked by a current-cycle injection. `ProbeResult.appliedFixes` is a (possibly empty) list of `FixProposal`s — backward-compat two-arg constructor preserved for existing call sites.
    - `CycleContextData.injectorFixes: List<InjectorFixRecord>` is the canonical provenance store. Persists into `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` automatically via Jackson; survives across cycles for analysis/planner context.
    - `EvaluationAgent.foldInjectorFixes(ctx, probeResult)` runs post-probe inside `execute(...)`: appends injector mutations to `evolutionOutcome.addedOrChangedFiles` (so the diff the evaluator sees includes them), appends `InjectorFixRecord`s to `ctx.injectorFixes`, emits one `mutation_injected` behaviour event per fix (with `rule`, `kind`, `missingToken`, `touchedPaths`, `reProbeResult`), and registers a `CompileFixInjectionRegistrar` graph action (`compile_fix_injection` ActionEntity + JSON ArtifactEntity).
  - **Prompt surfacing (provenance only where it changes downstream decisions, per "not provenance everywhere"):**
    - Evaluator: `INJECTOR FIXES THIS CYCLE` block warns "do not penalise the LLM mutation for these edits, do not flag them as missing — they are already in place."
    - Planner: same block warns "do NOT propose `[BUILD_FIX]` subgoals for these — already resolved."
    - Evolution / merge / heuristics: no provenance surfaced (no consumer makes a different decision because of it).
  - **Live validation (2026-05-19 20:12 run, 26 cycles, 4 merges):** First cycle hit `bootstrap-pom` cleanly; the `MISSING_POM` stall that ate 5 cycles in the prior run was eliminated in 1. `mutation_injected` event observed in behaviour log with `rule=bootstrap-pom, kind=MISSING_POM`. The follow-on log-noise bug (`reProbeResult=UNKNOWN`) was fixed same session.
  - **Tests:** `BuildErrorClassifierExtractTokenTest` (5), `BootstrapPomResolverTest` (2), `KnownStarterResolverTest` (9 — longest-prefix match, dep-already-present skip, pom-missing defer, splice ordering), `CompileFixInjectorTest` (6 — first-match-wins, resolver walking, token threading), plus regression in `EvaluationAgentTest` for double-rollback guard. 1134/1134 green at end of session.
  - **Future:** New resolvers slot in via the SPI without touching the injector or the probe — `MavenCentralPackageResolver`, `LocalM2IndexResolver`, `SymbolResolver` (for `cannot find symbol class X` → propose `import` when X exists elsewhere in project). Symbol-resolver scope deferred; deterministic critics in T4-AW would catch the cases that survive the resolvers.
  - **Pairs with:** T4-AW (output critic — operates on planner/analyzer LLM output before goal-graph persistence; T4-AX operates on compile stderr before LLM evaluation. Together they catch hallucination on both sides of the LLM round-trip).

---

## Tier 5 — Quality / observability

- [x] **T5-AZ: `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` signal carry-over causes false-positive merge hook invocations** (`CycleContextCoordinator`, `applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation`)
  - **Problem:** `CycleContextCoordinator.prepareRuntimeContext()` uses `previous.toBuilder()` to seed the new context, carrying `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` from the prior cycle. On evaluation-only cycles, `applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation` checks `changedThisCycle = ctx.hasSignal(EVOLUTION_OCCURRED)` — returns `true` even though evolution never ran. The merge hook fires, calls `tryExecuteRole("merge", ...)`, which correctly fails with `missing variant`. Result: spurious eligibility check on every evaluation-only cycle.
  - **Root cause observed:** 2026-04-02 session — cycles 2, 4, 6 all showed `EVOLUTION_OCCURRED` signal despite no evolution running.
  - **Fix:** Introduce a `boolean evolutionRanThisCycle` field on `CycleContextData`, explicitly reset to `false` in `CycleContextCoordinator` (alongside `merged(false)`), and set to `true` only by `EvolutionAgent.runEvolution()`. Replace `ctx.hasSignal(EVOLUTION_OCCURRED)` check in `applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation` with `ctx.evolutionRanThisCycle()`.
  - **Note:** Not a functional bug (`hasAnyVariant` check correctly blocks the spurious attempt), but wasted work and a misleading signal.

- [x] **T5-BC: `ProjectDiffService` crashes on syntactically invalid Java — `IllegalArgumentException` not caught** (`ProjectDiffService`)
  - **Problem (observed 2026-04-11 session):** When the LLM generates syntactically invalid Java (e.g. `BookController.java`, `LoanService.java`), `JavaStructureParser.parse()` throws `IllegalArgumentException: Invalid Java source`. This propagates through `StructuralJavaDiffBuilder.diffJavaFile` and `ProjectDiffService.computeDiff`, logging an `ERROR` and losing the structural diff for that file. The outer `try/catch(Exception)` in `computeDiff` absorbed it — returning an empty diff result for the whole cycle — but the inner `try` per file entry only caught `IOException`, leaving `IllegalArgumentException` to bypass the per-file fallback.
  - **Fix:** Wrap the `StructuralJavaDiffBuilder.diffJavaFile` call in a nested `try/catch(IllegalArgumentException)` inside the per-file block. On catch, log at WARN and fall back to a file-level `StructuralDiffNode` (same as the non-Java path), so the diff continues for other files and the evolution context is not silently discarded.
  - **Changes:** `ProjectDiffService.java` — nested try/catch around `diffJavaFile` (~8 lines).

- [x] **T5-BA: `CyclePersistenceService.persistSnapshot()` creates empty mutation folders for evaluation-only cycles** (`CyclePersistenceService`, `MutationSnapshotService`, `FileVariantCollector`)
  - **Problem:** `persistSnapshot(context)` calls `mutationSnapshotService.writeMutationMeta(context)` unconditionally at the end of every cycle. On evaluation-only cycles, this creates `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` with no accompanying content files. `FileVariantCollector.getAllVariantPaths()` scans all subfolders of `mutations/` on every merge eligibility check — O(N) wasted I/O per merge check as cycles accumulate.
  - **Fix:** In `CyclePersistenceService.persistSnapshot()` (or `MutationSnapshotService.writeMutationMeta()`), only write `mutations/<cycleId>/meta.json` if evolution actually ran this cycle (i.e., `context.evolutionRanThisCycle()` — see T5-AZ).
  - **Observed:** 2026-04-02 session — cycles 1775152916594, 1775152953125, 1775152992090 each produced `mutations/<id>/meta.json` with no content, then appeared in subsequent meta deliberation eligibility reports as "missing variant" candidates.

- [x] **T5-F: Per-link observability logs for the acceptance-criteria chain** (`EvaluationAgent`)
  - **Motivation (observed 2026-04-08):** Both live failures in the T3-AW→T3-AY→T3-AZ→T3-BA chain were silent — no log line indicated that criteria were absent from the evaluation prompt or that the `CriteriaCheck:` block was incomplete. Diagnosing the failures required a full post-hoc log trawl.
  - **Fix:** Add two `INFO`/`WARN` log lines in `EvaluationAgent.execute()`:
    1. After building the evaluation context: `"Criteria delivered to evaluator: N"` (where N = `context.currentSubGoalCriteria().size()`, or 0 if null). Log at `INFO` when N > 0, `WARN` when N == 0 and a subgoal is active (hydration may have failed).
    2. After `parseCriteriaAssessments()`: `"CriteriaCheck lines parsed: M of N expected"` (M = `criteriaAssessments.size()`, N = criteria count). Log at `INFO` when M == N, `WARN` when M < N (partial LLM compliance), `WARN` when M == 0 and N > 0 (complete omission).
  - **Scope:** Two log lines, no logic change. The count-mismatch warning is the safety signal T3-BC needs to be actionable without a manual log trawl.
  - **Depends on:** T3-AZ (criteriaAssessments), T3-AW (currentSubGoalCriteria).

- [x] **T5-H: Dedicated NDJSON behaviour logger + SSE stream — v1 (core/mutation/steering)** (new `observability/behaviour/*`, `CycleExecutor`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `EvolutionAgent`, `EvaluationAgent`, `HeuristicNegotiator`, `MergeAgent`, `DeliberationService`, `logback-spring.xml`)
  - **Motivation:** ~394 log calls across ~70 files all funnel into `${ROOT_PATH}/logs/autobuilder.log` as free-form text. Diagnosing "why did this cycle do X?" requires grepping prose. We need a structured, machine-readable behaviour trace that complements (not replaces) the existing log.
  - **Output 1 — NDJSON file:** Add a dedicated SLF4J logger named `autobuilder.behaviour` wired in `logback-spring.xml` to its own appender at `${ROOT_PATH}/logs/behaviour.ndjson`. No console output. No mixing with the root logger. One JSON object per line. Every event carries `{ timestamp, cycleId, event, payload }`.
  - **Output 2 — SSE stream:** New `BehaviourEventPublisher` (parallel to `GoalStateEventPublisher`) and `GET /events/behaviour` endpoint. Same shape as the NDJSON line. Behaviour events are higher-frequency and have per-event-type shapes — do **not** piggyback on `GoalStateEventPublisher` (which currently emits one fat goal-tree snapshot per cycle).
  - **Service:** `BehaviourLogger` with one method per event type, fans out to (a) the SLF4J `autobuilder.behaviour` logger, (b) `BehaviourEventPublisher`. Jackson-serialised typed payloads (sealed interface or tagged records, one per event type) so the JSON shape is stable and tested.
  - **v1 event set:**
    - **Core loop** (`CycleExecutor`, `AdaptiveExecutionEngine`): `cycle_start { cycleId, currentGoalId, currentSubGoalId, deliberationMode }`, `agent_fitness_ranked { rankings: [{role, score, breakdown}] }`, `agent_selected { role, fitness }`, `agent_skipped { role, reason }`, `cycle_end { resultState, merged, endReason }`
    - **Mutation flow** (`EvolutionAgent` → `EvaluationAgent` → `HeuristicNegotiator` → `MergeAgent`): `mutation_proposed { mutationId, files }`, `mutation_scored { mutationId, llmScore, llmState, criteriaAssessments }`, `heuristic_result { mutationId, heuristic, score, state, weight }` (one per heuristic), `mutation_negotiated { mutationId, finalScore, finalState, overrides }`, `mutation_merged { mutationId, files }` / `mutation_rejected { mutationId, reason }`
    - **Steering** (`DeliberationService`): `deliberation_outcome { mode, signalAdjustments, agentSteps }`
  - **Implementation notes:**
    - Sealed interface `BehaviourEvent` with one record per event type, all carrying common fields via composition
    - Behaviour events MUST not throw — wrap publish in try/catch, log at WARN to the regular logger on failure
    - Tests: one shape-locking unit test per event type (assert serialised JSON keys); one integration test that subscribes to the SSE endpoint and verifies a `cycle_end` arrives
  - **Out of scope (v1):** Goal-tree transitions and pattern-detector firings — see T5-I.
  - **Estimate:** ~6 instrumentation hot spots, additive — does not modify any existing log call.

- [ ] **T5-J: Skip (or contextualise) evaluation meta-step after `#NoDiff`** (`AdaptiveExecutionEngine`, `DeliberationService`, `EvaluationAgent`, `BehaviourLogger`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-01 04:42 run, behaviour-2026-05-01_04-42-59.ndjson):** Cycles 2/4/6 emitted `mutation_scored { mutationId: null }`. Trace: evolution declared `#NoDiff` (`EvolutionResponseParser` → `EvolutionAgent` "Signalling noOp"), the subgoal was promoted to FULFILLED, but the `EVALUATION` meta-step in the same cycle still ran a full LLM call (`Scoring computed … perFileCount=17`, `Criteria delivered to evaluator: N`, `CriteriaCheck lines parsed: M of N expected`). The evaluator inspects the already-merged code and rubber-stamps `[1] ✓ … [2] ✓`, producing a phantom `mutation_scored`/`mutation_negotiated` pair with `mutationId=null`. Outcome is correct (subgoal promotes anyway) but every `#NoDiff` cycle wastes one evaluator LLM call and pollutes the event stream.
  - **Three approaches (in increasing order of generality — pick one or layer them):**
    1. **Direct skip in the engine.** When evolution returned `#NoDiff` this cycle (track via existing `EVOLUTION_NOOP` signal or a new `evolutionNoOpThisCycle` flag on `CycleContextData`), short-circuit the evaluation meta-step in `AdaptiveExecutionEngine` before invoking the agent. Smallest change, lowest risk; no `mutation_scored` event emitted at all for that cycle.
    2. **Inform the meta-agent.** Surface the prior step's `#NoDiff` outcome in the deliberation context (e.g. an "agent step history" / "last evolution outcome" block in the snapshot the meta-agent sees) so it stops scheduling EVALUATION as a follow-up step in the first place. Reuses the deliberation LLM as the gating decision-maker rather than hard-coding the skip in the engine. More flexible (the meta-agent can decide *when* a confirmation evaluation is warranted) but burns one more LLM call to make the decision.
    3. **Retrospective handling in `EvaluationAgent`.** Have `EvaluationAgent.execute()` short-circuit when the in-cycle `EvolutionAgent` outcome was `#NoDiff` (read from a shared per-cycle "step history" / `AgentOutcomeRegistry` exposed on `CycleContextData`). Same end-state as (1) but pushes the responsibility to the agent — and the broader infra change (a shared, structured step-history record per cycle, available to all agents) is reusable for any future cross-agent coordination (e.g. analysis reading evaluation outcomes, planner reading evolution outcomes).
  - **Recommended:** start with (1) for the immediate cost/pollution win; design (3)'s `AgentOutcomeRegistry` (cycle-scoped, append-only list of `{ role, outcome, timestamp }`) as a follow-up since it unlocks both (2) and richer behaviour-log payloads (`agent_step_outcome`).
  - **Acceptance:** behaviour log shows zero `mutation_scored` events with `mutationId=null` across a full healthy run; subgoal-promotion behaviour after `#NoDiff` is unchanged.
  - **2026-05-02 confirmation:** same `mutationId: null` pattern observed in cycles C5/C8 of the 21:52 run, but via a different path — evolution proposed output identical to pre-existing on-disk files (redundant output), not an explicit `#NoDiff`. `RedundantOutputHeuristic` correctly fired; merge was blocked; the phantom `mutation_scored` still appeared. Scope of fix should cover both the `#NoDiff` case and the redundant-output case (i.e. gate on no stored mutation variant, not just on the `EVOLUTION_NOOP` signal alone).

- [ ] **T5-M: Add `buildOutcome` to `BehaviourEvent.CycleEnd`** (`BehaviourEvent`, `BehaviourLogger`, `CycleExecutor`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-04, behaviour-2026-05-04_01-37-09.ndjson):** `BehaviourEvent.CycleEnd` (lines 61–69) carries only `resultState`, `merged`, `endReason`. When `BuildOutcome` is non-null on `CycleContextData` after a post-merge compile run (T4-AN-1), it is not emitted to the behaviour file. Offline BUILD_FIX deadlock analysis requires inferring the build state from evaluator `criteriaAssessments` rather than reading it directly from the event.
  - **Fix:** Add `String buildOutcome` field to `CycleEnd` record. In `BehaviourLogger.cycleEnd(...)`, pass `context.buildOutcome() != null ? context.buildOutcome().name() : null`. Null serialises as absent field — backward compatible with existing log consumers.
  - **Tests:** assert that a post-merge cycle with `BuildOutcome.COMPILE_ERROR` emits a `cycle_end` event with `"buildOutcome": "COMPILE_ERROR"`; assert null `BuildOutcome` omits the field.
  - **Scope:** tiny — one record field, one logger call, two test assertions.

- [ ] **T5-N: `build_fix_attempt` behaviour events for outer cycles and inner loop** (`BehaviourEvent`, `BehaviourLogger`, `MergeAgent`, `BuildFixInnerLoop`)
  - **Motivation:** With T4-AN-6 inner loop, individual build-fix attempts collapse out of the per-cycle event stream. With T2-CV staged escalation, the same cycle id may transit through multiple stages (cap → replan → analysis → pause). Today's behaviour log gives no visibility into either.
  - **New event:** `BuildFixAttempt { cycleId, source: "outer" | "inner-loop", attemptNumber, errorKind?, category, classifierSummary?, diagnosticHintApplied?: String, mutatedFiles: List<String>, outcome: COMPILE_OK | COMPILE_ERROR | TIMEOUT | SKIPPED, durationMs, escalationStage?: 1 | 2 | 3 }`. Emitted from `MergeAgent` for outer-cycle attempts and from `BuildFixInnerLoop` for inner attempts.
  - **Pairs with:** T5-M (`buildOutcome` in `CycleEnd` is the cycle-level summary; `BuildFixAttempt` is the attempt-level detail).
  - **Acceptance:** offline analysis of a build-fix-deadlock session can reconstruct attempt-by-attempt without grepping `autobuilder.log`; `BehaviourEvent.BuildFixAttempt` is sufficient.

- [ ] **T5-O: Replace Maven-specific `summariseStderr` with classifier output** (`MergeAgent`, `BuildErrorClassifier` / `BuildErrorClassifierAgent`)
  - **Problem:** `MergeAgent.summariseStderr` greps for `[ERROR]` prefix — Maven convention. Gradle uses `e:`, npm uses `npm ERR!`, Cargo uses `error[E…]:`, etc. The function will misclassify "informative line" vs "error line" on every non-Maven build tool.
  - **Fix:** T4-AN-4's classifier already returns a `summaryLine`. Thread it through to `injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent` and remove the bespoke summariser. Drops ~20 lines of code.
  - **Why T5:** quality / dead code removal. No behaviour change for Maven; positive change for everything else.
  - **Depends on:** T4-AN-4 (classifier provides `summaryLine`). Trivial after that lands.

- [ ] **T5-P: Build-execution cost telemetry per session and per subgoal** (`CycleContextData`, `BehaviourEvent.CycleEnd`, optional `BUILD_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` signal)
  - **Problem:** With T4-AN-6's inner loop adding up to 3× more `BuildExecutor` invocations per cycle, build wall-clock can dominate session cost. `BuildOutcome.durationMs` exists per invocation but isn't aggregated. No visibility into "this session has spent 47 minutes building."
  - **Fix:**
    1. Add `long cumulativeBuildMs` to `CycleContextData`; carry forward via `ContextLineageTracker`; increment in `MergeAgent` (and `BuildFixInnerLoop`) after each `BuildExecutor.execute` call.
    2. Surface in `BehaviourEvent.CycleEnd` (alongside T5-M's `buildOutcome`) and as a header field in `behaviour-{ts}.ndjson`.
    3. **Optional escalation:** when `cumulativeBuildMs > autobuilder.build.session-budget-ms` (default disabled), raise `BUILD_BUDGET_EXCEEDED` at high strength + force deliberation. T1-EB's zero-work-cycle breaker acts as the ultimate halt if budget exhaustion coincides with deadlock.
  - **Why T5:** observability with optional steering hook. Mostly a counter; the budget escalation is opt-in.
  - **Pairs with:** T5-N (per-attempt detail), T5-R (per-LLM-call detail) — together give a full session cost picture.

- [ ] **T5-Q: Retention policy for `firestarter/logs/`, `snapshots/`, `mutations/`, `responses/`** (new `core/retention/RetentionPolicy`, scheduled cleanup task, `application.yml`)
  - **Problem:** `firestarter/logs/` is at 160 MB after a few sessions; `firestarter/snapshots/` and `firestarter/mutations/` accumulate per-cycle entries (one directory per cycle id). Nothing prunes. Disk usage grows unboundedly per session and across sessions.
  - **Fix — declarative retention:**
    1. New `application.yml` block:
       ```
       autobuilder.retention:
         logs:        { max-age-days: 7,  max-size-mb: 500 }
         snapshots:   { max-age-days: 14, max-cycles: 1000 }
         mutations:   { max-age-days: 14, max-cycles: 1000 }
         responses:   { max-age-days: 7,  max-size-mb: 200 }
       ```
    2. `RetentionPolicy` Spring bean walks each directory on a configurable schedule (default daily); deletes entries older than `max-age-days` AND keeps total size/count within `max-size-mb` / `max-cycles`. Always preserves the **last N** entries unconditionally (default 100) for any directory.
    3. Skip during an active session (`AutobuilderService.isCycleInProgress`) to avoid deleting a snapshot mid-cycle.
  - **Why T5:** operational quality. No behaviour change to the loop; just stops disk growth.
  - **Risk:** deleting a snapshot referenced by Neo4j orchestration history breaks future graph queries. Mitigation: retention service consults `GoalGraphService` for "still-referenced" cycle ids and exempts them, or delete only after a configurable grace period beyond what Neo4j would query. Safer default: max-age applies but max-size is advisory until validated.

- [x] **T5-S: Log resolved JDK / `mvn -v` once per session in `BuildExecutor`** (`BuildExecutor`)
  - **Observed (2026-05-07 session):** Firestarter build failed with `Failed to execute goal org.apache.maven.plugins:maven-compiler-plugin:3.13.0:compile … Fatal error compiling: error: release version 21 not supported`. Operator's interactive shell `mvn -v` reported `Java version: 21.0.10, runtime: /usr/lib/jvm/java-21-openjdk-amd64` — but the autobuilder JVM was running on `Java 21.0.9.0.9` (a different OpenJDK build). The autobuilder-spawned `mvn` was picking up a *third* JDK via inherited `JAVA_HOME` (older than 21), invisible to the operator without re-running `mvn -v` in the same launcher environment. Diagnosis took several rounds of guessing because nothing in the autobuilder's own logs surfaces what `JAVA_HOME` / `javac` the spawned `mvn` actually uses.
  - **Root cause:** `BuildExecutor.execute` (line 91) uses `ProcessBuilder` with default env inheritance. The spawned `mvn` resolves `javac` via `JAVA_HOME`, which can differ from both the autobuilder JVM's own JDK and the operator's interactive shell. There is no telemetry that surfaces this resolution at runtime — `BehaviourEvent.CycleEnd` carries the `BuildOutcome` but not the build environment.
  - **Fix — one-shot startup probe:**
    1. On first `execute(...)` call (lazy, gated by an `AtomicBoolean`), run `mvn -v` (or `${command tokens[0]} -v`) in the same `ProcessBuilder` with the same env, capture stdout, log at INFO. Skip on subsequent calls.
    2. Also log `JAVA_HOME` from `System.getenv("JAVA_HOME")` and `java.home` from `System.getProperty("java.home")` — they can differ (autobuilder JVM vs spawned-process resolution), and the discrepancy is itself diagnostic.
    3. Optional: capture the same triple into a `BehaviourEvent.BuildEnvironment { mavenVersion, javaVersion, javaHome }` event emitted once per session, so post-hoc analysis can correlate compile errors with the resolved JDK.
  - **Why T5:** pure observability. Doesn't change behaviour; saves several rounds of guessing when the build fails for environment reasons rather than code reasons.
  - **Acceptance:**
    1. First `BuildExecutor.execute` call in a session emits a single INFO log containing the spawned `mvn`'s reported Maven version, Java version, Java home, and runtime path.
    2. Same call also logs the `JAVA_HOME` env var as seen by the autobuilder JVM.
    3. Subsequent calls do not repeat the probe (cost of `mvn -v` is ~200ms; once per session is free).
  - **Edge cases:**
    - **`mvn -v` itself fails** (e.g. `mvn` not on `PATH`). Log the failure and fall through — don't break the actual build call. The failure mode is information enough.
    - **`autobuilder.build.command` is overridden to a non-`mvn` tool** (Gradle, sbt). Probe `${tokens[0]} -v` (or `--version`) — most build tools accept it. If the tool doesn't recognise the flag, the failure path above swallows the error.
  - **Risks:**
    - **Probe noise.** ~200ms one-time cost on session bootstrap. Negligible vs. the 1–2 s per subsequent build.
    - **Sensitive paths in logs.** `JAVA_HOME` may contain operator user paths. Consistent with existing log content; not a new exposure.
  - **Depends on:** None. Self-contained.
  - **Pairs with:** T5-P (per-session/per-subgoal build cost telemetry — both ride on `BehaviourEvent.BuildEnvironment` if we add the event).
  - **Notes:** Filed 2026-05-07 after a session where the firestarter build failed with `release version 21 not supported` despite both autobuilder JVM (21.0.9) and operator shell `mvn` (21.0.10) reporting Java 21. Root cause: spawned-`mvn` `JAVA_HOME` resolved to a third (older) JDK; the resolution wasn't visible in any autobuilder log. ~30 LOC + tests.

- [ ] **T5-R: LLM token usage and cost tracking** (`LLMClient`, `ChatResponse`, new `LlmUsageTracker`, `BehaviourEvent.LlmCall`)
  - **Problem:** No token usage or cost tracking anywhere in `LLMClient` / `LlmCircuitBreaker`. With ~6 agent roles each making LLM calls per cycle (planner, evolution, evaluation, analysis, interpretation, deliberation, plus Chain H's classifier and inner-loop calls), session cost is invisible. A run that should have cost $1 may have cost $20; the operator can't tell until the bill arrives.
  - **Fix:**
    1. Capture `usage.{prompt_tokens, completion_tokens, total_tokens}` from every LLM response (`ChatResponse` extension). Most providers return this in the standard OpenAI-compatible shape; defensive null-handling for those that don't.
    2. New `LlmUsageTracker` accumulates per-role and per-session counters; expose via REST endpoint (`GET /autobuilder/usage`) and emit `BehaviourEvent.LlmCall { cycleId, role, model, promptTokens, completionTokens, durationMs }` per call.
    3. **Optional cost mapping:** `application.yml` `autobuilder.llm.pricing.{model}: { prompt-per-1k-usd: …, completion-per-1k-usd: … }` — turns token counts into dollars in the same telemetry.
    4. Log a session-end summary: total tokens by role, top-3 most expensive cycles, cumulative dollars.
  - **Why T5:** pure observability. Doesn't affect the loop; necessary for operating it responsibly.
  - **Pairs with:** T5-P (build-cost) — together cover both LLM-cost and compute-cost dimensions of a session.

- [ ] **T5-I: Behaviour logger v2 — goal-tree + pattern-detector events** (`GoalPlannerService`, `ReplanPatternDetector`, `ScopeThrashSignalAnalyzer`)
  - **Depends on:** T5-H (the `BehaviourLogger` service and `BehaviourEvent` sealed type must already exist).
  - **v2 event set:**
    - **Goal tree** (`GoalPlannerService`): `subgoal_promoted { fromId, toId }`, `goal_status_changed { goalId, from, to, reason }`, `planning_run { goalId, injected, deduped }`
    - **Pattern detectors** (`ReplanPatternDetector`, `ScopeThrashSignalAnalyzer`): `pattern_detected { detector, evidence, signalEmitted }`
  - **Why split from T5-H:** v1 answers "why did this cycle do X?" (per-cycle decisions); v2 answers "is the meta-system stuck?" (cross-cycle dynamics). Different debugging questions, and v1 is where you look first when something looks wrong.

- [ ] **T5-G: Investigate persistent SCOPE_RESULT_OUT_OF_SCOPE signal (decayPerCycle=0.0)** (`CycleStartSignalSeeder`, `DefaultSubgoalScopeGuard`, `BasicSignals`)
  - **Observed (2026-04-21 02:00–02:18 session):** `SCOPE_RESULT_OUT_OF_SCOPE` signal held at `strength=1.0, decayPerCycle=0.0` for every cycle throughout the session. A zero-decay signal that persists for the entire run suggests it is being re-seeded each cycle (intentional) or that the decay is misconfigured (bug).
  - **Questions to answer:** (1) Who emits this signal and with what decay value? (2) Is zero decay intentional for this signal type? (3) Does it correctly suppress evaluation/merge in scope-mismatch cycles, or is it a false positive that throttles the loop unnecessarily? (4) Is there a mechanism to clear it when the scope guard stops firing?
  - **Investigation entry points:** `CycleStartSignalSeeder`, `DefaultSubgoalScopeGuard`, `BasicSignals.SCOPE_RESULT_OUT_OF_SCOPE` definition, and any call site that emits the signal.

- [ ] **T5-C: SMS Phase 2 — richer semantic model** (`SmsCycleIngestor`)
  - Emit `INTERPRETATION(type=ACCEPTED_MUTATION)` / `REJECTED_MUTATION` with evaluation score as confidence
  - Link to diff artifact
  - Makes SMS queryable for learning patterns over accepted vs rejected mutations

- [x] **T5-P: Unify cycle reference field name across artifact types**
  - `planner_outcome` artifacts use `cycleName`; other artifact types use `cycleId` for the same concept
  - Fix: `PlannerActionRegistrar` payload key `"cycleName"` → `"cycleId"`; `PlannerActionSnapshot.cycleName` → `cycleId`; Cypher alias + row getter in `Neo4jAgentGraphService` updated to match

- [x] **T5-A: Fix `GraphQueryService` attempt ID lookup + diagnostic coverage**
  - `ctx.parentAttemptId()` → `ctx.lastAttemptId()` for `DIAGNOSTIC_SCAN` query
  - Added `targetsGoalId = $scopeGoalId` fallback OR clause to queries A0–A2 (matching A3's existing pattern) — robust when attempt ID is stale after restart/subgoal promotion
  - Added **A4 rejection-loop query**: fires when ≥3 evaluations exist and ≥60% are REJECTED
  - Added **scan history** to `GraphQueryService` (bounded deque, last 5 results); `DiagnosticScanFormatter.toLLMBlock(r, history)` annotates each finding with recurrence count across prior scans

- [x] **T5-D: Persist scope assessments to Neo4j** (new `graph/scope/ScopeActionRegistrar`, `DefaultSubgoalScopeGuard`)
  - `ScopeActionRegistrar` created (follows `EvaluationActionRegistrar` pattern): action type=`"scope"`, status=`fit.name()`, artifact type=`"scope_payload"` with JSON payload: `{ fit, confidence, message, bestMatchingSubGoalId, subGoalId, goalId, cycleId, attemptId }`.
  - Injected into `DefaultSubgoalScopeGuard`; called after every `assess()` return (all scope assessment types: IN_SCOPE, PARTIAL, OUT_OF_SCOPE), fail-open.
  - `scope` nodes appear automatically in the "Recent Actions" block of `OrchestrationSnapshotFormatter` LLM context.
  - **Prerequisite for T2-AM** (scope thrash detection).

- [x] **T5-E: Expose last scope assessment explicitly in OrchestrationSnapshot** (`OrchestrationSnapshotService`, `OrchestrationSnapshot`, `OrchestrationSnapshotFormatter`)
  - **Depends on T5-D.**
  - Add a dedicated Cypher fetch in `OrchestrationSnapshotService` for the most recent `scope_assessment` action node in the current attempt/subgoal scope.
  - Add `lastScopeAssessment` (`ActionEvidence` + payload fields: `fit`, `confidence`, `bestMatchingSubGoalId`) to the `OrchestrationSnapshot` record.
  - Add a `--- Last Scope Assessment ---` section to `OrchestrationSnapshotFormatter.toLLMBlock()` so the meta-deliberation agent sees it without having to parse the raw "Recent Actions" list.

- [x] **T5-K: Extract deterministic blocker-subgoal injector from `MergeAgent`** (new `core/blocker/BlockerSubgoalInjector`, `MergeAgent`, future callers)
  - **Context (2026-05-03):** T1-DM landed deterministic `BUILD_FIX` subgoal injection inside `MergeAgent.injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent()` — fires on COMPILE_ERROR, sets the new subgoal as current, idempotent via `metadata[blockerType]=BUILD_FIX`. The mechanism (idempotent injection + `setAsCurrent` + criteria + metadata tag) is intentionally generic but its only caller today is `MergeAgent`, so it lives there for now per the "extract on second caller" rule.
  - **Scope when extracted:** Move the helper methods (`injectBuildFixSubgoalIfAbsent`, `hasActiveBuildFixSubgoal`, `isActiveBuildFix`, `summariseStderr`) plus the `METADATA_KEY_BLOCKER_TYPE` / `BLOCKER_TYPE_BUILD_FIX` constants into a `BlockerSubgoalInjector` component. Method shape: `inject(BlockerType type, String evidence, List<String> criteria)`. Add `BlockerType` (small enum or interface with constants — decide based on caller breadth at extraction time).
  - **Trigger to extract:** the second deterministic blocker becomes a real need — e.g. test runner adding `TEST_FAIL`, scope guard adding `SCOPE_VIOLATION`, dependency resolution adding `DEP_MISSING`. Until then, in-place is fine.
  - **Acceptance:** `MergeAgent` keeps a 2-line call into the injector; the injector has its own unit-test class covering idempotency / setAsCurrent / metadata write-back; existing `MergeAgentTest` T1-DM cases keep passing unchanged at the integration boundary.

- [ ] **T5-L: Security hardening for non-local / multi-tenant deployment posture** (`LLMClient`, `ChatRequest`, `application.yml`, `SECURITY.md`, `MergeAgent`)
  - **Threat model:** Today the autobuilder runs on a single dev machine against a localhost Ollama and merges only to a sandboxed `output/` directory under `file.base-path`. In that posture, the items below are theoretical. They become load-bearing in any of these futures: (a) Ollama exposed beyond localhost (multi-user host, LAN-reachable, behind a reverse proxy); (b) Ollama shared with workloads that process untrusted input; (c) `MergeAgent` wired to push to an actual VCS / CI / deploy target without a human-review gate; (d) any autobuilder run artefact (logs, ndjson, mutations, snapshots, commits) ever leaves the host. The point of this ticket is to *recognise* the boundaries before any of those futures is taken, not to defend against them speculatively now.
  - **Specific exposures observed during the 2026-05-03 cold/warm Ollama investigation:**
    1. **Bind surface.** `application.yml` points at `http://localhost:11434/v1/chat/completions`, but Ollama's bind address is governed by `OLLAMA_HOST` *outside* the autobuilder repo. If that ever flips to `0.0.0.0` (intentionally or by mistake), every chat request becomes reachable to anything that can route to the host.
    2. **KV-cache prefix-reuse side channel.** Ollama (and most modern serving frameworks) reuses cached KV-prefix across requests for latency. With shared access, an attacker can probe candidate prefixes and measure time-to-first-token to recover prior users' prompts (cf. *"InputSnatch"*, Zhang et al. 2024 and successors against vLLM/SGLang). Equivalent leakage exists in Ollama's prefix-cache implementation.
    3. **MoE expert-routing leakage.** `qwen3-coder:30b` is a Mixture-of-Experts model. Co-batched-tenant attacks on MoE inference (cf. *"Stealing User Prompts from Mixture of Experts"*, Yona et al. 2024) can reconstruct victim tokens via observed expert-activation patterns. Mitigated by sole-tenant batching (Ollama default for solo workloads) — broken if the same Ollama process ever serves more than one source of requests.
    4. **State-priming amplification via auto-merge.** The cold-vs-warm Ollama variance documented in the May-03 analysis means allocator/kernel state is a load-bearing input to planner output. An attacker who can force Ollama restarts (OOM another big model into the host, exploit a known Ollama crash) chooses *which mode* the code generator runs in — i.e. picks the *distribution* of generated mutations. Today the human reviews the merge; in any auto-merge future, that distributional control becomes a code-shipping vector.
    5. **Reproducibility as a defensive property.** `ChatRequest` doesn't currently send `seed` or `top_k` to Ollama. Without them, identical prompts produce slightly different outputs across runs even at `temperature=0` (FP-tie sampler behaviour, MoE routing drift). Anomalous outputs become harder to flag as anomalous when the baseline isn't reproducible.
    6. **First-cycle output as a side channel for inference-host state.** The cold-vs-warm Ollama variance has a symmetric reading: if an observer ever sees the autobuilder's *outputs* across multiple runs, the file-count / structural pattern of cycle 0 leaks information about the inference host without any access to it.
       - **Operator-activity oracle.** A "cold-Ollama signature" (broad first-cycle mutation, ~15 files) versus a "warm-Ollama signature" (narrow, ~5 files) tells an observer whether Ollama was recently restarted — which in turn tells them when the operator was last debugging, switched models, or rebooted the host. Useful for activity-pattern inference on the developer.
       - **Model + prompt-template fingerprinting.** First-cycle file-set, naming conventions, structural choices (entity + controller + DTO + exception handler split, package layout, framework idioms) are characteristic of a specific model + prompt-contract combination. An attacker comparing autobuilder outputs against a corpus of known model fingerprints can identify the underlying LLM and the prompt scaffolding with reasonable confidence — which then guides targeted prompt-injection or jailbreak crafting against that specific stack.
       - **Seed-text recovery.** Because the planner's first subgoal derives near-deterministically from `bootstrap/seed.txt`, an observer with several consecutive first-cycle outputs can reverse-engineer or constrain the seed text content even without read access to the file. Relevant if the seed contains anything sensitive (private goal language, internal terminology, references to non-public systems).
       - **MoE-routing inference.** With enough samples, the *variation* between first-cycle outputs across runs is itself signal: which decision points flipped between cold and warm states identifies which planner-prompt subdecisions have FP-tie ambiguity in this model — narrowing the search space for an attacker who later tries to influence those same decisions through co-tenant priming or prompt crafting.
       - **Where this becomes observable:** `behaviour-*.ndjson` files shared with anyone (debugging help, bug reports, screenshots); `mutations/` or `snapshots/` directories committed to or synced with any external location; autobuilder runs published in any form (research, demos, blog posts, presentations); centralised log aggregation (autobuilder logs sent to a multi-tenant observability backend); future: any VCS push from `MergeAgent` produces a per-commit sample of model+state output.
  - **Fix — phased:**
    - **Phase 1 (cheap, do whenever T5-L is picked up):**
      - Add `seed` (long, default `null` ⇒ omit) and `top_k` (int, default `null` ⇒ omit) fields to `ChatRequest`. Expose them via `autobuilder.llm.api.{seed,topK}` in `application.yml`. Setting them produces reproducible inference; leaving null keeps current Ollama defaults. Document the trade-off in the YAML comments: *pin them when investigating regressions or doing security-review of generated mutations; leave unset for ordinary use to retain MoE diversity.*
      - Add a startup health-check to `LLMClient` that hits `OLLAMA_HOST` (or the equivalent `/api/version` for Ollama) and logs a WARN at boot if the URL resolves to anything other than `127.0.0.1` / `::1` / `localhost`. One-line warning, not a failure — but visible in logs so a deployment misconfig surfaces immediately.
      - Add a `SECURITY.md` at repo root that captures the threat model summary above plus the deployment guidance: localhost-only bind, no shared-tenant Ollama, no auto-deploy without review.
      - **Output-publication awareness in `SECURITY.md`.** Document that any externally-shared autobuilder artefact (logs, ndjson behaviour files, mutations, snapshots, commits) is a fingerprinting sample of the model + prompt contract + inference-host state. Recommendations: (a) treat the `bootstrap/seed.txt` content as the same sensitivity tier as the artefacts derived from it; (b) before publishing any run output (e.g. to file an issue, write a blog post, demo the system), redact or paraphrase if the seed or any prompt block contains anything non-public; (c) for research / demo runs, deliberately use a synthetic seed that's safe to publish, rather than a real internal goal.
    - **Phase 2 (only when posture changes):**
      - If/when Ollama is moved off localhost or the autobuilder is run as a service: add request authentication on the autobuilder REST endpoints (`/autobuilder/evolve`, `/autobuilder/status`, manual reset), since today they're unauthenticated by Spring config. Trivial for a single-user private deployment; required before any wider exposure.
      - If/when `MergeAgent` is wired to push to VCS / CI: add a mandatory human-review checkpoint between `mutation_merged` (filesystem write) and any out-of-process side effect. The point isn't to gate dev-loop merges; it's to ensure the auto-deploy pathway doesn't bypass review when it eventually exists.
      - Pin the Ollama process to single-tenant via systemd/docker resource isolation. Document that running other models (or other Ollama clients) against the same daemon while autobuilder is running is the failure mode that breaks (3) and amplifies (4).
      - **Stable-output mode for publishable runs.** When `autobuilder.llm.api.seed` and `top_k=1` are pinned (Phase 1 mechanism), first-cycle outputs become reproducible — paradoxically *more* fingerprintable in absolute terms, but at least the operator gains a *known* fingerprint they can choose to publish, rather than an unknowable distribution. Add a config flag `autobuilder.publication-mode: true` that bundles: (a) seed/top_k pinned to documented values, (b) prompt-block versions logged in run metadata, (c) optional stripping of timestamps and host-identifying fields from `behaviour-*.ndjson` before snapshot. None of this prevents fingerprinting — it makes the fingerprint deliberate. Useful beyond security as a reproducible-regression-test mechanism.
    - **Phase 3 (only if running on shared inference):**
      - Migrate to a serving framework that supports per-tenant KV-cache isolation (vLLM with `--enable-prefix-caching=false` per-request, or a hosted endpoint with documented isolation guarantees). Out of scope for current setup.
  - **Changes:** Phase 1 only — `ChatRequest.java` (~6 lines for two optional fields), `LLMClient.java` (~10-line startup health check + WARN), `application.yml` (two new optional config keys with comments), `SECURITY.md` (new file at repo root, one page). No behavioural changes when keys are unset. Tests: `chatRequest_omitsSeedAndTopKWhenNull_passesThroughDefaults`, `chatRequest_seedAndTopKSet_serialisedInPayload`, `llmClient_nonLocalhostUrl_logsWarn`.
  - **Depends on:** None.
  - **Out of scope (intentionally):** Sandboxing of generated code (already partially handled by the `output/` boundary), prompt-injection resistance in the LLM itself (model-level concern, not framework), TLS for `LLMClient` (irrelevant for localhost). Listed here so the ticket isn't quietly expanded later.
  - **Notes:** Filed during 2026-05-03 cold/warm Ollama analysis after observing that allocator state perturbs first-cycle file counts. The empirical observation isn't itself a bug — but it's the symptom of an attack surface that does matter once the deployment posture changes. Phase 1 is the cheap-now hedge; Phase 2/3 are explicit gates against shipping broader exposure without the corresponding hardening. Output-side observation channel (bullet 6) added 2026-05-04 — distinct from the input-side concerns (1)–(5) and easy to overlook because the surface is *artefacts*, not endpoints.

---

## Proposed chains

Chains are ordered sequences of tickets that should be implemented together or in strict dependency order. Each ticket within a chain is a pre-requisite or strong enabler for the next.

---

### Chain A — Merge hygiene (from 2026-04-13 session)
**Goal:** Stop writing wrong/stale/cross-subgoal mutations to the output directory. Reduces batch size and makes each merge intentional.

```
T1-CD ✓  →  T1-CE
T1-CG ✓  (independent — fingerprint bleed on subgoal promotion)
```

- **T1-CD done:** filter non-ACCEPTED cycles out of `FileVariantCollector.collect()` and `getAllVariantPaths()`. Also required: SKIPPED merge registration in `MergeAgent` when no ACCEPTED variants exist (keeps graph in sync); `hasAnyVariant()` guard on evolution blocker (backstop against graph/filesystem drift); fixed dead-code path in `CycleExecutor.applyMergeOnAcceptedMutation()`.
- **T1-CG done:** clear evolution fingerprints on subgoal change in `ContextLineageTracker`. Prevented evaluation from being permanently blocked on every newly promoted subgoal — was causing immediate GIVEN_UP cascade after each promotion.
- **T1-CE done:** scope merge batches to the active subgoal ID. `collect()` and `getAllVariantPaths()` now take/use `activeSubGoalId` and skip cycles whose `currentSubGoalId` doesn't match. Bootstrap cycles (null/blank active subgoal) are unaffected. Cross-subgoal contamination eliminated.

---

### Chain B — False GIVEN_UP on fulfilled subgoals (from 2026-04-13 session)
**Goal:** When evolution runs out of diffs because the code is already correct, the system should recognise completion rather than abandoning the subgoal.

```
T1-CF  →  T2-CC  →  T2-CB (safety net only)
```

- **T1-CF first:** in the `SUBGOAL_EXHAUSTED` handler, consult `cumulativeCriteriaStatus` before calling `GIVEN_UP`. If all criteria are met, promote to `FULFILLED`. Requires T1-BG + T3-BI to be in place (both already done). This is the primary fix — removes the false-GIVEN_UP at the decision point.
- **T2-CC next:** add a `#NoDiff` short-circuit to `EvolutionAgent`. When evolution detects the implementation is already present, it returns `noOp=true` instead of generating an empty diff. Stops `REPETITION_DETECTED` from accumulating in the first place. Builds on T1-CF by giving the signal for a criteria-only evaluation.
- **T2-CB last (safety net):** caps the empty-backlog injection bypass so even if T1-CF and T2-CC miss a case, the loop terminates after 3 failed recovery attempts rather than churning indefinitely.

---

### Chain E — Endless subgoal creation on GIVEN_UP cascade (from 2026-04-13 23:03 session)
**Goal:** Stop T1-CA injections from immediately stagnating and re-triggering further injections due to a stale diff context and weak re-injection guard.

```
T1-CH  (fingerprint clearing on T1-CA injection)
T1-CI  (lower GIVEN_UP_RETRY_LIMIT from 2 → 1)
```

These two tickets are independent; either can ship first, but both are needed together for full protection.

- **T1-CH first (higher impact):** After T1-CA injects new subgoals at root, clear `lastEvolutionFingerprint` and `lastEvaluatedEvolutionFingerprint` on the live context. Without this, the stale diff from the dead subgoal is scope-checked against every new subgoal → always `OUT_OF_SCOPE` → immediate `SUBGOAL_SCOPE_MISMATCH` → `mut=null` cascade → `ScopeThrashSignalAnalyzer` gives up the new subgoal → T1-CA fires again. T1-CH gives each injected subgoal a clean evolution slate.
